is a solution of the system (
1.4
). As in section
3
we assume that

\begin{split}\|h(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}}}+\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}-1/2}}+\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}}}&\leq A,\\
\|h(t)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1},0}}+\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(t)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-1/2,0}}+\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega(t)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1},0}}&\leq\varepsilon(t),\end{split}

(4.1)

for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
, where
A\in(0,\infty)
is a number and
\varepsilon:[T_{1},T_{2}]\to[0,\infty)
is a continuous function satisfying
\varepsilon(t)\ll 1
.

For
\delta\in(0,1/100)
let

m_{N_{0}}(\xi)=m_{N_{0},\delta}(\xi):=\varphi_{\geq 0}(\xi)\langle\xi\rangle^{N_{0}}/(1+\delta\langle\xi\rangle^{N_{0}})

(4.2)

and let
J^{N_{0}}
denote the operator defined by the multiplier
m_{N_{0}}
. Let

A(t):=\big\|(h+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega)(t)\big\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\lesssim A.

(4.3)

We define the energy functional

\mathcal{E}_{2}:=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}h\cdot J^{N_{0}}h\,dx+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}|\partial_{x}|\omega\cdot J^{N_{0}}\omega\,dx.

(4.4)

4.1.
The first normal form

Our goal in this section is to eliminate the cubic bulk terms (using essentially a Shatah normal form), and show that the increment of the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{2}
is controlled by a space-time integral that can be written as sum of quartic expressions in the main variables
h^{\ast}
and
\omega^{\ast}
and an acceptable quintic contribution. At this stage it is very important to get precise formulas for the quartic increment, as described in Lemma
4.7
, and it is convenient to work with the real variables
h^{\ast}
and
\omega^{\ast}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

For any
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
we have

|\mathcal{E}_{2}(t_{2})-\mathcal{E}_{2}(t_{1})|\lesssim\sum_{j\in\{1,2\}}\varepsilon(t_{j})A(t_{j})^{2}+\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\sum_{a\in\{1,\ldots,11\}}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{a}(s)\,ds\Big|+A^{2}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}[\varepsilon(s)]^{3}\,ds,

(4.5)

where the quartic expressions
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{a}
are given by

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{1}:=2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}G_{3}(\phi,h,h)\cdot J^{N_{0}}h\,dx,

(4.6)

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{2}:=2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}|\partial_{x}|\omega\cdot J^{N_{0}}L_{3}(h,\phi,\phi)\,dx,

(4.7)

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{3}:=-2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}\partial_{x}(T_{V}h)\cdot T_{\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h\,dx+2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}G_{2}(\phi,h)\cdot T_{\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h\,dx,

(4.8)

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{4}:=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\big\{\widehat{h}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{\phi}(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}-\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\big\}\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)q_{2}(\rho,\eta)}{|\rho|^{1/2}},

(4.9)

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{5}:=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega}(\xi)\big\{\widehat{\phi}(\eta)|\eta|^{1/2}\widehat{\phi}(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}-\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\big\}\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\xi|\ell_{2}(\rho,\eta)}{|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}},

(4.10)

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{6}:=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\big\{\widehat{h}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{V}(\rho)-\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)i\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}\big\}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)i\xi,

(4.11)

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{7}:=\frac{-2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega}(\xi)\widehat{\omega}(\eta)\big\{\widehat{V}(\rho)-\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)i\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}\big\}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)i\eta|\xi|,

(4.12)

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{8}:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}&\big\{\widehat{h}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{\partial_{t}\alpha}(\rho)\\
&+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)|\rho|^{3/2}\big\}m_{N_{0}}(\xi)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)\chi(\rho,\eta),\end{split}

(4.13)

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{9}:=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}&\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\big\{\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{\alpha}(\rho)\\
&+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)|\rho|\big\}m_{N_{0}}(\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}[m_{N_{0}}(\eta)-m_{N_{0}}(\xi)]\chi(\rho,\eta),\end{split}

(4.14)

and, with the symbols
A,B,D:\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}\to\mathbb{R}
defined as in (
4.30
)–(
4.31
) and (
4.38
),

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 4}&:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\frac{A(\xi,\eta,\rho)}{D(\xi,\eta,\rho)}\Big\{6|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\\
&+3|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\rho)+6|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\rho)\Big\},\end{split}

(4.15)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 4}:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\frac{B(\xi,\eta,\rho)}{D(\xi,\eta,\rho)}\\
&\times\Big\{2|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\big[2\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)+\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\rho)\big]\\
&+|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\big[2\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\rho)\big]\\
&-2|\eta|^{1/2}(|\xi|-|\eta|+|\rho|)\big[\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}}(\rho)\big]\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.16)

Remark 4.2
.

The
\delta
-mollification of the weights in (
4.2
)
is only needed to guarantee that our algebraic manipulations
are justified (the integrals and sums converge absolutely),
but all the bounds are independent on
\delta
. We will later let
\delta\to 0
to prove Theorem
1.1
.

We notice also that our multipliers
m_{N_{0}}
are supported at frequencies
|\xi|\gtrsim 1
.
This is useful to avoid some low-frequency issues, related to our functional framework. The low-frequency part of the energy can be controlled separately, using the conservation
of the Hamiltonian (
1.6
), see section
6
for details.

Proof.

The variables
h
and
\omega
satisfy the evolution equations

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}h=|\partial_{x}|\omega-\partial_{x}(T_{V}h)+G_{2}(\phi,h)+G_{3}(\phi,h,h)+G_{\geq 4},\\
\partial_{t}\omega=-h-T_{\alpha}h-T_{V}\partial_{x}\omega+L_{2}(\phi,\phi)+L_{3}(h,\phi,\phi)+L_{\geq 4},\end{array}\right.

(4.17)

as a consequence of (
1.4
), (
3.5
), and (
3.19
).

Step 1.
With
\mathcal{E}_{2}
defined as in (
4.4
) we start by calculating

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}_{2}&=2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}\partial_{t}h\cdot J^{N_{0}}h\,dx+2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}|\partial_{x}|\omega\cdot J^{N_{0}}\partial_{t}\omega\,dx\\
&=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{1}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{2}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{3}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{4}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{\#}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{1}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{2}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{1},\end{split}

(4.18)

where the quartic terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{2}

are defined as in (
4.6
)–(
4.7
),

\mathcal{B}^{1}_{\geq 5}
is an acceptable remainder satisfying

|\mathcal{B}^{1}_{\geq 5}(t)|\lesssim A^{2}[\varepsilon(t)]^{3}
, and

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{1}&:=2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}G_{2}(\phi,h)\cdot J^{N_{0}}h\,dx,\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{2}:=2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}|\partial_{x}|\omega\cdot J^{N_{0}}L_{2}(\phi,\phi)\,dx,\\
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{3}&:=-2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}\partial_{x}T_{V}h\cdot J^{N_{0}}h\,dx,\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{4}:=-2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}|\partial_{x}|\omega\cdot J^{N_{0}}T_{V}\partial_{x}\omega\,dx,\\
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{\#}&:=-2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}|\partial_{x}|\omega\cdot J^{N_{0}}T_{\alpha}h\,dx.\end{split}

(4.19)

Let

\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\geq 3}:=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}h\cdot T_{\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h\,dx,

(4.20)

where
\alpha:=\partial_{t}B+V\partial_{x}B
as before. Using also (
2.44
), we calculate

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\geq 3}&=2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}\partial_{t}h\cdot T_{\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h\,dx+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}h\cdot T_{\partial_{t}\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h\,dx\\
&=-\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{\#}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{5}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{6}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{3}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{2},\end{split}

(4.21)

where the cubic term
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{\#}
is defined as in (
4.19
), the quartic term
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{3}

is defined as in (
4.8
),

\mathcal{B}^{2}_{\geq 5}
is an acceptable remainder satisfying
|\mathcal{B}^{2}_{\geq 5}(t)|\lesssim A^{2}[\varepsilon(t)]^{3}
,
and

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{5}&:=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}h\cdot T_{\partial_{t}\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h\,dx,\\
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{6}&:=2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}|\partial_{x}|\omega\cdot\big\{T_{\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h-J^{N_{0}}T_{\alpha}h\big\}\,dx.\end{split}

(4.22)

The point of the correction cubic energy functional
\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\geq 3}
is to cancel the term
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{\#}
which loses derivatives. To summarize,

\begin{split}\partial_{t}(\mathcal{E}_{2}+\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\geq 3})&=(\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{1}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{2}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{3}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{4}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{5}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{6})\\
&+(\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{1}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{2}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{3})+(\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{1}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{2}),\end{split}

(4.23)

where
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{2}
are acceptable quintic errors,
and the cubic terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{1},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{6}
and the quartic
terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{1},\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{2},\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{3}
are defined as above.

Step 2.
We would like now to express the remaining cubic bulk terms in terms of the normalized variables
h^{\ast}=T_{\sqrt{1+\alpha}}h
and
\omega^{\ast}=|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega
defined in Lemma
3.4
. For this we pass to the Fourier space and rewrite,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{1}=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{h}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{\phi}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)q_{2}(\rho,\eta)=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{1,0}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{4},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{1,0}:=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\frac{q_{2}(\rho,\eta)}{|\rho|^{1/2}},\end{split}

(4.24)

where
q_{2}
is as in (
3.6
) and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{4}
is defined as in (
4.9
). Similarly

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{2}=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega}(\xi)\widehat{\phi}(\eta)\widehat{\phi}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\xi|\ell_{2}(\rho,\eta)=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{2,0}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{5},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{2,0}:=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}\frac{\ell_{2}(\rho,\eta)}{|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}},\end{split}

(4.25)

where
\ell_{2}
is as in (
3.20
) and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{5}
is defined as in (
4.10
).
Also,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{3}=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{h}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{V}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)i\xi=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{3,0}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{6},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{3,0}:=\frac{-2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)\xi\rho|\rho|^{-1/2},\end{split}

(4.26)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{4}=\frac{-2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega}(\xi)\widehat{\omega}(\eta)\widehat{V}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)i\eta|\xi|=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{4,0}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{7},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{4,0}:=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)\eta|\eta|^{-1/2}\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}|\xi|^{1/2},\end{split}

(4.27)

where
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{6}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{7}
are as in (
4.11
)–(
4.12
). Finally, using also (
3.39
)–(
3.40
),

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{5}=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{h}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{\partial_{t}\alpha}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}(\xi)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)\chi(\rho,\eta)=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{5,0}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{8},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{5,0}:=\frac{-1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}(\xi)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)\chi(\rho,\eta)|\rho|^{3/2},\end{split}

(4.28)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{6}=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{\alpha}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}(\xi)|\xi|[m_{N_{0}}(\eta)-m_{N_{0}}(\xi)]\chi(\rho,\eta)=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{6,0}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{9},\\
&\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{6,0}:=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)m_{N_{0}}(\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|[m_{N_{0}}(\xi)-m_{N_{0}}(\eta)]\chi(\rho,\eta)\end{split}

(4.29)

where
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{8},\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{9}
are defined as in (
4.13
)–(
4.14
).

We examine the cubic expressions
\mathcal{B}^{2,0}_{\geq 3}
and
\mathcal{B}^{4,0}_{\geq 3}
. We symmetrize in
\xi,\eta,\rho
to avoid derivative loss and rewrite

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{2,0}_{\geq 3}+\mathcal{B}^{4,0}_{\geq 3}=\mathcal{B}^{7}_{\geq 3}:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)A(\xi,\eta,\rho),\\
&A(\xi,\eta,\rho):=\frac{\widetilde{A}(\xi,\eta,\rho)+\widetilde{A}(\eta,\rho,\xi)+\widetilde{A}(\rho,\xi,\eta)}{3},\\
&\widetilde{A}(\xi,\eta,\rho):=m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}\frac{\eta\rho+\widetilde{\chi}(\rho,\eta)|\eta||\rho|}{|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}}.\end{split}

(4.30)

Similarly, we combine the expressions
\mathcal{B}^{1,0}_{\geq 3}
,
\mathcal{B}^{3,0}_{\geq 3}
,

\mathcal{B}^{5,0}_{\geq 3}
,
\mathcal{B}^{6,0}_{\geq 3}
, and symmetrize in
\xi
and
\eta
to rewrite

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}^{1,0}_{\geq 3}+\mathcal{B}^{3,0}_{\geq 3}+\mathcal{B}^{5,0}_{\geq 3}+\mathcal{B}^{6,0}_{\geq 3}=\mathcal{B}^{8}_{\geq 3}:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)B(\xi,\eta,\rho),\\
&B(\xi,\eta,\rho):=-|\rho|^{-1/2}\big\{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\widetilde{\chi}(\rho,\eta)(\xi\rho+|\xi||\rho|)+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\widetilde{\chi}(\rho,\xi)(\eta\rho+|\eta||\rho|)\big\}\\
&\quad-\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}\big\{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\xi\chi(\rho,\eta)+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\eta\chi(\rho,\xi)\big\}-m_{N_{0}}(\xi)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)|\rho|^{3/2}\frac{\chi(\rho,\eta)+\chi(\rho,\xi)}{2}\\
&\quad+m_{N_{0}}(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}\big\{|\xi|[m_{N_{0}}(\rho)-m_{N_{0}}(\eta)]\chi(\xi,\eta)+|\eta|[m_{N_{0}}(\rho)-m_{N_{0}}(\xi)]\chi(\eta,\xi)\big\}.\end{split}

(4.31)

Notice that the symbol
A
is symmetric in all three variables, while the symbol
B
is symmetric in the first two variables.

It is easy to see that the multipliers
A
and
B
satisfy the bounds

\begin{split}&\big\|A(\xi,\eta,\rho)\cdot\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\big\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{9}}\lesssim 2^{3\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})/2}2^{2N_{0}\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})},\\
&\big\|B(\xi,\eta,\rho)\cdot\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\big\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{9}}\lesssim 2^{3\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})/2}2^{2N_{0}\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}2^{-|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2},\end{split}

(4.32)

for any
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
(recall the definition (
2.23
)–(
2.24
)). In particular

\begin{split}&\big\|A(\xi,\eta,\rho)\cdot\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\big\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})/2}2^{2N_{0}\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})},\\
&\big\|B(\xi,\eta,\rho)\cdot\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\big\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{3\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})/2}2^{2N_{0}\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}2^{-|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2},\end{split}

(4.33)

for any
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, due to Lemma
2.2
(iv) (recall the definition of the
\widetilde{S}^{\infty}
norm in (
2.9
)).

To summarize, we showed that

\partial_{t}(\mathcal{E}_{2}+\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\geq 3})=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{7}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{8}+\Big\{\sum_{a\in\{1,\ldots,9\}}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{a}\Big\}+(\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{1}+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{2}),

(4.34)

where
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{2}
are acceptable quintic errors.
The cubic terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{7},\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{8}

and the quartic terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{a}
are defined as in the identities
(
4.30
)–(
4.31
) and (
4.6
)–(
4.14
) above.

Step 3.

We use now a normal form transformation to remove the cubic bulk terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{7}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{8}
. For this we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}D(\xi,\eta,\rho)&:=|\xi|(|\xi|-|\eta|-|\rho|)+|\eta|(|\eta|-|\rho|-|\xi|)+|\rho|(|\rho|-|\xi|-|\eta|)\\
&=|\xi|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}+|\rho|^{2}-2|\xi||\eta|-2|\xi||\rho|-2|\eta||\rho|,\end{split}

(4.35)

and two cubic energy functionals

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\geq 3}:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\frac{A(\xi,\eta,\rho)}{D(\xi,\eta,\rho)}\Big\{&3\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}(-|\xi|-|\eta|+|\rho|)\\
&-2\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\Big\},\end{split}

(4.36)

and

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}^{3}_{\geq 3}:=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\frac{B(\xi,\eta,\rho)}{D(\xi,\eta,\rho)}\Big\{2\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)|\eta|^{1/2}(|\xi|-|\eta|+|\rho|)\\
&-2\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}(-|\xi|-|\eta|+|\rho|)\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.37)

Notice that if
\xi,\eta,\rho\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi+\eta+\rho=0
then

D(\xi,\eta,\rho)=\frac{-4|\xi||\eta||\rho|}{\max(|\xi|,|\eta|,|\rho|)}.

(4.38)

Using (
4.33
), Lemma
2.2
(iv), and (
2.13
), and letting
A^{\prime}:=A/D
and
B^{\prime}=B/D
, we have

\begin{split}\begin{split}&\big\|A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\cdot\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\big\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})/2}2^{(2N_{0}-1)\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})},\\
&\big\|B^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\cdot\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\big\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})/2}2^{(2N_{0}-1)\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}2^{-|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2},\end{split}\end{split}

(4.39)

for any
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Recalling also the definition (
4.20
) and using Lemma
2.1
(ii) (with the two high frequencies estimated in
L^{2}
and the low frequency estimated in
L^{\infty}
), we have

\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\geq 3}(t)+\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\geq 3}(t)+\mathcal{E}^{3}_{\geq 3}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon(t)\big\|(h+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega)(t)\big\|_{H^{N_{0}}}

(4.40)

for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
. Moreover, the formulas
\partial_{t}h^{\ast}=|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega^{\ast}+\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2}^{h^{\ast}}
and

\partial_{t}\omega^{\ast}=-|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}h^{\ast}+\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2}^{\omega^{\ast}}
in (
3.31
) show that

\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\geq 3}=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{7}-\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{10},\qquad\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}^{3}_{\geq 3}=\mathcal{B}_{\geq 3}^{8}-\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{11},

where the quartic terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{10}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{11}
are defined in (
4.15
)–(
4.16
). Recalling also (
4.34
), the desired conclusion of the lemma follows.
∎

4.2.
Analysis of the quartic bulk integrals

We examine now more closely the space-time integrals generated in Lemma
4.1
.
We define first two general classes of symbols, “weakly admissible” and “regular”,
that appear in many of our operators.
In the next section we will need to introduce the more specific class of “admissible” symbols
(see Definition
5.1
).

Definition 4.3

(Weakly Admissible Symbols)
.

A symbol
M:\mathbb{H}_{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}
will be called “weakly admissible” if:

(i)
M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=0
if one of the arguments
\xi_{j}
is equal to
0
;

(ii) For any lattice point
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}

{\|M\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}\|}_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{2\widetilde{k}_{3}}2^{N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}2^{N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{2},0)},

(4.41)

see (
2.9
).
Here
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi)=\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4})

and
\widetilde{k}_{1}\geq\widetilde{k}_{2}\geq\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq\widetilde{k}_{4}

is the decreasing rearrangement of the set
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}
.

Definition 4.4

(Regular Symbols)
.

A symbol
m:\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}\to{\mathbb{C}}
, will be called “regular of class
\mathcal{M}^{b}
”
if for every
\underline{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}

we have

{\big\|m\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}\big\|}_{\mathcal{HM}_{8}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{(b-1/2)\widetilde{k}_{3}}\cdot 2^{N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}2^{N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{2},0)},

(4.42)

(recall Definition
2.3
) for some
b\geq 0
.

Remark 4.5
.

(i) A regular symbol of class
\mathcal{M}^{5/2}
is weakly admissible, due to Lemma
2.2
(iv). Regular symbols of class
\mathcal{M}^{5/2}
will often be called simply “regular” in the rest of the paper.

(ii) Using Lemma
2.5
and Lemma
2.2
(iv), we have

\Big|\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h_{1}}(\xi_{1})\widehat{h_{2}}(\xi_{2})\widehat{h_{3}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{h_{4}}(\xi_{4})M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\Big|\lesssim A_{2}^{2}A_{\infty}^{2},

(4.43)

provided that
M
is a weakly admissible quartic symbol and the
functions
h_{j}
satisfy
\|h_{j}\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\lesssim A_{2}
and
\|h_{j}\|_{\widetilde{W}^{2}}\lesssim A_{\infty}
,
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
; recall (
2.26
).

We now calculate explicitly the quartic bulk terms in Lemma
4.1
.
We will further manipulate and re-arrange these symbols in Lemma
4.7

to obtain more convenient expressions for the purpose of verifying
suitable non-resonance conditions later on.

Lemma 4.6
.

With the hypothesis and notation in Lemma
4.1
, for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
we have

\sum_{a\in\{1,\ldots,11\}}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{a}(t)=\mathcal{Q}_{1}(h^{\ast},h^{\ast},h^{\ast},\omega^{\ast})(t)+\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},h^{\ast})(t)+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}(t),

(4.44)

where
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}(t)
is an acceptable error satisfying

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}(t)|\lesssim A^{2}[\varepsilon(t)]^{3},

(4.45)

and

\begin{split}\mathcal{Q}_{1}(h^{\ast},h^{\ast},h^{\ast},\omega^{\ast})&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},h^{\ast})&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma).\end{split}

(4.46)

The symbols
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
are given by

\begin{split}&M_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=\sum_{a\in\{1,\ldots,14\}}M_{1}^{a}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
&M_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=\sum_{a\in\{1,\ldots,6\}}M_{2}^{a}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\end{split}

(4.47)

where
M_{1}^{a}
and
M_{2}^{b}
are weakly admissible (see Definition
4.3
)
given explicitly by

\begin{split}M^{1}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\sigma|^{1/2}\Big\{[1-\chi(\sigma+\rho,\eta)][|\sigma+\rho||\xi|-|\sigma||\xi|+\rho\xi]\\
&+[1-\chi(\sigma+\eta,\rho)][|\sigma+\eta||\xi|-|\sigma||\xi|+\eta\xi]+\big[|\sigma||\xi|+\sigma\xi\big]\Big\},\end{split}

(4.48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}M_{1}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&-m_{N_{0}}(\xi+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)(\xi+\sigma)\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}|\rho|\chi(\sigma,\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)\\
&-m_{N_{0}}(\eta+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\xi)(\eta+\sigma)\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}|\rho|\chi(\sigma,\eta)\chi(\rho,\xi),\end{split}

(4.49)

\displaystyle M_{1}^{3}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=-2m_{N_{0}}(\xi+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)[(\xi+\sigma)\sigma-|\xi+\sigma||\sigma|]|\sigma|^{-1/2}|\rho|\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma,\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta),

(4.50)

\displaystyle M_{1}^{4}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi+\eta)\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma,\rho)\chi(\eta,\xi)|\eta|[(\rho+\sigma)\sigma-|\rho+\sigma||\sigma|]}{|\sigma|^{1/2}},

(4.51)

\displaystyle M_{1}^{5}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\frac{|\rho|q_{2}(\sigma,\eta+\rho)\chi(\rho,\eta)+2|\sigma|q_{2}(\rho+\sigma,\eta)\chi(\sigma,\rho)}{|\sigma|^{1/2}},

(4.52)

\displaystyle\begin{split}M_{1}^{6}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&-\frac{1}{2}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi+\rho)\chi(\sigma,\eta)\chi(\rho,\xi)(\xi+\rho)|\rho|\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta+\rho)\chi(\sigma,\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)(\eta+\rho)|\rho|\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2},\end{split}

(4.53)

\displaystyle\begin{split}M_{1}^{7}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&-\frac{1}{2}\xi m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\sigma,\eta+\rho)\chi(\rho,\eta)|\rho|\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\eta m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\chi(\sigma,\xi+\rho)\chi(\rho,\xi)|\rho|\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2},\end{split}

(4.54)

\displaystyle\begin{split}M_{1}^{8}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&-|\sigma|^{1/2}\big[(\sigma+\rho)\chi(\sigma,\rho)-\rho\big]\\
&\times\big\{\xi m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta)+\eta m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\chi(\rho+\sigma,\xi)\big\},\end{split}

(4.55)

\displaystyle M_{1}^{9}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=m_{N_{0}}(\xi)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta)b(\sigma,\rho),

(4.56)

\displaystyle M_{1}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}m_{N_{0}}(\xi+\rho)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)[\chi(\sigma,\eta)+\chi(\sigma,\xi+\rho)]\chi(\rho,\xi)|\rho||\sigma|^{3/2},

(4.57)

\displaystyle M_{1}^{11}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)[m_{N_{0}}(\xi+\eta)-m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)]\chi(\rho,\xi+\eta)\chi(\xi,\eta)|\rho||\sigma|^{1/2}\,|\xi|,

(4.58)

\displaystyle M_{1}^{12}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=2m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)[m_{N_{0}}(\eta)-m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)]\chi(\rho+\xi,\eta)|\sigma|^{1/2}a_{2}^{rr}(\rho,\xi),

(4.59)

\begin{split}M_{1}^{13}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=6A^{\prime}(\eta+\rho,\sigma,\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}(|\eta+\rho|+|\sigma|-|\xi|)n_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho)\\
&+6A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}n_{2}^{ri}(\rho,\sigma),\end{split}

(4.60)

\begin{split}M_{1}^{14}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=4B^{\prime}(\eta+\rho,\sigma,\xi)|\eta+\rho|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}|\sigma|^{1/2}n_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho)\\
&+2B^{\prime}(\rho+\sigma,\eta,\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}(|\rho+\sigma|+|\eta|-|\xi|)n_{2}^{ri}(\rho,\sigma)\\
&+B^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho+\sigma|)n_{2}^{ri}(\rho,\sigma)\\
&-2B^{\prime}(\xi,\eta+\rho,\sigma)|\eta+\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\sigma|-|\eta+\rho|)n_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho)\\
&-2B^{\prime}(\xi,\sigma,\eta+\rho)|\sigma|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta+\rho|-|\sigma|)n_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho),\end{split}

(4.61)

and

\begin{split}M^{1}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=\frac{2m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}}{|\eta|^{1/2}}\big\{\eta(\sigma+\rho)\chi(\eta,\sigma+\rho)\chi(\rho,\sigma)+\eta\sigma\chi(\sigma+\rho,\eta)\\
&-\eta\sigma\chi(\rho,\sigma+\eta)-\rho\eta\chi(\eta+\rho,\sigma)+(|\rho|-|\sigma+\rho|)|\eta|\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma+\rho,\eta)\big\},\end{split}

(4.62)

\displaystyle M_{2}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\eta,\sigma)\widetilde{\chi}(\rho,\eta+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}[|\eta+\sigma||\rho|+(\eta+\sigma)\rho]}{|\rho|^{1/2}},

(4.63)

\begin{split}M_{2}^{3}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=|\rho|^{1/2}\big[(\rho+\sigma)\chi(\rho,\sigma)-\sigma\big]\\
&\times\big\{|\xi|^{1/2}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta)\eta|\eta|^{-1/2}+|\eta|^{1/2}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\chi(\rho+\sigma,\xi)\xi|\xi|^{-1/2}\big\},\end{split}

(4.64)

\displaystyle M_{2}^{4}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=2m_{N_{0}}(\xi)[m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)-m_{N_{0}}(\xi)]\chi(\rho+\eta,\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}a_{2}^{ii}(\rho,\eta),

(4.65)

\begin{split}M_{2}^{5}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=6A^{\prime}(\xi+\rho,\eta,\sigma)|\sigma|^{1/2}(|\xi+\rho|+|\eta|-|\sigma|)n_{2}^{ii}(\xi,\rho)\\
&+3A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho+\sigma|)n_{2}^{ri}(\sigma,\rho),\end{split}

(4.66)

\begin{split}M_{2}^{6}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=4B^{\prime}(\xi+\rho,\eta,\sigma)|\xi+\rho|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\sigma|^{1/2}n_{2}^{ii}(\xi,\rho)\\
&+2B^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}n_{2}^{ri}(\sigma,\rho)\\
&-2B^{\prime}(\rho+\sigma,\eta,\xi)|\eta|^{1/2}(|\rho+\sigma|+|\xi|-|\eta|)n_{2}^{ri}(\sigma,\rho)\\
&-2B^{\prime}(\sigma,\xi+\rho,\eta)|\xi+\rho|^{1/2}(|\sigma|+|\eta|-|\xi+\rho|)n_{2}^{ii}(\xi,\rho)\\
&-2B^{\prime}(\sigma,\eta,\xi+\rho)|\eta|^{1/2}(|\sigma|+|\xi+\rho|-|\eta|)n_{2}^{ii}(\xi,\rho).\end{split}

(4.67)

The new symbols
a_{2}^{rr}
,
a_{2}^{ii}
,
b
,
n_{2}^{ri}
,
n_{2}^{rr}
, and
n_{2}^{ii}
are defined in (
4.73
), (
4.75
), and (
4.78
)–(
4.80
), and the symbols
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
are defined by
A^{\prime}:=A/D
and
B^{\prime}:=B/D
.

Proof.

The idea is to examine the formulas (
4.6
)–(
4.16
),
extract the purely quartic terms expressed in terms of the main variables
h^{\ast}
and
\omega^{\ast}
,
and show that the errors are indeed quintic remainders that do not lose derivatives.
In this proof
X\sim Y
means that
X-Y
is an acceptable quintic error,

|X-Y|(t)\lesssim A^{2}[\varepsilon(t)]^{3}
for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
.

Step 1: the terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{1}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{2}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{3}
.
Using (
3.7
) and (
3.21
) we have

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{1}\sim\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{1}^{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),

and

\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{2}\sim\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{2}^{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),

where the symbols
M_{1}^{1}
and
M_{2}^{1}
are defined in (
4.48
) and (
4.62
). These identities follow by replacing
h
with
h^{\ast}
and
|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi
with
\omega^{\ast}
at the expense of quadratic errors, and noticing that there is no derivative loss since the operators
G_{3}
and
L_{3}
are smoothing (see (
3.10
) and (
3.24
)).

For the term
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{3}
in (
4.8
) we use
the expansions (
3.13
) for

and (
3.39
) for
\alpha
, the definitions (

),
and the formula (

,
to see that

\begin{split}-2&\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}\partial_{x}(T_{V}h)\cdot T_{\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h\,dx\\
&=\frac{-2}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{y,\xi,\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\big\{m_{N_{0}}(y)iy\chi(y-\xi,\xi)\widehat{V}(y-\xi)\widehat{h}(\xi)\cdot\widehat{\alpha}(-y-\eta)\chi(-y-\eta,\eta)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)\widehat{h}(\eta)\big\}\\
&\sim\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{1}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\end{split}

where the symbol
M_{1}^{2}
is defined in (
4.49
) by symmetrization in
\xi
and
\eta
(to avoid derivative loss such that
M_{1}^{2}
becomes a regular symbol
4
4

4

For presentation purposes, we postpone the routine proofs of regularity/admissibility of symbols to section 6. The symbols
M_{i}^{j}
are converted into the slightly simpler symbols

in Lemma

, and the symbols

are then all analyzed, sometimes fully symmetrized, in section 6.
). Also

\begin{split}2&\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}J^{N_{0}}G_{2}(\phi,h)\cdot T_{\alpha}J^{N_{0}}h\,dx\\
&=\frac{2}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{x,\xi,\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\big\{m_{N_{0}}(y)q_{2}(y-\xi,\xi)\widehat{\phi}(y-\xi)\widehat{h}(\xi)\cdot\widehat{\alpha}(-y-\eta)\chi(-y-\eta,\eta)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)\widehat{h}(\eta)\big\}\\
&\sim\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{1}^{3}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\end{split}

using (
3.6
) and (
3.9
). Therefore

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{3}\sim\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)\big[M_{1}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)+M_{1}^{3}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\big].

Step 2: the terms
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{a}
,
a\in\{4,5,6,7\}
.
To identify properly the quartic terms we expand

\displaystyle\widehat{h}(\xi)-\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\frac{1}{2}|\xi-\eta|\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)+\widehat{\mathcal{R}^{1}_{\geq 3}}(\xi),

(4.68)

(4.69)

\displaystyle\widehat{V}(\xi)-i\xi|\xi|^{-1/2}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}i|\xi-\eta|^{1/2}\big(\xi\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)-\eta\big)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)+\widehat{\mathcal{R}^{3}_{\geq 3}}(\xi),

(4.70)

where the cubic remainders

satisfy

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{R}^{1}_{\geq 3}(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{2}_{\geq 3}(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}-1/2}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{3}_{\geq 3}(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}}&\lesssim A\varepsilon(t)^{2},\\
\|\mathcal{R}^{1}_{\geq 3}(t)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{2}_{\geq 3}(t)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-1/2}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{3}_{\geq 3}(t)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-1}}&\lesssim\varepsilon(t)^{3},\\
\end{split}

(4.71)

for any

. These identities follow from the definitions (

), (

), and the expansions (

)–(

) and (

)–(

Using the formulas we can write

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{4}\sim\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)\big[M_{1}^{4}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)+M_{1}^{5}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\big],

These four formulas can be justified in a similar way. We only provide the details for the more complicated identity for
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{6}
. We symmetrize first in

and

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{6}=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\big\{\widehat{h}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{V}(\rho)&-\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)i\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}\big\}\\
&\times[i\xi m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)+i\eta m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\chi(\rho,\xi)].\end{split}

Then we use the identities (

) and (

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{6}&\sim\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}\big\{\widehat{h}(\xi)\widehat{h}(\eta)-\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\big\}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)i\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times[i\xi m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\chi(\rho,\eta)+i\eta m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\chi(\rho,\xi)]\\
&+\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{1}^{8}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma).\end{split}

and

Step 3: the terms
\mathcal{B}^{8}_{\geq 4}
,

(4.75)

(4.76)

Step 4: the terms
\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 4}
,
\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 4}
.
To analyze the more complicated expressions in (

defined in Lemma
3.4
. We define the new symbols

\displaystyle\begin{split}n_{2}^{ri}(\rho,\eta)&:=n^{ri}(\rho,\eta)-\chi(\rho,\eta)|\rho||\eta|^{1/2}/2+(\rho+\eta)\eta|\eta|^{-1/2}\chi(\eta,\rho)\\
&=\widetilde{\chi}(\eta,\rho)\frac{(\rho+\eta)\eta-|\rho+\eta||\eta|}{|\eta|^{1/2}}-\frac{|\rho||\eta|}{2|\eta|^{1/2}}\chi(\rho,\eta)+\frac{2\rho\eta+\eta^{2}}{2|\eta|^{1/2}}\chi(\eta,\rho),\end{split}

(4.78)

\displaystyle\begin{split}n_{2}^{rr}(\rho,\eta)&:=\frac{1}{2}\Big\{n^{rr}(\rho,\eta)+n^{rr}(\eta,\rho)+\frac{1}{2}\chi(\eta,\rho)|\eta||\rho|^{1/2}+\frac{1}{2}\chi(\rho,\eta)|\rho||\eta|^{1/2}\Big\}\\
&=\frac{1}{4}|\rho+\eta|^{1/2}\big[\chi(\rho,\eta)|\rho|+\chi(\eta,\rho)|\eta|\big],\end{split}

(4.79)

\displaystyle\begin{split}n_{2}^{ii}(\rho,\eta)&:=\frac{1}{2}\Big\{n^{ii}(\rho,\eta)+n^{ii}(\eta,\rho)+(\rho+\eta)\eta|\eta|^{-1/2}\chi(\eta,\rho)+(\eta+\rho)\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}\chi(\rho,\eta)\Big\}\\
&=|\rho+\eta|^{1/2}\frac{|\eta||\rho|+\eta\rho}{2|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}}-\frac{1}{2}\big[\chi(\rho,\eta)+\chi(\eta,\rho)\big]|\rho+\eta|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}.\end{split}

(4.80)

Using the identities in Lemma
3.4
we notice that we have the decompositions

\begin{split}&\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}=\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{2}+\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3},\\
&\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{2}}(\xi):=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)n^{ri}_{2}(\xi-\eta,\eta),\\
&\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}:=T_{\beta+|\partial_{x}|h^{\ast}/2}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega^{\ast}-\partial_{x}T_{V-\partial_{x}|\partial_{x}|^{-1/2}\omega^{\ast}}h^{\ast}+H^{\ast}_{\geq 3},\end{split}

(4.81)

and

\begin{split}&\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 2}=\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{2}+\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 3},\\
&\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{2}}(\xi):=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\big\{\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)n^{rr}_{2}(\xi-\eta,\eta)+\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)n^{ii}_{2}(\xi-\eta,\eta)\big\},\\
&\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}:=-T_{\beta+|\partial_{x}|h^{\ast}/2}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}h^{\ast}-\partial_{x}T_{V-\partial_{x}|\partial_{x}|^{-1/2}\omega^{\ast}}\omega^{\ast}+\Omega^{\ast}_{\geq 3}.\end{split}

(4.82)

We see easily that

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{10}&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{1}^{13}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\\
&+\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{2}^{5}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{10},\end{split}

(4.83)

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\geq 4}^{11}&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{1}^{14}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\\
&+\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\sigma)M_{2}^{6}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)+\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{11},\end{split}

(4.84)

where the symbols
M_{1}^{13}
,
M_{1}^{14}
,
M_{2}^{5}
,
M_{2}^{6}
are defined in (
4.60
), (
4.61
),(
4.66
), and (
4.67
), and the quintic remainders
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{10}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\geq 5}^{11}
are defined by

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5}&:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\big\{6|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\\
&+3|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\rho)+6|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\rho)\big\},\end{split}

(4.85)

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5}&:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}B^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\big\{2|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\big[2\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)+\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\rho)\big]\\
&+|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\big[2\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\rho)\big]\\
&-2|\eta|^{1/2}(|\xi|-|\eta|+|\rho|)\big[\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)+\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}}(\rho)\big]\big\}.\end{split}

(4.86)

We have to prove that these remainders are acceptable quintic error, i.e.
\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5}\sim 0
and
\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5}\sim 0
. The bounds (
4.32
) show that

\begin{split}&\big\|A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\cdot\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\big\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{9}}\lesssim 2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})/2}2^{(2N_{0}-1)\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})},\\
&\big\|B^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\cdot\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\big\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{9}}\lesssim 2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})/2}2^{(2N_{0}-1)\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}2^{-|k_{1}-k_{2}|/2},\end{split}

(4.87)

for any
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. The functions
H^{\ast}_{\geq 3}
and
\Omega^{\ast}_{\geq 3}
satisfy suitable cubic bounds without derivative loss (see (
3.36
)), so the corresponding contributions lead to acceptable quintic errors.

To bound the remaining contributions we need symmetrization to avoid derivative loss. Let

X:=\beta+|\partial_{x}|h^{\ast}/2,\qquad\qquad Y:=V-\partial_{x}|\partial_{x}|^{-1/2}\omega^{\ast},

Since
\beta=\sqrt{1+\alpha}-1
, it follows from Lemma
3.2
and (
3.39
) that

\|X(t)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-3}}+\|Y(t)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon(t)^{2},\qquad t\in[T_{1},T_{2}].

(4.88)

We need to bound the contributions of the functions
T_{X}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega^{\ast}-\partial_{x}T_{Y}h^{\ast}
and
-T_{X}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}h^{\ast}-\partial_{x}T_{Y}\omega^{\ast}
(compare with the terms
\mathcal{N}^{h^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\omega^{\ast}}_{\geq 3}
in (
4.81
)–(
4.82
)). Then we decompose

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5}&\sim\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,1}+\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,2}+\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,3}+\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,4},\end{split}

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,1}&:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)(-6)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\mathcal{F}[\partial_{x}T_{Y}h^{\ast}](\rho),\end{split}

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,2}&:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\big\{-6|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\mathcal{F}[T_{X}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}h^{\ast}](\xi)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+6|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\mathcal{F}[T_{X}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega^{\ast}](\rho)\big\},\end{split}

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,3}&:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\big\{-6|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\mathcal{F}[\partial_{x}T_{Y}\omega^{\ast}](\xi)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad-3|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\mathcal{F}[\partial_{x}T_{Y}h^{\ast}](\rho)\big\},\end{split}

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,4}&:=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{R}}A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)3|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho|)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\mathcal{F}[T_{X}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega^{\ast}](\rho).\end{split}

By symmetrization we can rewrite these expressions in the form

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,1}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{Y}(\sigma)L_{1}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
L_{1}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&-2A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}i(\rho+\sigma)\chi(\sigma,\rho)\\
&-2A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2}i(\xi+\sigma)\chi(\sigma,\xi)\\
&-2A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma)|\rho|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta+\sigma|^{1/2}i(\eta+\sigma)\chi(\sigma,\eta),\end{split}

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,2}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{X}(\sigma)L_{2}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
L_{2}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&-3A^{\prime}(\xi+\sigma,\rho,\eta)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}(|\xi+\sigma|-|\eta|+|\rho|)\chi(\sigma,\xi)\\
&-3A^{\prime}(\eta+\sigma,\rho,\xi)|\eta|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}(|\eta+\sigma|-|\xi|+|\rho|)\chi(\sigma,\eta)\\
&+6A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\chi(\sigma,\rho),\end{split}

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,3}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{Y}(\sigma)L_{3}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
L_{3}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&-3A^{\prime}(\xi+\sigma,\eta,\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}(|\xi+\sigma|+|\eta|-|\rho|)i(\xi+\sigma)\chi(\sigma,\xi)\\
&-3A^{\prime}(\eta+\sigma,\xi,\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}(|\eta+\sigma|+|\xi|-|\rho|)i(\eta+\sigma)\chi(\sigma,\eta)\\
&-3A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho+\sigma|)i(\rho+\sigma)\chi(\sigma,\rho),\end{split}

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,4}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{X}(\sigma)L_{4}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
L_{4}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&+A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho+\sigma|)|\rho|^{1/2}\chi(\sigma,\rho)\\
&+A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2}(|\eta|+|\rho|-|\xi+\sigma|)|\xi|^{1/2}\chi(\sigma,\xi)\\
&+A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma)|\eta+\sigma|^{1/2}(|\rho|+|\xi|-|\eta+\sigma|)|\eta|^{1/2}\chi(\sigma,\eta).\end{split}

In view of (
4.87
), the multipliers
L_{a}^{10}
,
a\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, do not lose derivatives after symmetrization, and are all regular. We provide the complete details for the symbol
L_{1}^{10}
. We look at

L_{1}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\varphi_{k_{3}}(\rho)\varphi_{k_{4}}(\sigma),

for
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and let
\widetilde{k}_{1}\geq\widetilde{k}_{2}\geq\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq\widetilde{k}_{4}
denote the decreasing rearrangement of
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}
. Notice that the localized symbol vanishes if
k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{2}-10
, due to the presence of the function
\chi
. So we may assume that
k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{2}-10
and
k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}
. Since
L_{1}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=L_{1}^{10}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)=L_{1}^{10}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)
we may also assume that
\{k_{1},k_{2}\}=\{\widetilde{k}_{1},\widetilde{k}_{2}\}
, so
\xi,\eta
are the large variables and
\rho,\sigma
are the small variables.

We have two cases: in the ”smoothing case”
k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{2}-40
there is no derivative loss, and each of the 3 terms in the definition of
L_{1}^{10}
satisfies suitable Hörmander-Michlin bounds which follow from the bounds (
4.87
). On the other hand, if
k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{2}-40
then we rewrite

\begin{split}L_{1}^{10}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=&-2i|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\big[A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2}(\xi+\sigma)-A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}\xi\big]\\
&-2i|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\big[A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma)|\eta+\sigma|^{1/2}(\eta+\sigma)-A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta)|\eta|^{1/2}\eta\big]\\
&-2i|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}(\rho+\sigma)\big[A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}\chi(\sigma,\rho)-A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}\big],\end{split}

since
\chi(\sigma,\xi)=\chi(\sigma,\eta)=1
,
A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)=A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi)=A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta)
, and
\xi+\eta+\rho+\sigma=0
. The expression in each line defines a regular symbol, due to the bounds (
4.87
).

The analysis of the symbols
L_{2}^{10},L_{3}^{10},L_{4}^{10}
is similar. In all cases we may assume that
k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{2}-10
. In the ”smoothing case”
k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{2}-40
there is no derivative loss, and the expression in each line defines a regular symbol, while in the ”unbalanced case”
k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{2}-40
we have to reorganize some of the symbols suitably to avoid derivative loss.

Using Lemma
2.5
it then follows that
|\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,a}|\lesssim A^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{3}
,
a\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,
as desired.

The analysis of the quintic term
\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5}
is similar. We can decompose as before

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5}&\sim\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5,1}+\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5,2}+\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5,3}+\mathcal{B}^{10}_{\geq 5,4},\\
\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5,1}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{Y}(\sigma)L_{1}^{11}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5,2}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{X}(\sigma)L_{2}^{11}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5,3}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{Y}(\sigma)L_{3}^{11}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5,4}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{X}(\sigma)L_{4}^{11}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma).\end{split}

The multipliers
L_{a}^{11}
,
a\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, can be calculated explicitly using the definition (
4.86
), and do not lose derivatives after symmetrization. Using again Lemma
2.5
it follows that
|\mathcal{B}^{11}_{\geq 5,a}|\lesssim A^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{3}
,
a\in\{1,2,3,4\}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

4.3.
The quartic terms

In this subsection we combine some of the multipliers in Lemma
4.6
and use (some) of the symmetries
of the operators
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
to obtain more convenient formulas.

Lemma 4.7
.

With the hypothesis and notation in Lemma
4.6
, for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{Q}_{1}(h^{\ast},h^{\ast},h^{\ast},\omega^{\ast})&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\sigma)K_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\\
\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},h^{\ast})&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\xi)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\eta)\widehat{\omega^{\ast}}(\rho)\widehat{h^{\ast}}(\sigma)K_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma).\end{split}

(4.89)

The symbols
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
are given by

K_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=\sum_{a\in\{1,\ldots,9\}}K_{1}^{a}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),\qquad K_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=\sum_{a\in\{1,\ldots,4\}}K_{2}^{a}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma),

(4.90)

where

\begin{split}&K^{1}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)}{|\sigma|^{1/2}}\Big\{|\sigma|\big[(|\xi||\rho+\sigma|+\xi(\rho+\sigma))+(|\xi||\eta+\sigma|+\xi(\eta+\sigma))-(|\xi||\sigma|+\xi\sigma)\big]\\
&-|\sigma|\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta)\big[|\xi||\rho+\sigma|-|\xi||\sigma|\big]-|\sigma|\chi(\eta+\sigma,\rho)\big[|\xi||\eta+\sigma|-|\xi||\sigma|\big]\\
&-|\sigma|\chi(\sigma,\rho)\widetilde{\chi}(\rho+\sigma,\eta)(|\xi||\rho+\sigma|+\xi(\rho+\sigma))-|\sigma|\chi(\sigma,\eta)\widetilde{\chi}(\eta+\sigma,\rho)(|\xi||\eta+\sigma|+\xi(\eta+\sigma))\\
&-|\sigma|\chi(\sigma,\rho)\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta)\xi(\sigma+\rho)-|\sigma|\chi(\sigma,\eta)\chi(\eta+\sigma,\rho)\xi(\sigma+\eta)\\
&-\frac{1}{2}[|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)+|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)]\big[\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma,\eta+\rho)(|\xi||\sigma|+\xi\sigma)+\chi(\sigma,\eta+\rho)\xi\sigma\big]\Big\},\end{split}

(4.91)

\begin{split}K_{1}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,&\sigma):=-\frac{m_{N_{0}}(\rho+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)|\xi|\chi(\xi,\eta)\big\{\sigma(\rho+\sigma)(1-\chi(\rho,\sigma))-|\sigma||\rho+\sigma|\widetilde{\chi}(\rho,\sigma)\big\}}{|\sigma|^{1/2}}\\
&-\frac{m_{N_{0}}(\eta+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\rho)|\xi|\chi(\xi,\rho)\big\{\sigma(\eta+\sigma)(1-\chi(\eta,\sigma))-|\sigma||\eta+\sigma|\widetilde{\chi}(\eta,\sigma)\big\}}{|\sigma|^{1/2}},\end{split}

(4.92)

\begin{split}K_{1}^{3}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=&\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta+\rho)[|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)+|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)]}{2|\sigma|^{1/2}}\\
&\times\big\{((\xi+\sigma)\sigma-|\xi+\sigma||\sigma|)\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma,\xi)+(\xi+\sigma)\sigma\chi(\sigma,\xi)\big\},\end{split}

(4.93)

\displaystyle K_{1}^{4}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=\frac{1}{2}m_{N_{0}}(\eta)m_{N_{0}}(\rho)b(\sigma,\xi)[\chi(\xi+\sigma,\eta)+\chi(\xi+\sigma,\rho)],

(4.94)

\displaystyle K_{1}^{5}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{m_{N_{0}}(\eta+\rho)m_{N_{0}}(\xi)|\sigma|^{3/2}[\chi(\sigma,\xi)+\chi(\sigma,\eta+\rho)][|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)+|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)]}{4},

(4.95)

\displaystyle K_{1}^{6}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-\frac{1}{2}m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)[m_{N_{0}}(\eta+\rho)-m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)]\chi(\xi,\eta+\rho)|\sigma|^{1/2}|\xi|\big[|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)+|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)],

(4.96)

\displaystyle K_{1}^{7}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=2m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)[m_{N_{0}}(\xi)-m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)]\chi(\eta+\rho,\xi)|\sigma|^{1/2}a_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho),

(4.97)

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=6n_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho)A^{\prime}(\xi,\sigma,\eta+\rho)|\xi|^{1/2}(|\eta+\rho|+|\sigma|-|\xi|)\\
&+6n_{2}^{ri}(\xi,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\rho|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2},\end{split}

(4.98)

K_{1}^{9}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-2n_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho)B^{\prime\prime}(\eta+\rho,\sigma,\xi)+n_{2}^{ri}(\xi,\sigma)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma),

(4.99)

and

\begin{split}K^{1}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}}{|\eta|^{1/2}}\Big\{\chi(\rho,\sigma)\big[\eta(\sigma+\rho)+|\eta||\sigma+\rho|\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma+\rho,\eta)\big]\\
&\qquad-\eta\sigma\chi(\rho,\sigma+\eta)-\rho\eta\chi(\eta+\rho,\sigma)+(|\rho|-|\sigma+\rho|)|\eta|\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma+\rho,\eta)\Big\}\\
&+\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}}{|\rho|^{1/2}}\Big\{\chi(\eta,\sigma)\big[\rho(\sigma+\eta)+|\rho||\sigma+\eta|\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma+\eta,\rho)\big]\\
&\qquad-\rho\sigma\chi(\eta,\sigma+\rho)-\eta\rho\chi(\rho+\eta,\sigma)+(|\eta|-|\sigma+\eta|)|\rho|\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma+\eta,\rho)\Big\},\end{split}

(4.100)

\displaystyle K_{2}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)

\displaystyle:=2m_{N_{0}}(\xi)[m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)-m_{N_{0}}(\xi)]\chi(\eta+\rho,\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}a_{2}^{ii}(\eta,\rho),

(4.101)

\begin{split}K_{2}^{3}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=6n_{2}^{ii}(\eta,\rho)A^{\prime}(\xi,\sigma,\eta+\rho)|\sigma|^{1/2}(|\eta+\rho|+|\xi|-|\sigma|)\\
&+3n_{2}^{ri}(\sigma,\xi)A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2}(|\rho|+|\eta|-|\xi+\sigma|),\end{split}

(4.102)

K_{2}^{4}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-2n_{2}^{ii}(\eta,\rho)B^{\prime\prime}(\eta+\rho,\xi,\sigma)-n_{2}^{ri}(\sigma,\xi)B^{\prime\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma).

(4.103)

All the symbols
K_{i}^{j}
are real-valued, even, and symmetric in the second and third variables, i.e.

K_{i}^{j}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=K_{i}^{j}(-\xi,-\eta,-\rho,-\sigma),\qquad K_{i}^{j}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=K_{i}^{j}(\xi,\rho,\eta,\sigma)

(4.104)

for any
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{H}_{R}^{3}
. The symbols
B^{\prime\prime},B^{\prime\prime\prime}:{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\infty}\to\mathbb{R}
are given by

\begin{split}B^{\prime\prime}(x,y,&z):=-2B^{\prime}(x,y,z)|x|^{1/2}|y|^{1/2}|z|^{1/2}\\
&+B^{\prime}(z,x,y)|x|^{1/2}(|z|+|y|-|x|)+B^{\prime}(z,y,x)|y|^{1/2}(|z|+|x|-|y|),\end{split}

(4.105)

\begin{split}B^{\prime\prime\prime}(x,y,&z):=B^{\prime}(x,y,z)|z|^{1/2}(|x|+|y|-|z|)\\
&+B^{\prime}(y,z,x)|x|^{1/2}(|y|+|z|-|x|)+B^{\prime}(z,x,y)|y|^{1/2}(|z|+|x|-|y|).\end{split}

(4.106)

Proof.

The symbol
K_{1}^{1}
is obtained by combining the symbols
M_{1}^{1}
,
M_{1}^{5}
,
M_{1}^{7}
,
M_{1}^{8}
.
The symbol
K_{1}^{2}
is obtained by combining the symbols
M_{1}^{2}
and
M_{1}^{3}
.
The symbol
K_{1}^{3}
is obtained by combining the symbols
M_{1}^{4}
and
M_{1}^{6}
,
while
K_{2}^{1}
is obtained by combining the symbols
M_{2}^{1}
,
M_{2}^{2}
, and
M_{2}^{3}
.
In all cases we symmetrize the symbols
K_{i}^{j}
in the variables
\eta
and
\rho
.
∎

5.
Estimates on quartic space-time integrals

In view of Lemmas
4.1
and
4.6
our main goal is to show that,
for
T_{1}\leq T_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
(h,\phi)\in C([T_{1},T_{2}]:H^{N_{0}}\times\dot{H}^{N_{0},1/2})

a solution of the system (
1.4
) satisfying (
1.7
), we have

\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big[\mathcal{Q}_{1}(h^{\ast},h^{\ast},h^{\ast},\omega^{\ast})(s)+\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},h^{\ast})(s)\Big]\,ds\Big|\lesssim A^{2}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}[\varepsilon(s)]^{3}\,ds,

(5.1)

for any
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
. To prove this we need to integrate by parts in time (the method of normal forms), to convert quartic expressions into quintic expressions. There are two main issues we need to be aware of: the presence of quartic resonances and the potential loss of derivative.

To address these issues, we first “complexify” the quartic terms, that is, we convert them into expressions involving the complex variables
(U^{+},U^{-})
defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&U=U^{+}:=h^{\ast}+i\omega^{\ast},\qquad\overline{U}=U^{-}:=h^{\ast}-i\omega^{\ast},\\
&h^{\ast}=\frac{1}{2}(U^{+}+U^{-}),\qquad\omega^{\ast}=\frac{1}{2i}(U^{+}-U^{-}).\end{split}

(5.2)

We also need to symmetrize our symbols. Note that the symbols
K_{i}^{a}
in (
4.90
) are symmetric with respect to the second and third variables, as stated in (
4.104
).
However, we may also replace them by their full symmetrization in the first three variables
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{a}
, where, by definition,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{K}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=\frac{1}{3}\Big[K(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)+K(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)+K(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\Big],\end{split}

(5.3)

for any symbol
K
symmetric in the second and third variables. Similarly, we may replace the symbols
K_{2}^{a}
with
\widetilde{K}_{2}^{a}
. In view of (
4.89
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}(2\pi R)^{3}\mathcal{Q}_{1}(h^{\ast},h^{\ast},h^{\ast},\omega^{\ast})&=\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{U}(\xi_{2})\widehat{U}(\xi_{3})\widehat{U}(\xi_{4})\frac{i}{16}K_{1}^{++++}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\\
&+\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{U}(\xi_{2})\widehat{U}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4})\frac{i}{16}K_{1}^{+++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\\
&+\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{U}(\xi_{2})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4})\frac{i}{32}SK_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\\
&+\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{2})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4})\frac{i}{16}K_{1}^{+---}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\\
&+\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{1})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{2})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4})\frac{i}{16}K_{1}^{----}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}),\end{split}

(5.4)

where, with
\widetilde{K}_{1}
defined as in (
5.3
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K_{1}^{++++}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=-\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}),\\
&K_{1}^{+++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})-\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{4},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})-\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{1})-\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{4},\xi_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2}),\\
&K_{1}^{+---}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{2})+\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})-\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1}),\\
&K_{1}^{----}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\widetilde{K}_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}),\end{split}

(5.5)

and the symbol
SK_{1}
is defined
by a suitable symmetrization according to the general formula

SL(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=3\widetilde{L}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+3\widetilde{L}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})-3\widetilde{L}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})-3\widetilde{L}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{1})

(5.6)

for any
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}_{R}^{3}
and any symbol

L:\mathbb{H}_{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}
symmetric in the second and the third variables (with
\widetilde{L}
defined as in (
5.3
)). Notice that
SL
satisfies the natural symmetries

\begin{split}&SL(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=SL(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=SL(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})=SL(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{3}),\\
&SL(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=-SL(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1},\xi_{2}).\end{split}

(5.7)

We can proceed similarly for the
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
type terms in (
4.89
) and write

\displaystyle\begin{split}(2\pi R)^{3}\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},h^{\ast})&=\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{U}(\xi_{2})\widehat{U}(\xi_{3})\widehat{U}(\xi_{4})\frac{-i}{16}K_{2}^{++++}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\\
&+\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{U}(\xi_{2})\widehat{U}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4})\frac{i}{16}K_{2}^{+++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\\
&+\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{U}(\xi_{2})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4})\frac{-i}{32}SK_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\\
&+\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{2})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4})\frac{i}{16}K_{2}^{+---}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\\
&+\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{1})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{2})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4})\frac{-i}{16}K_{2}^{----}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\end{split}

(5.8)

where
SK_{2}
is defined via (
5.6
), and, as in (
5.5
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K_{2}^{++++}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=-\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}),\\
&K_{2}^{+++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})-\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{4},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})-\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{1})-\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{4},\xi_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2}),\\
&K_{2}^{+---}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{2})+\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})-\widetilde{K}_{2}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1}),\\
&K_{2}^{----}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=-K_{2}^{++++}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}).\end{split}

(5.9)

To prove the bounds (
5.1
) we distinguish between the “resonant” contributions, that is,
those that have two copies of
U
and two copies of
\overline{U}
, and the other “non-resonant” contributions.
The phases associated to the non-resonant contributions are lower bounded, and we estimate them using Lemma
5.2
below.

The resonant contributions are harder to treat.
Since the associated phases can vanish, see (
1.11
) and (
1.12
),
we will need to verify
specific non-resonance (and regularity) properties
of the symbols in order to obtain a bound as in (
5.1
).
Sufficient non-resonant conditions are those stated in the assumptions of Lemma
5.3
,
see (
5.6
) and (
NR-BF
), which we will then verify in Sections
6
and
9
.

5.1.
Admissible symbols

To verify these conditions we introduce a suitable class of “admissible” symbols. Recall the notation

\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi):=\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4})
,
and that
\widetilde{k}_{1}\geq\widetilde{k}_{2}\geq\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq\widetilde{k}_{4}

denotes the decreasing rearrangement of the set
\{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
.
Also recall the Definitions
4.3
and
4.4
.

Definition 5.1

(Admissible Symbols)
.

We define the class
\mathcal{A}
of (quartic) “admissible symbols” as the class of symbols
m:\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\to\mathbb{C}

that satisfy the following:

(i) The symbol
m
is even

\displaystyle m(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=m(-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4})

(5.10)

for any
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
and weakly admissible (when restricted to
\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|m\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}\big\|}_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{2\widetilde{k}_{3}}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)},\qquad\text{for any }\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}.\end{split}

(5.11)

(ii) For any
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
and
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
, one can write

\displaystyle\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi)m(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi)\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\},\,i\neq j\in\{1,2,3,4\}}|\xi_{i}+\xi_{j}|^{\beta}F_{\beta;i,j}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})

(5.12)

where the functions
F_{\beta;i,j}=F_{\beta;i,j;\underline{k}}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
are even and satisfy the bounds

\displaystyle\big|2^{\alpha\cdot\underline{k}}\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}\big[F_{\beta;i,j}(\xi)\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi)\big]\big|\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{(2-\beta)\widetilde{k}_{3}}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)},

(5.13)

for
|\alpha|\leq 6
and
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
. Moreover, the functions
F_{\beta;i,j}
can be non-trivial only if
\beta=0
or
\max(k_{i},k_{j})\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}+6
.

Notice that property (i) follows from the stronger assumptions in property (ii), but we state it here mostly for clarity. Notice also the admisibility condition is invariant under permutations of the coordinates and that regular symbols in the class
\mathcal{M}^{5/2}
are admissible (with
\beta=0
).

For us the main point is that the symbols
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
defined in Lemma
4.7
are admissible after symmetrization in the first three variables, as we verify in section
6
. We can then apply Lemmas
5.2
and
5.3
below to prove acceptable quintic energy estimates, provided that we can check the non-resonant conditions (
NR-BF
) corresponding to Benjamin-Feir resonances.

5.2.
Non-resonant interactions

Given a multiplier
M:\mathbb{H}_{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}
and
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}:{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\to\mathbb{C}
,
we define the quatrilinear operator

\mathcal{A}_{M}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}):=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\widehat{f_{1}}(\xi_{1})\widehat{f_{2}}(\xi_{2})\widehat{f_{3}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{f_{4}}(\xi_{4}).

(5.14)

Our first main estimate for quartic bulks in this section is the following:

Lemma 5.2
.

If
M:\mathbb{H}_{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}
is an admissible quartic symbol (see Definition
5.1
), then

\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{A}_{M}(U,U,U,U^{\iota})(s)\,ds\Big|\lesssim[A(t_{1})\varepsilon(t_{1})]^{2}+[A(t_{2})\varepsilon(t_{2})]^{2}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}A^{2}[\varepsilon(s)]^{3}\,ds,

(5.15)

for any
\iota\in\{+,-\}
and any
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
.

Proof.

Step 1.
The function
U=h^{\ast}+i\omega^{\ast}
satisfies the equation

\begin{split}\partial_{t}U+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}U&=\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2}^{U}=\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,1}^{U}+\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,2}^{U}+\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U},\\
\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,1}^{U}&:=-iT_{\beta}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}U,\\
\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,2}^{U}&:=-\partial_{x}T_{V}U,\\
\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U}&:=(H_{2}^{\ast}+i\Omega_{2}^{\ast})+(H^{\ast}_{\geq 3}+i\Omega_{\geq 3}^{\ast}),\end{split}

(5.16)

as a consequence of (
3.31
). For any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
we also have

\begin{split}&\|U(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\lesssim A,\qquad\|U(t)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}}}\lesssim\varepsilon(t),\\
&\big\|\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U}(t)\big\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\lesssim A\varepsilon(t),\qquad\big\|\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U}(t)\big\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}}}\lesssim[\varepsilon(t)]^{2},\end{split}

(5.17)

as a consequence of the assumption (
4.1
), Lemma
3.4
, and Lemma
2.4
.

For any
s\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
we define the frequency envelopes

\begin{split}&A_{k}(s):=\sum_{l\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{-|l-k|/10}\|P_{l}U(s)\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\approx\sum_{l\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{-|l-k|/10}\big(2^{N_{0}l^{+}}\|P_{l}U(s)\|_{L^{2}}\big),\\
&B_{k}(s):=\sum_{l\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{-|l-k|/10}\big\|P_{l}\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U}(s)\big\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\approx\sum_{l\in{\mathbb{Z}}}2^{-|l-k|/10}\big(2^{N_{0}l^{+}}\big\|P_{l}\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U}(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}\big),\end{split}

(5.18)

and notice that, for any
s\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
,

\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[A_{k}(s)]^{2}\lesssim\|U(s)\|_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}\lesssim A^{2},\qquad\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}[B_{k}(s)]^{2}\lesssim\big\|\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U}(s)\big\|_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}\lesssim A^{2}[\varepsilon(s)]^{2}.

(5.19)

For (
5.15
) it suffices to prove that for any
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\iota\in\{+,-\}
, and
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
we have

\begin{split}&2^{[\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)-\min(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)]/10}\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{A}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\,ds\Big|\\
&\lesssim A_{l}^{2}(t_{1})[\varepsilon(t_{1})]^{2}+A^{2}_{l}(t_{2})[\varepsilon(t_{2})]^{2}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}A_{l}(s)[\varepsilon(s)]^{2}[\varepsilon(s)A_{l}(s)+B_{l}(s)]\,ds,\end{split}

(5.20)

for any
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in Z_{l}^{4}:=\{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}:\,l=\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\}
.

To bound the left-hand side of (
5.20
) we would like to integrate by parts in time.
We examine the formula (
5.16
) and let
\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2}=\mathcal{N}^{U}_{\geq 2}

and
\mathcal{N}^{U^{-}}_{\geq 2}=\overline{\mathcal{N}^{U}_{\geq 2}}
.
Let

\mathcal{T}_{M}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}):=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\frac{M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}{\Phi_{+++\iota}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\widehat{f_{1}}(\xi_{1})\widehat{f_{2}}(\xi_{2})\widehat{f_{3}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{f_{4}}(\xi_{4}),

(5.21)

where
\Phi_{+++\iota}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+\iota|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}
. We start from the identity

\begin{split}&\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(t_{2})-\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(t_{1})\\
&=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\,ds\end{split}

for any
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and use the formula
\partial_{t}U^{\pm}=\mp i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}U^{\pm}+\mathcal{N}^{U^{\pm}}_{\geq 2}
to expand the right-hand side. This gives the normal forms identity

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{A}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\,ds\\
&=-i\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Big\{\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2},P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2},P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\\
&\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2},P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\iota}}_{\geq 2})(s)\Big\}\,ds\\
&+i\big\{\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(t_{2})-\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(t_{1})\big\}.\end{split}

(5.22)

We will use this identity to bound the left-hand side of (
5.20
).

Step 2.
We would like to describe now the multiplier of the operator
\mathcal{T}_{M}

in (
5.21
), using the assumption that
M
is admissible.
We notice that for any
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}

\begin{split}&\big\|M^{\prime}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}\big\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }\iota=-\text{ and }k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-5,\\
2^{(\widetilde{k}_{3}-\widetilde{k}_{1})/2}&\text{ if }\iota=+\text{ or }k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-6.\end{cases}\end{split}

(5.23)

where
M^{\prime}:=M/\Phi_{+++\iota}
.

Indeed, assume first that
\iota=-
and
k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-5
.
The main observation is that

\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}+\sqrt{c}-\sqrt{a+b+c}\geq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}-\sqrt{a+b}\geq\sqrt{b}/2

(5.24)

for any real numbers
a\geq b\geq c\geq 0
, so that

\displaystyle{\Big\|\frac{\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(-\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3})}{\Phi_{+++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},-\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3})}\Big\|}_{S^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}.

(5.25)

The bound (
5.23
) then follows using also the product estimates (
2.13
),
and the assumptions (
5.11
).

On the other hand, if
\iota=+
or
k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-6
,
then the elliptic bounds
\Phi_{+++\iota}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\gtrsim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{1}/2}

hold in the support of the multiplier, and the bounds (
5.23
) follow again using the product estimates (
2.13
),
and the assumptions (
5.11
).

Step 3.
We examine now the terms in the right-hand side of (
5.22
).
The boundary terms can be estimated using just (
5.23
),
the bounds in the first line of (
5.17
), and Lemma
2.1
(ii),

\begin{split}\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}&(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|\\
&\lesssim A_{\widetilde{k}_{1}}(s)A_{\widetilde{k}_{2}}(s)(2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)}\varepsilon(s))(2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)}\varepsilon(s))\end{split}

for
s\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
. This is consistent with the desired estimates (
5.20
).

To control the space-time integrals we decompose
\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2}^{U^{\pm}}=\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,1}^{U^{\pm}}+\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,2}^{U^{\pm}}+\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U^{\pm}}

as in (
5.16
). The contribution of the nonlinear terms
\mathcal{N}_{\geq 2,3}^{U^{\pm}}
,
which do not lose derivatives, can be bounded using just (
5.17
), (
5.23
), and Lemma
2.1
(ii),

\begin{split}&\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,3},P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|+\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,3},P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|\\
&+\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,3},P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|+\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\iota}}_{\geq 2,3})(s)\big|\\
&\lesssim[B_{\widetilde{k}_{1}}(s)+\varepsilon(s)A_{\widetilde{k}_{1}}(s)]A_{\widetilde{k}_{2}}(s)(2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)}\varepsilon(s))(2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)}\varepsilon(s))\end{split}

(5.26)

for
s\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
. This is also consistent with the desired estimates (
5.20
).

After these reductions, for (
5.20
) it remains to prove that

\begin{split}&\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,j},P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,j},P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\\
&+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,j},P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\iota}}_{\geq 2,j})(s)\big|\\
&\qquad\qquad\lesssim[\varepsilon(s)]^{3}A_{l}^{2}(s)2^{[\min(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)-\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)]/10}\end{split}

(5.27)

for any
s\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
,
j\in\{1,2\}
, and
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in Z_{l}^{4}
.
For this we need to use symmetrization to avoid losing derivatives;
we will also need the
L^{\infty}
-type bounds

\|V(s)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-1}}+\|\beta(s)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon(s),

(5.28)

which follow from Lemma
3.2
.

Step 4.
We prove now the bounds (
5.27
) for
j=1
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
.
In view of (
5.28
) and (
5.17
), for any
s\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
and
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

\begin{split}&\big\|P_{n}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\pm}}_{\geq 2,1}(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{n/2-N_{0}\max(n,0)}A_{n}(s)\varepsilon(s),\\
&\big\|P_{n}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\pm}}_{\geq 2,1}(s)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{n/2-N_{1}\max(n,0)}[\varepsilon(s)]^{2}.\end{split}

(5.29)

In particular, using also (
5.23
) and Lemma
2.1
(ii),

\begin{split}&\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,1},P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|+\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,1},P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|\\
&+\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,1},P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|+\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\iota}}_{\geq 2,1})(s)\big|\\
&\lesssim A_{\widetilde{k}_{1}}(s)A_{\widetilde{k}_{2}}(s)[\varepsilon(s)]^{3}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)}2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}\begin{cases}2^{\widetilde{k}_{1}/2}&\text{ if }\iota=-\text{ and }k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-5,\\
2^{\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}&\text{ if }\iota=+\text{ or }k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-6.\end{cases}\end{split}

This suffices to prove the desired bounds (
5.27
) if
\iota=+
or if
