\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq l-20
or if
l\leq 20
.

Recalling the assumption
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
, it remains to prove (
5.27
)
in the case when
\iota=-
,
k_{1},k_{4}\in[l-6,l]
,
l\geq 20
, and
k_{2},k_{3}\leq l-20
.
Using (
5.29
), (
5.23
), and Lemma
2.1
(ii) we have

\begin{split}\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,1},P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{-})(s)\big|+\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,1},P_{k_{4}}U^{-})(s)\big|\\
\lesssim A_{\widetilde{k}_{1}}(s)A_{\widetilde{k}_{2}}(s)[\varepsilon(s)]^{3}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)}2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{2\widetilde{k}_{3}},\end{split}

which is better than what we need. We combine the remaining two terms
and recall that
\overline{T_{f}g}=T_{\overline{f}}\overline{g}
. After changes of variables we can therefore rewrite

\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{M}&(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,1},P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{-})+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{-}}_{\geq 2,1})\\
&=\frac{-i}{(2\pi R)^{4}}\sum_{(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{4}_{R}}N_{1}(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\widehat{\beta}(\eta)\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{U}(\xi_{2})\widehat{U}(\xi_{3})\widehat{\overline{U}}(\xi_{4}),\end{split}

(5.30)

where

\begin{split}&N_{1}(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\chi(\eta,\xi_{1})|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}M^{\prime}(\xi_{1}+\eta,\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4})\\
&\qquad-\chi(\eta,\xi_{4})|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}M^{\prime}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4}+\eta).\end{split}

(5.31)

To complete the proof of (
5.27
) when
j=1
it suffices to show that

\|(1+\eta^{2})^{-3/8}N_{1}(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{4}_{R})}\lesssim 2^{2N_{0}l}2^{k_{3}^{-}/10}2^{2.5k_{2}^{+}}.

(5.32)

To prove (
5.32
) we may replace
\chi(\eta,\xi_{1})
and
\chi(\eta,\xi_{4})
with
\varphi_{\leq l-30}(\eta)
,
at the expense of harmless multipliers that do not lose derivatives. Moreover, we may assume that, for some
\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}
,

\begin{split}&M(\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\rho_{3},\rho_{4})\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\rho)=|\rho_{2}+\rho_{3}|^{\beta}F_{\beta}(\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\rho_{3},\rho_{4})\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\rho),\\
&\big|2^{\alpha\cdot\underline{k}}\partial^{\alpha}_{\rho}\big[F_{\beta}(\rho)\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\rho)\big]\big|\lesssim 2^{k_{3}/2}2^{(2-\beta)k_{2}}2^{2N_{0}l},\qquad\text{ for }|\alpha|\leq 6\text{ and }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}.\end{split}

(5.33)

Letting
F^{\prime}_{\beta}=F_{\beta}/\Phi_{+++-}
, we write the remaining symbol as

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\leq l-30}(\eta)|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}|^{\beta}\big[|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}F^{\prime}_{\beta}(\xi_{1}+\eta,\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4})\\
&\qquad-|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}F^{\prime}_{\beta}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4}+\eta)\big].\end{split}

(5.34)

It follows from (
5.25
) and (
5.33
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|2^{\alpha\cdot\underline{k}}\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}\big[F^{\prime}_{\beta}(\xi)\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi)\big]\big|\lesssim 2^{k_{3}/2}2^{(3/2-\beta)k_{2}}2^{2N_{0}l}\qquad\text{ for }|\alpha|\leq 6\text{ and }\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}.\end{split}

(5.35)

We then split (
5.34
) as the sum of three symbols

\varphi_{\leq l-30}(\eta)|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}|^{\beta}\big(N_{1}^{1}+N_{1}^{2}\big)(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})

where

\begin{split}N_{1}^{1}(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})&:=\big[|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}+\eta|^{1/2}\big]\\
&\times F^{\prime}_{\beta}(\xi_{1}+\eta,\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4}),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}N_{1}^{2}&(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\\
&\times\big[-F^{\prime}_{\beta}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4}+\eta)+F^{\prime}_{\beta}(\xi_{1}+\eta,\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4})\big].\end{split}

Finally, we multiply the two symbols above by
(1+\eta^{2})^{-3/8}
and use (
5.35
)
and Lemma
2.2
(i) and (iv) to prove the bounds (
5.32
).

Step 5.

For the last step we need to prove the bounds (
5.27
) for
j=2
.
In view of (
5.28
) and (
5.17
), for any
s\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
and
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

\begin{split}&\big\|P_{n}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\pm}}_{\geq 2,2}(s)\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 2^{n-N_{0}\max(n,0)}A_{n}(s)\varepsilon(s),\\
&\big\|P_{n}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\pm}}_{\geq 2,2}(s)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{n-N_{1}\max(n,0)}[\varepsilon(s)]^{2}.\end{split}

(5.36)

As in the case
j=1
, this suffices to prove the desired bounds (
5.27
) if
\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq l-20
or if
l\leq 20
. In the remaining case when
\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq l-20
and
l\geq 20
, we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
. There are two cases, depending on whether
k_{4}
is one of the two larger indices, or not. The cases are identical, so assume that
k_{4}\geq l-6
, so
k_{2},k_{3}\leq l-20
. We can still estimate

\begin{split}\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,2},P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|+\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,2},P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\big|\\
\lesssim A_{\widetilde{k}_{1}}(s)A_{\widetilde{k}_{2}}(s)[\varepsilon(s)]^{3}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)}2^{-N_{1}\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)}2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{2.5\widetilde{k}_{3}},\end{split}

using (
5.36
), (
5.23
), and Lemma
2.1
(ii). We combine the remaining two terms and rewrite

\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{M}&(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,2},P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{\iota}}_{\geq 2,2})\\
&=\frac{-i}{(2\pi R)^{4}}\sum_{(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{4}_{R}}N_{2}(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\widehat{V}(\eta)\widehat{U}(\xi_{1})\widehat{U}(\xi_{2})\widehat{U}(\xi_{3})\widehat{U^{\iota}}(\xi_{4}),\end{split}

(5.37)

using the fact that
V
is real-valued, where

\begin{split}N_{2}(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},&\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\chi(\eta,\xi_{1})(\xi_{1}+\eta)M^{\prime}(\xi_{1}+\eta,\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4})\\
&+\chi(\eta,\xi_{4})(\xi_{4}+\eta)M^{\prime}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}+\eta)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4}+\eta).\end{split}

As in the case
j=1
above, one can show that

\|(1+\eta^{2})^{-3/4}N_{2}(\eta,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{4}_{R})}\lesssim 2^{2N_{0}l}2^{k_{3}^{-}/10}2^{2.5k_{2}^{+}},

(5.38)

which suffices to prove the desired bound (
5.27
) and concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

5.3.
Resonant cases

In the next lemma we consider the more subtle case of resonant quartic interactions
which involve two
U
and two
\overline{U}
functions and the resonant phase

\Phi_{++--}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}.

(5.39)

Since we will need to distinguish cases depending on the signs of the variables, we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{k}^{+}(x):=\varphi_{k}(x)\mathbf{1}_{+}(x),\qquad\varphi_{k}^{-}(x):=\varphi_{k}(x)\mathbf{1}_{-}(x),\\
&\varphi_{k}^{{}^{\prime}+}(x):=\varphi^{\prime}_{k}(x)\mathbf{1}_{+}(x),\qquad\varphi_{k}^{{}^{\prime}-}(x):=\varphi^{\prime}_{k}(x)\mathbf{1}_{-}(x),\\
&\varphi^{\underline{\iota}}_{\underline{k}}(\xi):=\varphi^{\iota_{1}}_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi^{\iota_{2}}_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi^{\iota_{3}}_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi^{\iota_{4}}_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4}),\qquad\widetilde{\varphi}^{\underline{\iota}}_{\underline{k}}(\xi):=\varphi^{{}^{\prime}\iota_{1}}_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi^{{}^{\prime}\iota_{2}}_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi^{{}^{\prime}\iota_{3}}_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi^{{}^{\prime}\iota_{4}}_{k_{4}}(\xi_{4}),\end{split}

(5.40)

where
\underline{\iota}:=(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3},\iota_{4})\in\{+,-\}^{4}
and
\underline{k}:=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
, and the functions
\varphi^{\prime}_{k}
are defined as in (
2.3
).

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume that
M
is defined according to the symmetrization formula

\begin{split}M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):&=m(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+m(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})+m(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+m(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})\\
&-m(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})-m(\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{1})-m(\xi_{4},\xi_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})-m(\xi_{4},\xi_{3},\xi_{2},\xi_{1})\end{split}

(5.41)

for any
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}_{\infty}^{3}
, where
m\in\mathcal{A}
is an admissible symbol as in Definition
5.1
. Assume in addition that
M
vanishes on the set of Benjamin-Feir resonances, i.e.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=0\qquad\text{ provided that }\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}_{\infty}^{3}\text{ satisfies}\\
&\xi_{1}\in(-\infty,0),\,\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\in(0,\infty)\text{ and }|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}=|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}.\end{split}

(NR-BF)

Then, with
\mathcal{A}_{M}
defined as in (
5.14
) and for any
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
we have

\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{A}_{M}(U,U,\overline{U},\overline{U})(s)\,ds\Big|\lesssim[A(t_{1})\varepsilon(t_{1})]^{2}+[A(t_{2})\varepsilon(t_{2})]^{2}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}A^{2}[\varepsilon(s)]^{3}\,ds.

(5.42)

Proof.

Step 1.
It follows from (
5.41
) that
M
satisfies the symmetry assumptions

M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=M(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})=M(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{3}).

(5.43)

Let
M^{\prime}:=M/\Phi_{++--}
. We will show below that for any
\underline{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}
and
\underline{\iota}\in\{+,-\}^{4}

\big\|M^{\prime}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}\big\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}.

(5.44)

Moreover, if
k_{1}\geq k_{2}+12
and
k_{3}\geq k_{4}+12
then we also show that
M^{\prime}
admits an extension
M^{\prime\prime}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
,
M^{\prime}(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)=M^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)
for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
, such that

\big\|2^{k_{1}}\partial_{\xi_{1}}[M^{\prime\prime}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}]\big\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}+\big\|2^{k_{3}}\partial_{\xi_{3}}[M^{\prime\prime}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}]\big\|_{\widetilde{S}^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}.

(5.45)

Assuming (
5.44
)–(
5.45
), the desired bounds (
5.42
) follow as in the proof of Lemma
5.2
. Indeed, as in (
5.20
), it suffices to show that for any
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
we have

\begin{split}&2^{[\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)-\min(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)]/10}\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{A}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}\overline{U},P_{k_{4}}\overline{U})(s)\,ds\Big|\\
&\lesssim A_{l}^{2}(t_{1})[\varepsilon(t_{1})]^{2}+A^{2}_{l}(t_{2})[\varepsilon(t_{2})]^{2}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}A_{l}(s)[\varepsilon(s)]^{2}[\varepsilon(s)A_{l}(s)+B_{l}(s)]\,ds,\end{split}

(5.46)

for any
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in Z_{l}^{4}=\{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}:\,l=\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\}
,
where
A_{k}
and
B_{k}
are defined as in (
5.18
). To prove this we define the operators

\mathcal{T}_{M}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}):=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{3}}\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{R}}\frac{M(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}{\Phi_{++--}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}\widehat{f_{1}}(\xi_{1})\widehat{f_{2}}(\xi_{2})\widehat{f_{3}}(\xi_{3})\widehat{f_{4}}(\xi_{4}),

(5.47)

use a normal forms identity similar to (
5.22
),
and apply arguments similar to those in the proof of Lemma
5.2
.
In particular, it suffices to show that

\begin{split}&\big|\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,j},P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,j},P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)\\
&+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{-}}_{\geq 2,j},P_{k_{4}}U^{\iota})(s)+\mathcal{T}_{M}(P_{k_{1}}U,P_{k_{2}}U,P_{k_{3}}U,P_{k_{4}}\mathcal{N}^{U^{-}}_{\geq 2,j})(s)\big|\\
&\qquad\qquad\lesssim[\varepsilon(s)]^{2}A_{l}(s)[\varepsilon(s)A_{l}(s)+B_{l}(s)]2^{[\min(\widetilde{k}_{4},0)-\max(\widetilde{k}_{3},0)]/10}\end{split}

(5.48)

for any
(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in Z_{l}^{4}
and
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, where the nonlinearities
\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,j}=\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,j}
and
\mathcal{N}^{U^{-}}_{\geq 2,j}=\overline{\mathcal{N}^{U^{+}}_{\geq 2,j}}
are defined as in (
5.16
).

The estimates (
5.48
) follows for
j=3
using the bounds (
5.17
) and (
5.44
). They also follow for
j=1
or
j=2
if
2^{\widetilde{k}_{3}}\approx 2^{l}
, using (
5.29
) and (
5.36
), since there is no derivative loss in this case. In the remaining case we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}
and
k_{3}\geq k_{4}
(due to (
5.43
)) and use a symmetrization argument as in the last two steps of the previous lemma. The desired bounds (
5.48
) follow in these cases as well, using the assumptions (
5.45
).

Step 2.
We prove now the bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
) in the case when
\underline{\iota}=(\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3},\iota_{4})
contains 3 signs
+
and 1 sign
-
or 3 signs
-
and 1 sign
+
. Indeed, in view of (
5.43
) we may assume that

\underline{\iota}=(-,+,+,+).

(5.49)

By symmetry we may also assume that
k_{3}\geq k_{4}
.

Notice that
|\xi_{i}+\xi_{j}|\gtrsim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{3}}
for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of the function
\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}
and any
i\neq j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
. In particular, since
m
is admissible, we may assume that
m_{\sigma}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}
does not contain any of the singular factors
|\xi_{i}+\xi_{j}|^{\beta}
in the definition (
5.12
), with
\beta\neq 0
, for any permutation
\sigma
of the set
\{1,2,3,4\}
, where
m_{\sigma}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=m(\xi_{\sigma(1)},\xi_{\sigma(2)},\xi_{\sigma(3)},\xi_{\sigma(4)})
. This is because such factors could simply be absorbed into the associated smooth symbols. As a consequence,
M
admits an extension
M^{\ast}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
,
M(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)=M^{\ast}(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)
for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
, such that

\big|2^{\alpha\cdot\underline{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}[M^{\ast}(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)]\big|\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{2\widetilde{k}_{3}}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}

(5.50)

for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}_{\infty}^{3}
and
|\alpha|\leq 6
.

We notice that if
\xi_{1}\in(-\infty,0],\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\in[0,\infty)
and
\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}=0
then we can write

\begin{split}&\Phi_{++--}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}\\
&=\frac{2\sqrt{\xi_{2}}(\sqrt{\xi_{3}}+\sqrt{\xi_{4}})-2\sqrt{\xi_{3}}\sqrt{\xi_{4}}}{|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}}\\
&=\frac{2\xi_{2}(\sqrt{\xi_{3}}+\sqrt{\xi_{4}})^{2}-2\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}{(|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2})(\sqrt{\xi_{2}}\sqrt{\xi_{3}}+\sqrt{\xi_{2}}\sqrt{\xi_{4}}+\sqrt{\xi_{3}}\sqrt{\xi_{4}})}.\end{split}

(5.51)

In particular,
\Phi_{++--}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=0
if and only if

\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\in[0,\infty),\qquad\xi_{2}=\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}{(\sqrt{\xi}_{3}+\sqrt{\xi}_{4})^{2}},\qquad\xi_{1}=-\frac{(\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}+\sqrt{\xi}_{3}\sqrt{\xi}_{4})^{2}}{(\sqrt{\xi}_{3}+\sqrt{\xi}_{4})^{2}}.

(5.52)

More importantly, we can define the extension
M^{\prime\prime}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}

M^{\prime\prime}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\frac{M^{\ast}(-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}{\Phi_{++--}(-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}

(5.53)

such that
(M/\Phi_{++--})(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)=M^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)
for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
, where
M^{\ast}
satisfies (
5.50
).

We show now that

\big|2^{\alpha\cdot\underline{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}[M^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)]\big|\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}

(5.54)

for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}_{\infty}^{3}
and
|\alpha|\leq 5
. Indeed, since
k_{3}\geq k_{4}
we may assume that
2^{k_{1}}\approx 2^{k_{3}}\approx 2^{\widetilde{k}_{1}}\approx 2^{\widetilde{k}_{2}}
. If
|k_{2}-k_{4}|\geq 6
then the phase

\Phi_{++--}
is elliptic in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}
, due to (
5.51
), and the bounds (
5.54
) follow directly from (
5.50
). On the other hand, if
|k_{2}-k_{4}|\leq 6
then
2^{k_{2}}\approx 2^{k_{4}}\approx 2^{\widetilde{k}_{3}}\approx 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}}
. The main assumption (
NR-BF
) shows that

M^{\ast}(-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=0\qquad\text{ if }\qquad\xi_{2}=\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}{(\sqrt{\xi}_{3}+\sqrt{\xi}_{4})^{2}}.

Therefore we can write, with
\xi^{\ast}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}{(\sqrt{\xi_{3}}+\sqrt{\xi_{4}})^{2}}

\begin{split}(M^{\ast}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})&(-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=\int_{\xi^{\ast}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}^{\xi_{2}}\frac{d}{ds}\big[(M^{\ast}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(-s-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},s,\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\big]\,ds\\
&=\int_{\xi^{\ast}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}^{\xi_{2}}(-\partial_{1}+\partial_{2})(M^{\ast}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(-s-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},s,\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\,ds\\
&=(\xi_{2}-\xi^{\ast}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4}))M^{\ast\ast}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}),\end{split}

(5.55)

where

\begin{split}M^{\ast\ast}&(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=\int_{0}^{1}(-\partial_{1}+\partial_{2})(M^{\ast}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})\\
&(-\xi^{\ast}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})-\alpha(\xi_{2}-\xi^{\ast}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4}))-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},\xi^{\ast}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+\alpha(\xi_{2}-\xi^{\ast}(\xi_{3},\xi_{4})),\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\,d\alpha.\end{split}

(5.56)

One can check that the symbol
M^{**}
satisfies the differential bounds

\sup_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{|\alpha|\leq 5}\big|2^{\alpha_{2}k_{2}+\alpha_{3}k_{3}+\alpha_{4}k_{4}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}[M^{\ast\ast}(\xi)]\big|\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{\widetilde{k}_{3}}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)},

(5.57)

in view of the differential bounds on
M^{*}
in (
5.50
), using the condition that

2^{k_{2}}\approx 2^{k_{4}}\approx 2^{\widetilde{k}_{3}}\approx 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}}
.
Finally, thanks to the identity (
5.55
) we can explicitly cancel the vanishing factor

\displaystyle(M^{\prime\prime}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=\Big(\xi_{2}-\frac{\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}{(\sqrt{\xi}_{3}+\sqrt{\xi}_{4})^{2}}\Big)\frac{M^{\ast\ast}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}{\Phi_{++--}(-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}

\displaystyle=\frac{M^{\ast\ast}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}{2(\sqrt{\xi}_{3}+\sqrt{\xi}_{4})^{2}}\frac{2\xi_{2}(\sqrt{\xi}_{3}+\sqrt{\xi}_{4})^{2}-2\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}{\Phi_{++--}(-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}-\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}

\displaystyle=\frac{M^{\ast\ast}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}{2(\sqrt{\xi}_{3}+\sqrt{\xi}_{4})^{2}}(|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2})(\sqrt{\xi_{2}}\sqrt{\xi_{3}}+\sqrt{\xi_{2}}\sqrt{\xi_{4}}+\sqrt{\xi_{3}}\sqrt{\xi_{4}}),

having used (5.51) for the last identity. The claimed bound (
5.54
) follow from (
5.57
). The main bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
) follow from (
5.54
) and Lemma
2.2
(iv).

We use this basic strategy several times in the proof this lemma: derivative bounds on symbols and vanishing on suitable sets lead to favorable decompositions like (
5.55
), and suffice to perform the division by singular phases with no loss of derivative.

Step 3.
We prove now the bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
) in the case when

\underline{\iota}=(+,+,-,-).

(5.58)

By symmetry we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}
and
k_{3}\geq k_{4}
; in particular
2^{k_{1}}\approx 2^{k_{3}}\approx 2^{\widetilde{k}_{1}}\approx 2^{\widetilde{k}_{2}}
.

Notice that if
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in[0,\infty),\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\in(-\infty,0]
and
\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}=0
then we can write

\begin{split}\Phi_{++--}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})&=\frac{2\sqrt{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}-2\sqrt{\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}}{|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}}\\
&=\frac{2(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}\xi_{4})}{(\sqrt{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}+\sqrt{\xi_{3}\xi_{4}})(|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2})}\\
&=\frac{-2(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})(\xi_{2}+\xi_{4})}{(\sqrt{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}+\sqrt{\xi_{3}\xi_{4}})(|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2})}.\end{split}

(5.59)

Notice also that if
|k_{2}-k_{4}|\geq 4
then
|\xi_{i}+\xi_{j}|
is elliptically bounded away from
0
for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}
, and the desired bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
) follow easily. It remains to prove these bounds in the main case

k_{1},k_{3}\in[l-4,l],\qquad k_{2},k_{4}\in[n-4,n],\qquad\text{ for some }n\leq l\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

(5.60)

Substep 3.1.
We show first that we can write

(M\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\xi)=\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}\big[|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}|^{\beta}F_{\beta}^{23}(\xi)+|\xi_{2}+\xi_{4}|^{\beta}F_{\beta}^{24}(\xi)\big]

(5.61)

for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
, for some functions
F_{\beta}^{23},F_{\beta}^{24}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying

\begin{split}&(F_{\beta}^{j}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\rho,\sigma,-\rho,-\sigma)=(F_{\beta}^{j}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\rho,\sigma,-\sigma,-\rho)=0,\\
&\big|2^{\alpha\cdot\underline{k}}\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}\big[F^{j}_{\beta}(\xi)\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)\big]\big|\lesssim 2^{(5/2-\beta)n}2^{2N_{0}\max(l,0)},\end{split}

(5.62)

for any
j\in\{23,24\}
,
\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}
,
\rho,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
, multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})
with
|\alpha|\leq 6
, and
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
. Moreover, the functions
F_{\beta}^{23}
are identically
0
if
l\geq n+20
.

For this we use the main formula (
5.41
) and Definition
5.1
. We need to be careful however because the vanishing in the first line of (
5.62
) depends on the symmetrization formula (
5.41
), while the multipliers in the right-hand side of (
5.12
) are allowed to depend on
\underline{k}
.

Assume first that
l-n\geq 20
. Since
m
is admissible, it follows from Definition
5.1
and (
5.60
) that we can write

\begin{split}&m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}|\rho_{2}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}H_{\beta}^{1}(\rho)\,\,\text{ for any }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{+,+,-,-}_{l,n,l,n},\\
&\text{ where }B^{\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\iota_{3},\iota_{4}}_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4}}:=\big\{\rho\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:\,\iota_{j}\rho_{j}\in[2^{l_{j}-6},2^{l_{j}+2}],\,j\in\{1,2,3,4\}\big\},\end{split}

(5.63)

for some function
H_{\beta}^{1}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying, for
\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}
,

\big|\rho^{\alpha}\cdot(\partial^{\alpha}_{\rho}H^{1}_{\beta})(\rho)\big|\lesssim 2^{(5/2-\beta)n}2^{2N_{0}\max(l,0)},\qquad\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{+,+,-,-}_{l,n,l,n},\,|\alpha|\leq 6.

(5.64)

This representation follows from the main formula (
5.12
), by combining non-singular factors
|\rho_{i}+\rho_{j}|
with the corresponding symbols and summing over
k_{1},k_{3}\in[l-8,l+4]
and
k_{2},k_{4}\in[n-8,n+4]
.

Similarly, we can also write

\begin{split}&m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}|\rho_{2}+\rho_{3}|^{\beta}H_{\beta}^{2}(\rho)\,\,\text{ for any }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{+,+,-,-}_{l,n,n,l},\\
&m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}|\rho_{1}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}H_{\beta}^{3}(\rho)\,\,\text{ for any }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{+,+,-,-}_{n,l,l,n},\\
&m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}|\rho_{1}+\rho_{3}|^{\beta}H_{\beta}^{4}(\rho)\,\,\text{ for any }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{+,+,-,-}_{n,l,n,l},\\
\end{split}

(5.65)

for some functions
H_{\beta}^{j}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
,
j\in\{2,3,4\}
, satisfying differential inequalities similar to (
5.63
).

Then we define, for
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in B^{+,+,-,-}_{l,n,l,n}
,

\begin{split}F_{\beta}^{24}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=H_{\beta}^{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},&\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+H_{\beta}^{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})+H_{\beta}^{3}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+H_{\beta}^{4}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})\\
&-H_{\beta}^{1}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-H_{\beta}^{2}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1})\\
&-H_{\beta}^{3}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-H_{\beta}^{4}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1}).\end{split}

(5.66)

The identities (
5.61
) (with
F_{\beta}^{23}\equiv 0
) follow from the formulas (
5.41
), (
5.63
), and (
5.65
) (recall also that
m
is even). The differential inequalities in the second line of (
5.62
) follow from the differential inequalities (
5.64
) satisfied by the functions
H_{\beta}^{j}
. The identity
(F_{\beta}^{j}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\rho,\sigma,-\sigma,-\rho)=0
holds because
\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\rho,\sigma,-\sigma,-\rho)=0
in our case. The identity
(F_{\beta}^{j}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\rho,\sigma,-\rho,-\sigma)=0
holds because
F_{\beta}^{j}(\rho,\sigma,-\rho,-\sigma)=0
if
\rho\in[2^{l-6},2^{l+2}],\sigma\in[2^{n-6},2^{n+2}]
, due to the definition (
5.66
). The point is that even though the functions
H_{\beta}^{1},H_{\beta}^{2},H_{\beta}^{3},H_{\beta}^{4}
are not related, this crucial cancellation is already present for each of the four pairs of terms involving each of these functions. This completes the proof of the representation (
5.62
) in the case
l-n\geq 20
.

Assume now that
l-n\leq 19
. Using again Definition
5.1
and (
5.60
) we can write

m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}[|\rho_{2}+\rho_{3}|^{\beta}G_{\beta}^{3}(\rho)+|\rho_{2}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}G_{\beta}^{4}(\rho)]

(5.67)

for any
\rho=(\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\rho_{3},\rho_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
satisfying
\rho_{1},\rho_{2},-\rho_{3},-\rho_{4}\in[2^{l-30},2^{l+2}]
. The functions
G_{\beta}^{3},G_{\beta}^{4}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
satisfy the differential inequalities

\big|\rho^{\alpha}\cdot(\partial^{\alpha}_{\rho}G^{j}_{\beta})(\rho)\big|\lesssim 2^{(5/2-\beta)l}2^{2N_{0}\max(l,0)},

(5.68)

for
\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}
,
j\in\{3,4\}
,
|\alpha|\leq 6
, and
\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
satisfying
\rho_{1},\rho_{2},-\rho_{3},-\rho_{4}\in[2^{l-30},2^{l+2}]
.
This representation follows from the main formula (
5.12
), by summing over
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in[l-32,l+4]
.

Then we define, for
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})
, with
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4}\in[2^{l-30},2^{l+2}]
,

\begin{split}F_{\beta}^{23}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=G_{\beta}^{3}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},&\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+G_{\beta}^{4}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})+G_{\beta}^{4}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+G_{\beta}^{3}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})\\
&-G_{\beta}^{3}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-G_{\beta}^{4}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1})\\
&-G_{\beta}^{4}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-G_{\beta}^{3}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1}),\end{split}

(5.69)

\begin{split}F_{\beta}^{24}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=G_{\beta}^{4}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},&\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+G_{\beta}^{3}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})+G_{\beta}^{3}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+G_{\beta}^{4}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})\\
&-G_{\beta}^{4}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-G_{\beta}^{3}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1})\\
&-G_{\beta}^{3}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-G_{\beta}^{4}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1}).\end{split}

(5.70)

The identities (
5.61
) follow from the formulas (
5.41
) and (
5.69
)–(
5.70
) (recall also that
m
is even). The differential inequalities in the second line of (
5.62
) follow from the differential inequalities (
5.68
) satisfied by the functions
G_{\beta}^{j}
. The key cancellations in the first line of (
5.62
) follow from the formulas (
5.69
)–(
5.70
), by canceling suitable pairs of terms.

Substep 3.2.
We can now prove the bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
). Notice that if
f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to{\mathbb{C}}
is a
C^{2}
function satisfying
f(x,u,0)=f(x,0,v)=0
for any
x,u,v\in\mathbb{R}
then

\begin{split}f(x,u,v)&=uv\cdot g(x,u,v),\\
g(x,u,v)&:=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}(\partial^{2}_{uv}f)(x,\mu u,\nu v)\,d\mu d\nu,\end{split}

(5.71)

for any
x,a,b\in\mathbb{R}
. We apply this identity to the functions

f(x,u,v):=(F_{\beta}^{j}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(x+u+v,x,-x-u,-x-v),

(5.72)

for
\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}
and
j\in\{23,24\}
. The formula (
5.61
) shows that

\begin{split}(M\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})&(\xi)=\widetilde{\varphi}_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)(\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})(\xi_{2}+\xi_{4})\\
&\times\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}\big[|\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}|^{\beta}\widetilde{F}_{\beta}^{23}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+|\xi_{2}+\xi_{4}|^{\beta}\widetilde{F}_{\beta}^{24}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\big],\end{split}

(5.73)

where
\xi\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
and
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)
is defined as in (
5.40
). In view of (
5.62
) and (
5.71
)
\widetilde{F}_{\beta}^{23}\equiv 0
if
l\geq n+20
, and the functions
\widetilde{F}_{\beta}^{23},\widetilde{F}_{\beta}^{24}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to{\mathbb{C}}
satisfy the differential inequalities

\big|2^{\alpha_{2}k_{2}+\alpha_{3}k_{3}+\alpha_{4}k_{4}}\partial_{\xi_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi_{3}}^{\alpha_{3}}\partial_{\xi_{4}}^{\alpha_{4}}\widetilde{F}^{j}_{\beta}(\xi)\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)\big]\big|\lesssim 2^{(3/2-\beta)n}2^{-l}2^{2N_{0}\max(l,0)},

(5.74)

if
j\in\{23,24\}
,
\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\leq 4
, and
\xi=(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

The bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
) follow from (
5.73
)–(
5.74
) and (
5.59
). The point is that the singular factors
\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}
and
\xi_{2}+\xi_{4}
cancel, and the desired bounds follow using Lemma
2.2
(iv).

Step 4.
Finally we prove the bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
) in the case when

\underline{\iota}=(+,-,-,+).

(5.75)

Notice that if
\xi_{1},\xi_{4}\in[0,\infty),\xi_{2},\xi_{3}\in(-\infty,0]
and
\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}=0
then we can write

\begin{split}\Phi_{++--}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})&=\frac{\xi_{1}+\xi_{3}}{|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}}-\frac{\xi_{2}+\xi_{4}}{|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}}\\
&=-(\xi_{2}+\xi_{4})\frac{|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}}{(|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2})(|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2})}.\end{split}

(5.76)

As before, we notice that if
|\widetilde{k}_{3}-\widetilde{k}_{4}|\geq 4
then all the factors of the form
|\xi_{i}+\xi_{j}|
are elliptically bounded away from
0
for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}
, and the bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
) follow easily. In view of the symmetries (
5.43
) and recalling that
M
is even, it remains to prove these bounds in the main case

k_{1},k_{3}\in[l-4,l],\qquad k_{2},k_{4}\in[n-4,n],\qquad\text{ for some }n\leq l\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

(5.77)

Substep 4.1.
As before, we show first that we can write

(M\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\xi)=\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}\big[|\xi_{2}+\xi_{4}|^{\beta}G_{\beta}^{24}(\xi)+|\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}|^{\beta}G_{\beta}^{34}(\xi)\big]

(5.78)

for any
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
, for some functions
G_{\beta}^{24},G_{\beta}^{34}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying

\begin{split}&(G_{\beta}^{j}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\rho,\sigma,-\rho,-\sigma)=0,\\
&\big|2^{\alpha\cdot\underline{k}}\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}\big[G^{j}_{\beta}(\xi)\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)\big]\big|\lesssim 2^{(5/2-\beta)n}2^{2N_{0}\max(l,0)},\end{split}

(5.79)

for any
j\in\{24,34\}
,
\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}
,
\rho,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
, multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})
with
|\alpha|\leq 6
, and
\xi\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
. Moreover, the functions
G_{\beta}^{34}
are identically
0
if
l\geq n+20
.

This is similar to the proof of (
5.61
)–(
5.62
), slightly simpler because we only prove one cancellation condition in (
5.79
). As in (
5.63
) and (
5.65
), since
m
is admissible, it follows from Definition
5.1
and (
5.77
) that we can write

\begin{split}&m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}[|\rho_{2}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}X_{\beta}^{1}(\rho)+|\rho_{3}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}Y_{\beta}^{1}(\rho)]\,\,\text{ for any }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{+,-,-,+}_{l,n,l,n},\\
&m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}|\rho_{2}+\rho_{3}|^{\beta}X_{\beta}^{2}(\rho)+|\rho_{3}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}Y_{\beta}^{2}(\rho)]\,\,\text{ for any }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{+,-,+,-}_{l,n,n,l},\\
&m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}|\rho_{1}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}X_{\beta}^{3}(\rho)+|\rho_{3}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}Y_{\beta}^{3}(\rho)]\,\,\text{ for any }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{-,+,-,+}_{n,l,l,n},\\
&m(\rho)=\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}|\rho_{1}+\rho_{3}|^{\beta}X_{\beta}^{4}(\rho)+|\rho_{3}+\rho_{4}|^{\beta}Y_{\beta}^{4}(\rho)]\,\,\text{ for any }\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{-,+,+,-}_{n,l,n,l},\end{split}

(5.80)

for some function
X^{j}_{\beta},\,Y_{\beta}^{j}:\mathbb{R}^{4}\to{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying, for
\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}
,

\big|\rho^{\alpha}\cdot(\partial^{\alpha}_{\rho}X^{j}_{\beta})(\rho)\big|+\big|\rho^{\alpha}\cdot(\partial^{\alpha}_{\rho}Y^{j}_{\beta})(\rho)\big|\lesssim 2^{(5/2-\beta)n}2^{2N_{0}\max(l,0)},\qquad\rho\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}\cap B^{j},\,|\alpha|\leq 6,

(5.81)

where
B^{j}
are the suitable balls as in (
5.80
) where the functions
X^{j}_{\beta},Y^{j}_{\beta}
are defined. These representation follow from the main formula (
5.12
), by summing over indices
k_{1},k_{3}\in[l-8,l+4]
and
k_{2},k_{4}\in[n-8,n+4]
. Moreover, the functions
Y^{j}_{\beta}
are trivial if
|l-n|\geq 6
.

Then we define, for
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in B^{+,-,-,+}_{l,n,l,n}
,

\begin{split}G_{\beta}^{24}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=X_{\beta}^{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},&\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+X_{\beta}^{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})+X_{\beta}^{3}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+X_{\beta}^{4}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})\\
&-X_{\beta}^{1}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-X_{\beta}^{2}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1})\\
&-X_{\beta}^{3}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-X_{\beta}^{4}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1})\end{split}

\begin{split}G_{\beta}^{34}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}):=Y_{\beta}^{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},&\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+Y_{\beta}^{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})+Y_{\beta}^{3}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+Y_{\beta}^{4}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{4},\xi_{3})\\
&-Y_{\beta}^{1}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-Y_{\beta}^{2}(-\xi_{3},-\xi_{4},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1})\\
&-Y_{\beta}^{3}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{1},-\xi_{2})-Y_{\beta}^{4}(-\xi_{4},-\xi_{3},-\xi_{2},-\xi_{1}).\end{split}

The identities (
5.78
) follow from the formulas (
5.41
) and (
5.80
) (recall also that
m
is even). The differential inequalities in the second line of (
5.79
) follow from (
5.81
) and the identities
(G_{\beta}^{j}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(\rho,\sigma,-\rho,-\sigma)=0
follow from the definitions of the functions
G^{24}_{\beta}
and
G^{34}_{\beta}
above.

Substep 4.2.
We can now prove that bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
). As in Substep 3.2 above, we notice that if
f:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to{\mathbb{C}}
is a
C^{2}
function satisfying
f(x,y,0)=0
for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
then

f(x,y,u)=u\cdot\int_{0}^{1}(\partial_{u}f)(x,y,\mu u)\,d\mu.

for any
x,y,u\in\mathbb{R}
. We apply this identity to the functions

f(x,y,u):=(G_{\beta}^{j}\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})(x+u,y,-x,-y-u),

(5.82)

for
\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}
and
j\in\{24,34\}
. The formula (
5.78
) shows that

\begin{split}(M\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}})&(\xi)=\widetilde{\varphi}_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)(\xi_{2}+\xi_{4})\\
&\times\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1,3/2\}}\big[|\xi_{2}+\xi_{4}|^{\beta}\widetilde{G}_{\beta}^{24}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})+|\xi_{3}+\xi_{4}|^{\beta}\widetilde{G}_{\beta}^{34}(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\big],\end{split}

(5.83)

for
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
. In view of (
5.79
)
\widetilde{G}_{\beta}^{34}\equiv 0
if
l\geq n+20
, and the functions
\widetilde{G}_{\beta}^{24},\widetilde{G}_{\beta}^{34}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to{\mathbb{C}}
satisfy the differential inequalities

\big|2^{\alpha_{2}k_{2}+\alpha_{3}k_{3}+\alpha_{4}k_{4}}\partial_{\xi_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi_{3}}^{\alpha_{3}}\partial_{\xi_{4}}^{\alpha_{4}}\widetilde{G}^{j}_{\beta}(\xi)\varphi_{\underline{k}}^{\underline{\iota}}(\xi)\big]\big|\lesssim 2^{(3/2-\beta)n}2^{2N_{0}\max(l,0)},

(5.84)

if
j\in\{24,34\}
,
\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\leq 5
, and
\xi=(\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

As before, the bounds (
5.44
)–(
5.45
) now follow from (
5.83
)–(
5.84
) and (
5.76
) by cancelling the singular factor
\xi_{2}+\xi_{4}
and using Lemma
2.2
(iv). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.
The admissibility condition and proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section we prove that the symbols
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{j}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,9\}
, and
\widetilde{K}_{2}^{j}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
obtained by symmetrization in the first three variables of the symbols in Lemma
4.7
are all admissible. Then we show how to complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
, provided that we can verify the vanishing of the fully symmetrized symbol
SK_{1}-SK_{2}
on the set of Benjamin-Feir resonances.

6.1.
Admissibility

We will prove the following lemma:

Lemma 6.1
.

With
K_{i}^{j}
as in (
4.91
)–(
4.103
), we define the symmetrized symbols
\widetilde{K}_{i}^{j}
according to the formula (
5.3
),

\widetilde{K}_{i}^{j}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=\frac{1}{3}\big[K_{i}^{j}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)+K_{i}^{j}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)+K_{i}^{j}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\big].

(6.1)

Then the symbols
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{j}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,9\}
, and
\widetilde{K}_{2}^{j}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
, are all admissible.

The rest of this subsection is concerned with the proof of this lemma. To prove admissibility, we need to pay attention to three main aspects:
(1) the loss of derivatives, (2) the presence of singular factors of the form
|\xi_{l}+\xi_{k}|
,
l\neq k
, and (3) the identification of a lowest frequency factor. In some cases we are able to prove that the symbols
K_{i}^{j}
are already admissible, without symmetrization, but in some cases the symbols become admissible only after symmetrization.

Recall Definition
4.4
of the regular symbol classes
\mathcal{M}^{b}
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}
let
\iota(x)\in\{-1,1\}
denote its sign,
\iota(x):=x/|x|
. For simplicity of notation, for a given
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
and symbols
m,m^{\prime}:{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}\to{\mathbb{C}}
we will sometimes write

\varphi_{\underline{k}}m\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}m^{\prime}

(6.2)

if
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot(m-m^{\prime})
satisfies the properties (
5.12
)–(
5.13
) in Definition
5.1
.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{1}
.
We examine the formula (
4.91
) and decompose

K_{1}^{1}=K_{1}^{1,1}+K_{1}^{1,2}+K_{1}^{1,3}+K_{1}^{1,4}+K_{1}^{1,5},

\begin{split}&K^{1,1}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\sigma|^{1/2}\big\{(|\xi||\rho+\sigma|+\xi(\rho+\sigma))+(|\xi||\eta+\sigma|+\xi(\eta+\sigma))\\
&\qquad-(|\xi||\sigma|+\xi\sigma)-\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta)\big[|\xi||\rho+\sigma|-|\xi||\sigma|\big]-\chi(\eta+\sigma,\rho)\big[|\xi||\eta+\sigma|-|\xi||\sigma|\big]\big\},\\
&K^{1,2}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\sigma|^{1/2}\big\{\chi(\sigma,\rho)\widetilde{\chi}(\rho+\sigma,\eta)(|\xi||\rho+\sigma|+\xi(\rho+\sigma))\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\chi(\sigma,\eta)\widetilde{\chi}(\eta+\sigma,\rho)(|\xi||\eta+\sigma|+\xi(\eta+\sigma))\big\},\\
&K^{1,3}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)\xi|\sigma|^{1/2}\big\{\chi(\sigma,\rho)\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta)(\sigma+\rho)+\chi(\sigma,\eta)\chi(\eta+\sigma,\rho)(\sigma+\eta)\big\},\\
&K^{1,4}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)}{2|\sigma|^{1/2}}[|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)+|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)]\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma,\eta+\rho)(|\xi||\sigma|+\xi\sigma),\\
&K^{1,5}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)}{2|\sigma|^{1/2}}[|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)+|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)]\chi(\sigma,\eta+\rho)\xi\sigma.\end{split}

(6.3)

It is easy to see that the symbols
K^{1,2}_{1}
and
K^{1,4}_{1}
are already admissible, more precisely

K^{1,2}_{1},\,K^{1,4}_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{5/2}.

(6.4)

The symbols
K_{1}^{1,1}
,
K_{1}^{1,3}
, and
K_{1}^{1,5}
require symmetrization, to avoid derivative loss. We start with the easier symbol
K^{1,5}_{1}
. If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is a balanced
4
-tuple (meaning
\widetilde{k}_{1}-\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq 9
as in the proof of Lemma
4.6
) then there is no derivative loss and it is easy to see that the symbol
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{1,5}_{1}
itself satisfies the regular bounds

\|K^{1,5}_{1}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{8}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{2\widetilde{k}_{3}}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}.

(6.5)

By permuting the variables we see that similar bounds hold on the symmetrized symbol
\widetilde{K}^{1,5}_{1}
.

On the other hand, if
\underline{k}
is an imbalanced
4
-tuple (meaning
\widetilde{k}_{1}-\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq 10
) then we may assume by symmetry that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
. Clearly
\widetilde{K}^{1,5}_{1}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}\equiv 0
on
{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
if
k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-5
. Otherwise, if
k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-5
then we may assume that
k_{1},k_{2}
are large and
k_{3},k_{4}
are small,

k_{1},k_{2}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}],\qquad k_{3},k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10.

(6.6)

In this case we replace
\xi
by
-\eta-\rho-\sigma
and use the identity
\chi(\sigma,-\sigma-x)=\chi(\sigma,x)
to write

\begin{split}\widetilde{K}^{1,5}_{1}&(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=\frac{\iota(\sigma)|\sigma|^{1/2}}{6}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta+\rho+\sigma)(\rho+\sigma)|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)\chi(\sigma,\eta+\rho)\\
&-\frac{\iota(\sigma)|\sigma|^{1/2}}{6}\eta\big[-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta+\rho+\sigma)|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)\chi(\sigma,\eta+\rho)+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta+\sigma)\chi(\sigma,\eta)\big],\end{split}

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of the function
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
. The point is that the symmetrization in
\xi
and
\eta
avoids the derivative loss, and the desired bounds

\|\widetilde{K}^{1,5}_{1}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{8}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{2\widetilde{k}_{3}}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}

(6.7)

follow in this case as well.

The analysis of the symbol
\widetilde{K}^{1,3}_{1}
is similar. If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is a balanced
4
-tuple then there is no derivative loss and the symbol
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{1,3}_{1}
itself satisfies regular bounds similar to (
6.5
). On the other hand, if
\underline{k}
is imbalanced then we may assume, by symmetry, the frequency configuration (
6.6
), and symmetrize in
\xi
and
\eta
as before, by substituting
\xi=-\eta-\rho-\sigma
.

The analysis of the symbol
\widetilde{K}^{1,1}_{1}
is also similar, but slightly longer. We rewrite first

\begin{split}K^{1,1}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\sigma|^{1/2}|\xi|\big\{(|\rho+\sigma|+|\eta+\sigma|-|\xi|-|\sigma|)\\
&-\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta)\big[|\rho+\sigma|-|\sigma|\big]-\chi(\eta+\sigma,\rho)\big[|\eta+\sigma|-|\sigma|\big]\big\},\end{split}

(6.8)

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
. If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is a balanced
4
-tuple then there is no derivative loss and no singular factors, and the symbol
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{1,3}_{1}
satisfies regular bounds similar to (
6.5
).

If
\underline{k}
is an imbalanced
4
-tuple (meaning
\widetilde{k}_{1}-\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq 10
) then we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
, and consider two cases. If
k_{4}
is one of the larger frequency parameters,
k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-5
then

k_{1},k_{4}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}],\qquad k_{2},k_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10.

(6.9)

and we use the identity (
6.8
) to calculate, for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
,

\begin{split}\widetilde{K}^{1,1}_{1}(\xi,\eta,&\rho,\sigma)=\frac{1}{3}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)|\sigma|^{1/2}|\eta|\big\{(|\eta+\rho|+|\rho+\sigma|-|\eta|-|\sigma|)-\chi(-\eta-\rho,\rho)\big(|\eta+\rho|-|\sigma|\big)\big\}\\
&+\frac{1}{3}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\rho)|\sigma|^{1/2}|\rho|\big\{(|\eta+\rho|+|\eta+\sigma|-|\rho|-|\sigma|)-\chi(-\eta-\rho,\eta)\big(|\eta+\rho|-|\sigma|\big)\big\}.\end{split}

The desired bounds (similar to (
6.7
)) follow in this case, since the multiplier
m_{N_{0}}
is applied to the lower frequencies. On the other hand, if
k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-5
then we may assume that the frequency parameters
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3}
are as in (
6.6
). Then we write, using (
6.8
),

\begin{split}\widetilde{K}^{1,1}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&=\frac{1}{3}\Big\{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\sigma|^{1/2}|\xi|\big\{\iota(\xi)\rho+(1-\chi(\rho+\sigma,\eta))\big[|\rho+\sigma|-|\sigma|\big]\big\}\\
&+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)|\sigma|^{1/2}|\eta|\big\{\iota(\eta)\rho+(1-\chi(\rho+\sigma,\xi))\big[|\rho+\sigma|-|\sigma|\big]\big\}\\
&+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\rho)|\sigma|^{1/2}|\rho|(|\xi+\sigma|+|\eta+\sigma|-|\rho|-|\sigma|)\Big\},\end{split}

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
(using the identity
|\eta+\sigma|-|\xi|=|\xi+\rho|-|\xi|=\iota(\xi+\rho)(\xi+\rho)-\iota(\xi)\xi=\iota(\xi)\rho
and a similar identity with
\xi
and
\eta
interchanged). The desired bounds (similar to (
6.7
)) follow by symmetrizing two terms in
\xi
and
\eta
to avoid derivative loss.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{2}
.
We will show that the symbol
K_{1}^{2}
is admissible without symmetrization,

\|K^{2}_{1}\cdot\varphi_{\underline{k}}\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{8}}\lesssim 2^{\widetilde{k}_{4}/2}2^{2\widetilde{k}_{3}}2^{2N_{0}\max(\widetilde{k}_{1},0)}

(6.10)

for any
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. By symmetry, we may assume that
k_{2}\geq k_{3}
. These bounds follow easily from the formula (
4.92
) if
\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-40
, since in this case there is no derivative loss and the factors
|\rho+\sigma|=|\xi+\eta|
and
|\eta+\sigma|=|\xi+\rho|
are non-singular in the support of the symbol.

On the other hand, if
\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-40
then we have two nontrivial cases. Either

k_{2},k_{4}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\qquad k_{1},k_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-40,

in which case the formula (
4.92
) gives

K_{1}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=-\frac{m_{N_{0}}(\eta+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\rho)|\xi|\chi(\xi,\rho)\big\{\sigma(\xi+\rho)(1-\chi(\eta,\sigma))-|\sigma||\xi+\rho|\widetilde{\chi}(\eta,\sigma)\big\}}{|\sigma|^{1/2}}

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of the function
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
. There is no derivative loss in this case (since one of the factors
m_{N_{0}}(\rho)
applies to one of the low frequencies), and the bounds (
6.10
) follow easily. The remaining possibility is

k_{2},k_{3}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\qquad k_{1},k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-40,

in which case the formula (
4.92
) gives

K_{1}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=-\frac{m_{N_{0}}(\rho+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)|\xi|\sigma(\rho+\sigma)}{|\sigma|^{1/2}}-\frac{m_{N_{0}}(\eta+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\rho)|\xi|\sigma(\eta+\sigma)}{|\sigma|^{1/2}},

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
. The bounds (
6.10
) follow in this case as well, by symmetrization in the variables
\eta
and
\rho
(replacing
\eta
with
-\rho-\sigma-\xi
in the formula above).

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{3}
.
We examine the formula (
4.93
) and decompose
K_{1}^{3}=K_{1}^{3,1}+K_{1}^{3,2}
,

\begin{split}K_{1}^{3,1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta+\rho)[|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)+|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)]}{2|\sigma|^{1/2}}(-(\eta+\rho)\sigma-|\eta+\rho||\sigma|)\widetilde{\chi}(\sigma,\xi),\\
K_{1}^{3,2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&:=\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta+\rho)[|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)+|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)]}{2|\sigma|^{1/2}}(\xi+\sigma)\sigma\chi(\sigma,\xi),\end{split}

(6.11)

since
\xi+\sigma=-(\eta+\rho)
along
{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
. It is easy to see that there is no derivative loss in
K_{1}^{3,1}
and, in fact,
K_{1}^{3,1}
is regular,
K_{1}^{3,1}\in\mathcal{M}^{5/2}
.

The symbol
K_{1}^{3,2}
requires symmetrization, to avoid derivative loss. The analysis is similar to some of the symbols discussed earlier. If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is a balanced
4
-tuple then there is no derivative loss and the symbol
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{3,2}_{1}
itself satisfies regular bounds similar to (
6.5
). On the other hand, if
\underline{k}
is imbalanced then we may assume, by symmetry, the frequency configuration (
6.6
), and symmetrize in
\xi
and
\eta
as before, by substituting
\xi=-\eta-\rho-\sigma
.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{4}
.

We do not need symmetrization in this case, and we will prove that
K_{1}^{4}
is already admissible. Recall the expression for
b
from (
4.75
), which contains the possibly singular factor
|\xi+\sigma|=|\eta+\rho|
. There is no derivative loss in this case and
m_{N_{0}}
satisfies homogeneous bounds of the form
|\partial_{\mu}^{\alpha}m_{N_{0}}(\mu)|\lesssim|\mu|^{N_{0}-|\alpha|}
for
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
and
|\alpha|\leq 8
(see definition (
4.2
)). Therefore,
(\varphi_{\underline{k}}K_{1}^{4})\simeq 0
for any
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
(see the notation (
6.2
)), with a possibly singular factor
|\xi+\sigma|
that could be present if if
|k_{1}-k_{4}|\leq 4
and
|k_{2}-k_{3}|\leq 4
.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{5}
.

By inspection of the formula (
4.95
) we see directly that
K_{1}^{5}\in\mathcal{M}^{5/2}
.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{6}
.
We inspect the formula (
4.96
) and notice that there is no derivative loss due to the difference
m_{N_{0}}(\eta+\rho)-m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)
. It follows easily that
K_{1}^{6}\in\mathcal{M}^{5/2}
.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{7}
.

We show that the symbol
K_{1}^{7}
itself is admissible. Recall the formula for
a_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho)
from (
4.73
). By symmetry, to analyze the localized multiplier
\varphi_{\underline{k}}K_{1}^{7}
we may assume that
k_{2}\geq k_{3}
. We have two cases. If
k_{1}\geq k_{2}+6
then
k_{1}
and
k_{4}
are the large frequency parameters, and we have a suitable decomposition with a possibly singular factor
|\eta+\rho|
. On the other hand, if
k_{1}\leq k_{2}+5
then we replace
\eta+\rho
with
-\xi-\sigma
and notice that
\varphi_{\underline{k}}K_{1}^{7}
is nontrivial only if
|k_{1}-k_{4}|\leq 2
. We can then see easily that
(\varphi_{\underline{k}}K_{1}^{7})\simeq 0
for any
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
, with a possibly singular factor
|\xi+\sigma|
.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{8}
.

We examine the formula (
4.98
), decompose
K_{1}^{8}=K_{1}^{8,1}+K_{1}^{8,2}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K_{1}^{8,1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=6n_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho)A^{\prime}(\xi,\sigma,\eta+\rho)|\xi|^{1/2}(|\eta+\rho|+|\sigma|-|\xi|),\\
&K_{1}^{8,2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=6n_{2}^{ri}(\xi,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\rho|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2},\end{split}

(6.12)

and recall the formulas (
4.78
)–(
4.79
) for
n_{2}^{ri}
and
n_{2}^{rr}
. The formulas (
4.30
) and (
4.38
) show that

\begin{split}A^{\prime}(x,y,z)&=\frac{\max(|x|,|y|,|z|)}{-12|x|^{1/2}|y|^{1/2}|z|^{1/2}}\big\{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(x)[\iota(y)\iota(z)+\widetilde{\chi}(y,z)]\\
&\qquad\qquad+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(y)[\iota(z)\iota(x)+\widetilde{\chi}(z,x)]+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(z)[\iota(x)\iota(y)+\widetilde{\chi}(x,y)]\big\},\end{split}

(6.13)

for any
(x,y,z)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\infty}
, since
A^{\prime}=A/D
. As a consequence, for any
l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\|A^{\prime}(x,y,z)\cdot\varphi_{l_{1}}(x)\varphi_{l_{2}}(y)\varphi_{l_{3}}(z)\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{12}}\lesssim 2^{\min(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})/2}2^{(2N_{0}-1)\max(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})}.

(6.14)

If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is a balanced
4
-tuple then there is no derivative loss and the symbol
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{8,1}_{1}
satisfies regular bounds similar to (
6.5
). The symbol
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{8,2}_{1}
contains the possibly singular factor
|\xi+\sigma|
, but can be expanded as in (
5.12
), which still suffices to prove admissibility.

If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is an imbalanced
4
-tuple then we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
and consider two cases:

\bullet

k_{1},k_{2}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{3},k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. In this case

\begin{split}\widetilde{K}^{8,1}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&=(1/2)|\rho+\eta|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)A^{\prime}(\xi,\sigma,\eta+\rho)(|\xi+\sigma|+|\sigma|-|\xi|)\\
&+(1/2)|\rho+\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|\chi(\rho,\xi)A^{\prime}(\eta,\sigma,\xi+\rho)(|\eta+\sigma|+|\sigma|-|\eta|),\end{split}

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
, since
n_{2}^{rr}(\xi,\eta)=0
and therefore
K^{8,1}_{1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)=0
. Using (
6.13
) it is easy to see that both terms are regular, satisfying bounds similar to (
6.5
).

We consider now the symbol
K_{1}^{8,2}
in the second line of (
6.12
). We write

\begin{split}\widetilde{K}^{8,2}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&=2n_{2}^{ri}(\xi,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\rho|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2}\\
&+2n_{2}^{ri}(\eta,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}|\eta+\sigma|^{1/2}\\
&+2n_{2}^{ri}(\rho,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho+\sigma)|\eta|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho+\sigma|^{1/2}\end{split}

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
. The formulas (
6.13
) and (
4.78
) show easily that the multiplier in the last line satisfies suitable admissibility bounds. Moreover, we may replace
n_{2}^{ri}(\xi,\sigma)
with
\xi\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}\chi(\sigma,\xi)
and
n_{2}^{ri}(\eta,\sigma)
with
\eta\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}\chi(\sigma,\eta)
at the expenses of acceptable errors, therefore, with the notation (
6.2
),

\begin{split}(\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}^{8,2}_{1})(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\big\{&2\xi\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}\chi(\sigma,\xi)A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\rho|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2}\\
+&2\eta\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}\chi(\sigma,\eta)A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}|\eta+\sigma|^{1/2}\big\}.\end{split}

In view of (
6.14
) the remaining terms are regular, satisfying bounds similar to (
6.5
).

\bullet

k_{1},k_{4}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{2},k_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. In this case we need to recombine the multipliers
K_{1}^{8,1}
and
K_{1}^{8,2}
to avoid a potential
1/2
derivative loss. Using (
6.12
) and recalling the definition (
6.2
) we notice that

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{8,1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq 0,\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{8,1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|\chi(\rho,\xi)A^{\prime}(\eta,\sigma,\rho+\xi)|\eta|^{1/2}|\sigma|,\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{8,1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|\chi(\eta,\xi)A^{\prime}(\rho,\sigma,\eta+\xi)|\rho|^{1/2}|\sigma|,\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{8,2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq 0,\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{8,2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-3)|\eta||\sigma|\chi(\eta,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2},\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{8,2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-3)|\rho||\sigma|\chi(\rho,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\eta,\xi,\rho+\sigma)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}.\end{split}

Therefore

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)[K_{1}^{8,1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)+K_{1}^{8,2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)]\\
&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3|\rho||\eta|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}|\sigma|\big\{\chi(\rho,\xi)A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho+\xi,\sigma)-\chi(\rho,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\eta,\xi,\rho+\sigma)\big\}\simeq 0\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)[K_{1}^{8,1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)+K_{1}^{8,2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)]\\
&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3|\eta||\rho|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}|\sigma|\big\{\chi(\eta,\xi)A^{\prime}(\rho,\eta+\xi,\sigma)-\chi(\eta,\sigma)A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma)\big\}\simeq 0,\end{split}

as desired.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{9}
.
We examine the formula (
4.99
) and decompose
K_{1}^{9}=K_{1}^{9,1}+K_{1}^{9,2}
, where

\begin{split}&K_{1}^{9,1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-2n_{2}^{rr}(\eta,\rho)B^{\prime\prime}(\eta+\rho,\sigma,\xi),\\
&K_{1}^{9,2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=n_{2}^{ri}(\xi,\sigma)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma).\end{split}

(6.15)

The functions
B^{\prime\prime}
and
B^{\prime\prime\prime}
are defined in (
4.105
)–(
6.3
). To calculate them explicitly we recall the formulas (
4.31
) and (
4.38
); in particular
B
and
B^{\prime}
are symmetric in the first two variables and we calculate, using (
2.43
),

B^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)=\frac{m_{N_{0}}(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}\big\{|\xi|\chi(\xi,\rho)[m_{N_{0}}(\rho)-m_{N_{0}}(\eta)]+|\eta|\chi(\eta,\rho)[m_{N_{0}}(\rho)-m_{N_{0}}(\xi)]\big\}}{-4|\xi||\eta|}

(6.16)

if
(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\infty}
and
|\rho|=\max(|\xi|,|\eta|,|\rho|)
, since
\xi\rho+|\xi||\rho|=\eta\rho+|\eta||\rho|=0
in this case. Moreover

\begin{split}B^{\prime}(\xi,\eta,\rho)&=(1/2)|\rho|^{-1/2}m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)+(1/4)\chi(\eta,\xi)m_{N_{0}}(\rho)|\rho|^{-1/2}[m_{N_{0}}(\xi)-m_{N_{0}}(\rho)]\\
&-\frac{\chi(\rho,\xi)[|\xi|m^{2}_{N_{0}}(\xi)+|\eta|m^{2}_{N_{0}}(\eta)-|\rho|m_{N_{0}}(\xi)m_{N_{0}}(\eta)]}{4|\eta||\rho|^{1/2}}\end{split}

(6.17)

if
(\xi,\eta,\rho)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\infty}
and
|\xi|=\max(|\xi|,|\eta|,|\rho|)
, using (
2.43
) again and the identities
\xi\rho=-|\xi||\rho|
and
\eta\rho=|\eta|\rho|
which hold in this case.

Then we observe that
B^{\prime\prime}
is symmetric in the first two variables and calculate

B^{\prime\prime}(x,y,z)=|x|^{1/2}|y|^{1/2}\big[(1-\chi(y,z))m_{N_{0}}^{2}(x)+(1-\chi(x,z))m_{N_{0}}^{2}(y)\big]

(6.18)

if
(x,y,z)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\infty}
and
|z|=\max(|x|,|y|,|z|)
, after careful algebraic simplifications. Similarly,

\begin{split}B^{\prime\prime}(x,y,z)=-\frac{1}{2}|x|^{1/2}|y|^{1/2}\big\{&2m_{N_{0}}^{2}(y)+\chi(y,x)[m_{N_{0}}^{2}(x)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(z)]\\
&-\chi(z,x)[m_{N_{0}}^{2}(y)+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(x)]\big\}\end{split}

(6.19)

if
(x,y,z)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\infty}
and
|x|=\max(|x|,|y|,|z|)
. Finally,
B^{\prime\prime\prime}
is symmetric in all the variables and

\begin{split}B^{\prime\prime\prime}(x,y,z)=\frac{1}{2}\big\{&2|y|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(y)+2|z|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(z)\\
&-\chi(y,x)[|x|m^{2}_{N_{0}}(x)+|x|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(z)-2|y|m_{N_{0}}(x)m_{N_{0}}(z)]\\
&-\chi(z,x)[|x|m^{2}_{N_{0}}(x)+|x|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(y)-2|z|m_{N_{0}}(x)m_{N_{0}}(y)]\big\}\end{split}

(6.20)

if
(x,y,z)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{\infty}
and
|x|=\max(|x|,|y|,|z|)
.

In particular, these formulas show easily that for any
l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\begin{split}\|B^{\prime\prime}(x,y,z)\cdot\varphi_{l_{1}}(x)\varphi_{l_{2}}(y)\varphi_{l_{3}}(z)\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{12}}&\lesssim 2^{\min(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})}2^{2N_{0}\max(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})}2^{-|l_{1}-l_{2}|/2},\\
\|B^{\prime\prime\prime}(x,y,z)\cdot\varphi_{l_{1}}(x)\varphi_{l_{2}}(y)\varphi_{l_{3}}(z)\|_{\mathcal{HM}_{12}}&\lesssim 2^{\min(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})}2^{2N_{0}\max(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})}.\end{split}

(6.21)

If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is a balanced
4
-tuple then there is no derivative loss and the formulas (
6.18
)–(
6.19
) and (
4.79
) show easily that the symbol
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{9,1}_{1}
satisfies regular bounds similar to (
6.5
). The symbol
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{9,2}_{1}
contains the possibly singular factor
|\xi+\sigma|
, but can be expanded as in (
5.12
), which still suffices to prove admissibility.

If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is an imbalanced
4
-tuple then we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
and consider two cases, as before:

\bullet

k_{1},k_{2}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{3},k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. In this case

\begin{split}\widetilde{K}^{9,1}_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&=-\frac{1}{6}|\rho+\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|\chi(\rho,\eta)B^{\prime\prime}(\eta+\rho,\sigma,\xi)-\frac{1}{6}|\rho+\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|\chi(\rho,\xi)B^{\prime\prime}(\xi+\rho,\sigma,\eta)\end{split}

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
, using the formulas (
4.79
) and (
6.15
). Using (
6.21
) it is easy to see that both terms are regular, satisfying bounds similar to (
6.5
). Moreover

\begin{split}(\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}^{9,2}_{1})(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\big\{&\xi\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}\chi(\sigma,\xi)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)\\
&+\eta\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}\chi(\sigma,\eta)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi,\rho,\eta+\sigma)\big\}/3,\end{split}

using (
4.78
), (
6.15
), and (
6.20
). In view of (
6.21
) the remaining terms are regular, satisfying bounds similar to (
6.5
), due to the symmetrization in
\xi
and
\eta
.

\bullet

k_{1},k_{4}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{2},k_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. In this case

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{9,1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq 0,\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{9,1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1/2)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|\chi(\rho,\xi)B^{\prime\prime}(\rho+\xi,\sigma,\eta),\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{9,1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1/2)|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|\chi(\eta,\xi)B^{\prime\prime}(\eta+\xi,\sigma,\rho),\end{split}

(6.22)

and

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{9,2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq 0,\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{9,2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1/2)|\eta||\sigma|^{1/2}\chi(\eta,\sigma)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma),\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{1}^{9,2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1/2)|\rho||\sigma|^{1/2}\chi(\rho,\sigma)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta,\xi,\rho+\sigma).\end{split}

(6.23)

using (
6.21
) and (
4.78
)–(
4.79
). The terms are all regular if
\widetilde{k}_{3}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-40
.

On the other hand, if
\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-40
then we need to recombine these terms to avoid derivative loss. Notice that

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\big[K_{1}^{9,1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)+K_{1}^{9,2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)]\\
&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1/2)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|\chi(\rho,\xi)[B^{\prime\prime}(\rho+\xi,\sigma,\eta)+B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta,\xi,\rho+\sigma)].\end{split}

(6.24)

We have two cases: if
\iota(\eta)=\iota(\xi)=-\iota(\sigma)
then we may assume that
|\sigma|=|\rho+\xi|+|\eta|
and
|\rho+\sigma|=|\xi|+|\eta|
. The formulas (
6.19
)–(
6.20
) show that

\begin{split}&2B^{\prime\prime}(\rho+\xi,\sigma,\eta)+2B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta,\xi,\rho+\sigma)=|\sigma|^{1/2}|\rho+\xi|^{1/2}[m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\sigma)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\rho+\xi)]\\
&+(2|\xi|-|\rho+\sigma|)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)-|\rho+\sigma|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\rho+\sigma)+2|\eta|m_{N_{0}}(\xi)m_{N_{0}}(\rho+\sigma)+2|\eta|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\\
&=[|\sigma|^{1/2}|\rho+\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi|][m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\sigma)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\rho+\xi)]-|\eta|[m_{N_{0}}(\xi)-m_{N_{0}}(\rho+\sigma)]^{2}\\
&+|\xi|[m_{N_{0}}^{2}(|\sigma|)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(|\sigma|-|\eta|)+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(|\xi|)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(|\xi|+|\eta|)]+2|\eta|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta),\end{split}

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
satisfying
\iota(\eta)=\iota(\xi)=-\iota(\sigma)
.
On the other hand, if
\iota(\eta)=-\iota(\xi)=\iota(\sigma)
then we may assume that
|\sigma|+|\eta|=|\rho+\xi|
and
|\rho+\sigma|+|\eta|=|\xi|
. The formulas (
6.19
)–(
6.20
) show that

\begin{split}&2B^{\prime\prime}(\rho+\xi,\sigma,\eta)+2B^{\prime\prime\prime}(\eta,\xi,\rho+\sigma)=|\sigma|^{1/2}|\rho+\xi|^{1/2}[m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\rho+\xi)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\sigma)]\\
&+(2|\rho+\sigma|-|\xi|)m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\rho+\sigma)-|\xi|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)+2|\eta|m_{N_{0}}(\rho+\sigma)m_{N_{0}}(\xi)+2|\eta|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)\\
&=[|\sigma|^{1/2}|\rho+\xi|^{1/2}-|\rho+\sigma|][m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\rho+\xi)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\sigma)]-|\eta|[m_{N_{0}}(\rho+\sigma)-m_{N_{0}}(\xi)]^{2}\\
&+|\rho+\sigma|[m_{N_{0}}^{2}(|\sigma|+\eta|)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(|\sigma|)+m_{N_{0}}^{2}(|\xi|-|\eta|)-m_{N_{0}}^{2}(|\xi|)]+2|\eta|m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta),\end{split}

for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
satisfying
\iota(\eta)=-\iota(\xi)=\iota(\sigma)
. These two formulas show that in both cases there is a derivative gain, compensating for the
1/2
derivative loss in the prefactor in (
6.24
). Therefore

\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\big[K_{1}^{9,1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)+K_{1}^{9,2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)]\simeq 0.

A similar calculation with the roles of
\rho
and
\eta
interchanged shows that

\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\big[K_{1}^{9,1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)+K_{1}^{9,2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)]\simeq 0

as well, as desired. This completes the analysis of the symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{9}
.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{2}^{1}
.
We examine the formula (
4.100
) and notice that
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K_{2}^{1}
already satisfies regular bounds similar to (
6.5
) if
\underline{k}
is balanced
4
-tuple. If
\underline{k}
is imbalanced then we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
and consider two cases:

\bullet

k_{1},k_{2}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{3},k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. We calculate

\begin{split}&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{2}^{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\xi)|\xi|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}}{|\eta|^{1/2}}\eta\big[(\rho+\sigma)\chi(\rho,\sigma)-\sigma\chi(\rho,\eta+\sigma)\big],\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{2}^{1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot\frac{m_{N_{0}}^{2}(\eta)|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}}{|\xi|^{1/2}}\xi\big[(\rho+\sigma)\chi(\rho,\sigma)-\sigma\chi(\rho,\xi+\sigma)\big],\\
&\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{2}^{1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\simeq 0.\end{split}

Since
\eta=|\eta|\iota(\eta)
,
\xi=|\xi|\iota(\xi)
, and
\iota(\xi)=-\iota(\eta)
, it follows easily that
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}_{2}^{1}\simeq 0
, as desired.

\bullet

k_{1},k_{4}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{2},k_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. In this case we have

\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{2}^{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{2}^{1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K_{2}^{1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\simeq 0,

by examining the formula (
4.100
). The desired conclusion follows.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{2}^{2}
.
We do not need symmetrization in this case. The formulas (
4.101
) and (
4.73
) show that

\begin{split}K_{2}^{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&=m_{N_{0}}(\xi)[m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)-m_{N_{0}}(\sigma)]\chi(-\xi-\sigma,\sigma)\\
&\times|\xi|^{1/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}(|\eta+\rho|-|\eta|-|\rho|)[\iota(\eta)\iota(\rho)-1]\end{split}

for any
\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
. In particular
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K_{2}^{2}
is nontrivial only if
|k_{1}-k_{4}|\leq 2
, and it is easy to see that the symbol is admissible with a possibly singular factor of
|\eta+\rho|=|\xi+\sigma|
.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{2}^{3}
.
As in the analysis of the symbol
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{8}
we decompose
K_{2}^{3}=K_{2}^{3,1}+K_{2}^{3,2}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K_{2}^{3,1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=6n_{2}^{ii}(\eta,\rho)A^{\prime}(\xi,\sigma,\eta+\rho)|\sigma|^{1/2}(|\eta+\rho|+|\xi|-|\sigma|),\\
&K_{2}^{3,2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=3n_{2}^{ri}(\sigma,\xi)A^{\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma)|\xi+\sigma|^{1/2}(|\rho|+|\eta|-|\xi+\sigma|).\end{split}

(6.25)

The relevant formulas are (
4.78
), (
4.80
), and (
6.13
). We notice that the symbol
n_{2}^{ii}(\rho,\eta)
is not singular in
|\rho+\eta|
, and in fact
|\rho+\eta|\approx|\rho|+|\eta|
in the support of this symbol. If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is a balanced
4
-tuple then there is no derivative loss and it is easy to see that the symbols
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{3,1}_{2}
and
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\cdot K^{3,2}_{2}
satisfy suitable admissible bounds.

If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is imbalanced then we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
and, as before, consider two cases:

\bullet

k_{1},k_{2}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{3},k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. In this case

\begin{split}\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,1}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3\eta|\rho|^{1/2}\iota(\rho)A^{\prime}(\xi,\sigma,\eta+\rho)|\sigma|^{1/2}(|\eta|+|\xi|),\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,1}_{2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3\xi|\rho|^{1/2}\iota(\rho)A^{\prime}(\eta,\sigma,\xi+\rho)|\sigma|^{1/2}(|\eta|+|\xi|),\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,1}_{2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)&=0.\end{split}

Using (
6.14
) we have
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}^{3,1}_{2}\simeq 0
, as desired. Moreover,

\begin{split}\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,2}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,2}_{2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,2}_{2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\simeq 0,\end{split}

therefore
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}^{3,2}_{2}\simeq 0
, as desired.

\bullet

k_{1},k_{4}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{2},k_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. Using (
6.13
)–(
6.14
) we see that

\begin{split}\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,1}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&\simeq 0,\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,1}_{2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}\xi|\sigma|^{1/2}(|\rho+\xi|+|\eta|-|\sigma|)A^{\prime}(\eta,\sigma,\rho+\xi),\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,1}_{2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3\eta|\eta|^{-1/2}\xi|\sigma|^{1/2}(|\eta+\xi|+|\rho|-|\sigma|)A^{\prime}(\rho,\sigma,\eta+\xi),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,2}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&\simeq 0,\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,2}_{2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3\eta|\eta|^{-1/2}\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}A^{\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma)(|\xi|+|\rho|-|\eta+\sigma|),\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{3,2}_{2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot 3\rho|\rho|^{-1/2}\sigma|\sigma|^{-1/2}A^{\prime}(\eta,\xi,\rho+\sigma)(|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho+\sigma|).\end{split}

As before, we need to combine these terms in order to avoid a
1/2
derivative loss. Notice that
|\rho+\xi|+|\eta|-|\sigma|=|\xi|+|\eta|-|\rho+\sigma|=|\eta|+\iota(\xi)(\rho+\xi+\sigma)
for
(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{3}_{\infty}
in the support of
\varphi_{\underline{k}}
. Using again (
6.14
) it follows that
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)[K_{2}^{3,1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)+K_{2}^{3,2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)]\simeq 0
. Similarly, we have
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)[K_{2}^{3,1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)+K_{2}^{3,2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)]\simeq 0
, so
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}_{2}^{3}\simeq 0
, as desired.

The symbol
\widetilde{K}_{2}^{4}
.
We examine the formula (
4.103
) and decompose
K_{2}^{4}=K_{2}^{4,1}+K_{2}^{4,2}
, where

\begin{split}&K_{2}^{4,1}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-2n_{2}^{ii}(\eta,\rho)B^{\prime\prime}(\eta+\rho,\xi,\sigma),\\
&K_{2}^{4,2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma):=-n_{2}^{ri}(\sigma,\xi)B^{\prime\prime}(\eta,\rho,\xi+\sigma).\end{split}

(6.26)

The function
B^{\prime\prime}
,
n_{2}^{ri}
, and
n_{2}^{ii}
are defined in (
6.18
)–(
6.19
), (
4.78
), and (
4.80
).

As in the analysis of the multiplier
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{9}
, it is easy to see that
\varphi_{\underline{k}}K_{2}^{4,1}\simeq 0
and
\varphi_{\underline{k}}K_{2}^{4,2}\simeq 0
if
\underline{k}
is a balanced
4
-tuple. If
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{4}
is imbalanced then we may assume that
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq k_{3}
and consider two cases, as before:

\bullet

k_{1},k_{2}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{3},k_{4}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. In this case, using (
6.21
),

\begin{split}\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,1}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1)\iota(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}\eta B^{\prime\prime}(\eta+\rho,\xi,\sigma),\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,1}_{2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1)\iota(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}\xi B^{\prime\prime}(\xi+\rho,\eta,\sigma),\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,1}_{2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)&=0,\end{split}

and, using also (
6.18
)–(
6.19
),

\begin{split}\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,2}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,2}_{2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,2}_{2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)\simeq 0.\end{split}

Therefore, using (
6.21
) we have
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}^{4,1}_{2}\simeq 0
and
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}^{4,2}_{2}\simeq 0
, as desired.

\bullet

k_{1},k_{4}\in[\widetilde{k}_{1}-4,\widetilde{k}_{1}]
,
k_{2},k_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
. Using (
6.18
)–(
6.19
) and (
6.21
) we see that

\begin{split}\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,1}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&\simeq 0,\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,1}_{2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1)\iota(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}\xi B^{\prime\prime}(\xi+\rho,\eta,\sigma),\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,1}_{2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1)\iota(\eta)|\eta|^{1/2}\xi B^{\prime\prime}(\xi+\eta,\rho,\sigma),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,2}_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)&\simeq 0,\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,2}_{2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1)\iota(\eta)|\eta|^{1/2}\sigma B^{\prime\prime}(\rho,\xi,\eta+\sigma),\\
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)K^{4,2}_{2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)&\simeq\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)\cdot(-1)\iota(\rho)|\rho|^{1/2}\sigma B^{\prime\prime}(\eta,\xi,\rho+\sigma).\end{split}

As before, we combine these terms in order to avoid a
1/2
derivative loss. Using again (
6.21
) it follows that
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)[K_{2}^{4,1}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)+K_{2}^{4,2}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)]\simeq 0
and
\varphi_{\underline{k}}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)[K_{2}^{4,1}(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma)+K_{2}^{4,2}(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)]\simeq 0
, so
\varphi_{\underline{k}}\widetilde{K}_{2}^{4}\simeq 0
, as desired.

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

Let
(h,\phi)\in C([T_{1},T_{2}]:H^{N_{0}}\times\dot{H}^{N_{0},1/2}
be a solution of (
1.4
) on the time-interval
[T_{1},T_{2}]
, satisfying the assumptions of the theorem.
Recall that we defined

\displaystyle\omega=\phi-T_{B}h,\qquad A(t)={\|(h+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega)(t)\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}.

(6.27)

In view of (
1.7
) we have
A(t)\lesssim A
for all
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
.
Recall also the definition of the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{2}
in (
4.4
)
with
J^{N_{0}}
the operator whose symbol is defined in (
4.2
),
and notice that, as
\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}
we have

\displaystyle{\|P_{\geq 2}h(t)\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}+{\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}P_{\geq 2}\omega(t)\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{2}(t)\lesssim A^{2}(t).

(6.28)

Combining Lemmas
4.1
,
4.6
and
4.7
we have,
for all
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|\mathcal{E}_{2}(t_{2})-\mathcal{E}_{2}(t_{1})\big|\lesssim{\varepsilon}(t_{1})A^{2}(t_{1})+{\varepsilon}(t_{2})A^{2}(t_{2})\\
&+\Big|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{1}(h^{\ast},h^{\ast},h^{\ast},\omega^{\ast})(s)+\mathcal{Q}_{2}(\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},\omega^{\ast},h^{\ast})(s)\big]\,ds\Big|+A^{2}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}{\varepsilon}^{3}(s)\,ds,\end{split}

(6.29)

where
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
are defined by (
4.89
)-(
4.90
).
These expressions are defined by the symbols
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{a}
and
\widetilde{K}_{2}^{b}
, and all these symbols are admissible in the sense of Definition
5.1
(see Lemma
6.1
). We would like to apply the formulas (
5.4
) and (
5.8
), and then Lemmas
5.2
and
5.3
. This is possible provided that we verify the key vanishing condition (
NR-BF
):

Proposition 6.2
.

The symmetrized symbol
SK_{1}-SK_{2}
(see definition (
5.6
