)) vanishes on the set of Benjamin-Feir resonances, i.e.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(SK_{1}-SK_{2})(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})=0\qquad\text{ provided that }\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3}.\xi_{4})\in{\mathbb{H}}_{\infty}^{3}\text{ satisfies}\\
&\xi_{1}\in(-\infty,0),\,\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\in(0,\infty)\text{ and }|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}=|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{4}|^{1/2}.\end{split}

(6.30)

We will prove this proposition in section
9
, by explicit calculations. Assuming this, we can apply Lemmas
5.2
and
5.3
, and the inequality (
6.29
) we conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{2}(t_{2})\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{2}(t_{1})+{\varepsilon}(t_{1})A^{2}(t_{1})+{\varepsilon}(t_{2})A^{2}(t_{2})+A^{2}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}{\varepsilon}^{3}(s)\,ds.\end{split}

(6.31)

To conclude the proof we need to include low frequencies
using the Hamiltonian (
1.6
) which is conserved. Under our assumptions
on
h
in (
1.7
),
for all
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
we have

\displaystyle{\|h(t)\|}_{L^{2}}^{2}+{\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(t)\|}_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{H}(h,\phi)(t)\lesssim A^{2}(t).

(6.32)

In particular, by the definition of
\omega
, for all
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\|P_{\leq 4}h(t_{2})\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}+{\|P_{\leq 4}|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega(t_{2})\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}\lesssim{\|h(t_{2})\|}_{L^{2}}^{2}+{\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(t_{2})\|}_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim A^{2}(t_{1}).\end{split}

(6.33)

Using (
6.28
), (
6.31
), (
6.33
) we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|h(t_{2})\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}+{\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega(t_{2})\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}&\lesssim A^{2}(t_{1})+{\varepsilon}(t_{2})A^{2}(t_{2})+A^{2}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}{\varepsilon}^{3}(s)\,ds,\end{split}

(6.34)

which gives the desired bounds (
1.8
).

7.
Long-term regularity: proof of Theorem
1.2

In this section we prove Theorem
1.2
on the long-term regularity of the system (
1.4
) with small initial-data. We provide the argument in the (harder) case of finite
R
, but the argument applies with almost no changes in the Euclidean case
R=\infty
as well (the Euclidean Strichartz estimates in Lemma
7.2
are global
T=\infty
, and the point is that all the estimates hold uniformly in
R
).

7.1.
Integration by parts and Strichartz estimates

We use sometimes integration by parts to estimate oscillatory integrals with non-degenerate phases, according to the following:

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume that
0<\epsilon\leq 1/\epsilon\leq K
,
N\geq 1
is an integer, and
f,g\in C^{N}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
d\geq 1
. Then

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{iKf}g\,dx\Big|\lesssim_{N}(K\epsilon)^{-N}\big[\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\epsilon^{|\alpha|}\|D^{\alpha}_{x}g\|_{L^{1}}\big],

(7.1)

provided that
f
is real-valued,

|\nabla_{x}f|\geq\mathbf{1}_{{\mathrm{supp}}\,g},\quad\text{ and }\quad\|D_{x}^{\alpha}f\cdot\mathbf{1}_{{\mathrm{supp}}\,g}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{N}\epsilon^{1-|\alpha|},\,2\leq|\alpha|\leq N+1.

(7.2)

Let
\Lambda(\xi):=|\xi|^{1/2}
denote the dispersion relation associated to gravity water waves. The proof of Theorem
1.2
is based on the following Strichartz estimates on the torus
\mathbb{T}_{R}
:

Lemma 7.2
.

If
R\geq 1
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
T\leq R2^{k^{-}/2}
then, for any
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{R})
,

\|e^{-it\Lambda}P_{k}f\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{T}_{R}\times[-T,T])}\lesssim 2^{3k/8}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{R})}.

(7.3)

Proof.

We adapt the standard proof of Strichartz estimates to our large box setting. Using Sobolev embedding

\|e^{-it\Lambda}P_{k}f\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{T}_{R}\times[-T,T])}\lesssim T^{1/4}2^{k/2}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{R})},

so the desired bounds (
7.3
) follow if
2^{k}\lesssim R^{-1}
. So we may assume that
R2^{k}\geq 2^{10}
. Let
K_{k}
denote the kernel of the operator
e^{-it\Lambda}P_{k}
, i.e.

\begin{split}&e^{-it\Lambda}P_{k}f(x)=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}}f(y)K_{k}(x-y,t),\\
&K_{k}(z,t):=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{Z}/R}\varphi_{k}(\xi)e^{-it\Lambda(\xi)}e^{i\xi z}.\end{split}

(7.4)

Then

\|e^{-it\Lambda}P_{k}f\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{T}_{R}\times[-T,T])}\lesssim\sup_{\|g\|_{L^{4/3}_{t}L^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{T}_{R}\times[-T,T])}\leq 1}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}^{2}\times[-T,T]}f(y)K_{k}(x-y,t)g(x,t)\,dtdxdy\Big|.

Therefore, for (
7.3
) it suffices to prove that

\|Ig\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\lesssim 2^{3k/8}\|g\|_{L^{4/3}_{t}L^{1}_{x}},\qquad\text{ where }\,\,Ig(y):=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}\times[-T,T]}K_{k}(x-y,t)g(x,t)\,dtdx.

(7.5)

The formula for the kernel
K_{k}
in (
7.4
) shows that

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}}K_{k}(x-y,t)\overline{K_{k}(z-y,s)}\,dy=K^{\prime}_{k}(x-z,t-s),\\
&K^{\prime}_{k}(w,t):=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{Z}/R}\varphi^{2}_{k}(\xi)e^{-it\Lambda(\xi)}e^{i\xi w},\end{split}

(7.6)

for any
x,z\in\mathbb{T}_{R}
and
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}}|Ig(y)|^{2}\,dy=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{R}\times[-T,T]^{2}}g(x,t)\overline{g(z,s)}K^{\prime}_{k}(x-z,t-s)\,dtdsdxdz.

By fractional integration, for (
7.5
) it suffices to prove that

|K^{\prime}_{k}(w,t)|\lesssim 2^{3k/4}|t|^{-1/2}\qquad\text{ for any }t\in[-2T,2T]\text{ and }w\in\mathbb{T}_{R}.

(7.7)

To prove the dispersive bounds (
7.7
) we need to use assumption
T\leq R2^{k^{-}/2}
. In view of the definition (
7.6
) it suffices to show that

\frac{1}{2\pi R}\Big|\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi^{2}_{0}(n/(2^{k}R))e^{-itR^{-1/2}\sqrt{|n|}}e^{inw}\Big|\lesssim 2^{3k/4}|t|^{-1/2}

(7.8)

for any
t\in[-2T,2T]
and
w\in[0,2\pi]
. We use the Poisson summation formula for the function
f(x):=(2\pi R)^{-1}\varphi^{2}_{0}(x/(2^{k}R))e^{-itR^{-1/2}\sqrt{|x|}}e^{ixw}
, thus

\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi^{2}_{0}(n/(2^{k}R))e^{-itR^{-1/2}\sqrt{|n|}}e^{inw}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}(2\pi n),

(7.9)

where
\widehat{f}
denotes the Fourier transform of
f
on
\mathbb{R}
. We write now

\begin{split}\widehat{f}(\xi)&=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{2}_{0}(x/(2^{k}R))e^{-itR^{-1/2}\sqrt{|x|}}e^{-ix(\xi-w)}\,dx\\
&=\frac{2^{k}}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{2}_{0}(x)e^{-i(t2^{k/2})\sqrt{|x|}}e^{-i(2^{k}R)(\xi-w)x}\,dx.\end{split}

(7.10)

If
|n|\geq 10
then we recall that
|t2^{k/2}|\leq 2R2^{k}
and
|w|\leq 2\pi
, and use integration by parts (Lemma
7.1
with
K\approx r^{k}R
,
\epsilon\approx 1
) to see that
|\widehat{f}(2\pi n)|\lesssim 2^{k}(2^{k}R)^{-2}n^{-2}
. On the other hand, if
|n|\leq 10
then we use general stationary phase estimates to show that
|\widehat{f}(2\pi n)|\lesssim 2^{k}|t2^{k/2}|^{-1/2}
. The desired bounds (
7.8
) follow using also (
7.9
).
∎

7.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.2

Given a solution of (
1.4
) with data as in (
1.15
),
we use the Strichartz estimate
7.3
and the energy estimate (
1.8
)
to prove the following bootstrap argument, which implies Theorem
1.2
: let
N_{1}:=N_{0}-3\geq 4
,

u:=h+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\omega,\qquad\omega=\phi-T_{B}h,

and assume that
u\in C([0,T]:H^{N_{0}})
,
T\leq T_{R,\varepsilon}:=c_{0}^{2}{\varepsilon}^{-3}\min\big({\varepsilon}^{-3},R^{3/4}\big)
, is a solution satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big[{\big\|u(t)\big\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}+\big\|\,|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(t)\big\|_{H^{N_{0}-1/2}}\big]&\leq 2C_{0}{\varepsilon},\qquad,\\
\sup_{t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[0,T],\,|t_{2}-t_{1}|\leq T_{S}}\|\delta(.)\|_{L^{4}_{[t_{1},t_{2}]}}&\leq 2C^{2}_{0}{\varepsilon},\\
\delta(s):=\big\|u(s)\big\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1},-1/4}_{x}}&+\big\|\,|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(s)\big\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-1/2,-1/4}_{x}},\end{split}

(7.11)

where
C_{0}\gg 1
and
c_{0}=C_{0}^{-20}\ll 1
are suitable constants, and
T_{S}:=c_{0}\min({\varepsilon}^{-4},R)
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big[{\big\|u(t)\big\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}+\big\|\,|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(t)\big\|_{H^{N_{0}-1/2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}{\varepsilon},

(7.12)

\displaystyle\sup_{t_{1}\leq t_{2}\in[0,T],\,|t_{2}-t_{1}|\leq T_{S}}\|\delta(.)\|_{L^{4}_{[t_{1},t_{2}]}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}_{0}{\varepsilon}.

(7.13)

Proof of (
7.12
)

With
\overline{C}
denoting sufficiently large constants (depending only on the implicit constants in (
1.8
) and (
7.1
), it follows from (
1.8
) that for any
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle{\|u(t)\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}{\varepsilon}^{2}+\overline{C}(C_{0}{\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{0}^{t}[\delta(s)]^{3}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}{\varepsilon}^{2}+\overline{C}(C_{0}{\varepsilon})^{2}\cdot T^{1/4}\cdot{\|\delta(.)\|}_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}}^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}\big[{\varepsilon}^{2}+(C_{0}{\varepsilon})^{2}\cdot T_{R,{\varepsilon}}^{1/4}\cdot\big((T_{R,{\varepsilon}}/T_{S})\cdot(C_{0}^{2}{\varepsilon})^{4}\big)^{3/4}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}\big[{\varepsilon}^{2}+C_{0}^{8}\cdot{\varepsilon}^{5}\cdot T_{R,{\varepsilon}}\cdot T_{S}^{-3/4}\big].

For the last inequality we have split the time integral over
[0,T]

into at most
T_{R,{\varepsilon}}/T_{S}
many intervals of size
T_{S}
and used the assumption on the Strichartz norm (
7.11
).
The desired bounds on
u
in (
7.12
) follow by choosing
C_{0}
large enough and then
c_{0}=C_{0}^{-20}
small enough.

The desired bounds on
|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi
follow if
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small, since
|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi=\Im(u)+|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}T_{B}h
and
\|B(s)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{0}-7/4,-1/4}_{x}}\leq\overline{C}C_{0}\varepsilon
for any
s\in[0,T]
(due to (
3.4
) and Sobolev embedding).

Proof of (
7.13
)

We first need to introduce a new variable satisfying a cubic equation.
Let

\displaystyle v:=h+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi+\frac{1}{2}|\partial_{x}|\Big(\frac{\partial_{x}}{|\partial_{x}|}h\cdot\frac{\partial_{x}}{|\partial_{x}|}h\Big)+i\frac{\partial_{x}}{|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}}\Big(\frac{\partial_{x}}{|\partial_{x}|}h\cdot|\partial_{x}|\phi\Big),

(7.14)

compare with
[
35
, Lemma 5.1]
. It follows from the assumptions (
7.11
) and Sobolev embedding that

\|h(s)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{0}-3/4.-1/4}}+\|\,|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(s)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{0}-5/4.-1/4}}+\|B(s)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{0}-7/4.-1/4}}\leq\overline{C}C_{0}\varepsilon,

(7.15)

for any
s\in[0,T]
. The definition (
7.14
) and the product estimates in Lemma
2.4
show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|(u-v)(t)\|}_{{\dot{W}^{N_{1},-1/4}_{x}}}\leq\overline{C}C_{0}{\varepsilon}\delta(t)\end{split}

(7.16)

for all
t\in[0,T]
, Therefore, if
{\varepsilon}
is sufficiently small and
I\subseteq[0,T]
is an interval with
|I|\leq T_{S}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|u\big\|}_{L^{4}_{t}(I){\dot{W}^{N_{1},-1/4}_{x}}}\leq{\|v\big\|}_{L^{4}_{t}(I){\dot{W}^{N_{1},-1/4}_{x}}}+C^{2}_{0}\varepsilon/4.\end{split}

(7.17)

On the other hand, the point of the quadratic correction in definition (
7.14
) is that the variable
v
solves a cubic equation

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2})v=\mathcal{N}_{3},

(7.18)

where
\mathcal{N}_{3}
is a cubic and higher order nonlinearity in
(h,\phi)
that
satisfies the bounds

\displaystyle{\big\|\mathcal{N}_{3}(t)\big\|}_{H^{N_{0}-2}}\leq\overline{C}\big({\|h(t)\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}+\|\,|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}-1/2}}\big)\delta(t)^{2}\leq\overline{C}(C_{0}{\varepsilon})\delta(t)^{2}

(7.19)

for any
t\in[0,T]
. Indeed, this follows easily from (
3.12
) and (
3.14
), or from the analysis in
[
35
, Section 5]
.
In particular, for any intervals
I\subseteq[0,T]
with
|I|\leq T_{S}
we have

\displaystyle{\big\|\mathcal{N}_{3}\big\|}_{L^{2}_{t}(I)H^{N_{0}-2}_{x}}\leq\overline{C}C_{0}^{5}{\varepsilon}^{3}.

(7.20)

We apply the Strichartz estimate (
7.3
) to the Duhamel formula associated to (
7.18
):
for any
I=[t_{1},t_{2}]\subseteq[0,T]
, with
|I|\leq T_{S}
, we have

\displaystyle{\|v\|}_{L^{4}_{t}(I)\dot{W}^{N_{1},-1/4}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}{\|v(t_{1})\|}_{H^{N_{1}+1/2}}+\overline{C}{\Big\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t}e^{i(t-s)|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}}\mathcal{N}_{3}(s)\,ds\Big\|}_{L^{4}_{t}(I)\dot{W}^{N_{1},-1/4}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}{\|u(t_{1})\|}_{H^{N_{0}}}+\overline{C}{\big\|\mathcal{N}_{3}\big\|}_{L^{1}_{t}(I)H^{N_{1}+1/2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}C_{0}{\varepsilon}+\overline{C}|I|^{1/2}{\big\|\mathcal{N}_{3}\big\|}_{L^{2}_{t}(I)H^{N_{0}-2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}C_{0}{\varepsilon}+\overline{C}|T_{S}|^{1/2}\cdot C_{0}^{5}{\varepsilon}^{3},

having used (
7.20
) for the last inequality.
Since
T_{S}\leq c_{0}{\varepsilon}^{-4}
and
c_{0}=C_{0}^{-20}
is small enough,
and using also (
7.17
), we obtain

{\|u\big\|}_{L^{4}_{t}(I){\dot{W}^{N_{1},-1/4}_{x}}}+{\|v\big\|}_{L^{4}_{t}(I){\dot{W}^{N_{1},-1/4}_{x}}}\leq C^{2}_{0}\varepsilon/2,

for any interval
I\subseteq[0,T]
with
|I|\leq T_{S}
. The desired bounds (
7.13
) follow using also (
7.15
), since
|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi=\Im(u)+|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}T_{B}h
.

8.
Proofs of Lemmas
3.1
and
3.2

8.1.
Proof of Lemma
3.1

We adapt the approach in
[
37
, Appendix B]
.

Step 1.
We use complex analysis to define our main objects. Given any function
f:{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\to\mathbb{C}
let
f^{\#}
denote its
2\pi R
-periodic extension to
\mathbb{R}
. In particular, let
h^{\#}
denote the periodic extension of the height function
h
. Let

\gamma(x):=x+ih^{\#}(x)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\Omega^{\#}:=\{x+iy\in\mathbb{C}:y<h^{\#}(x)\}.

(8.1)

For any
f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{R})
we define the
perturbed Hilbert transform

\begin{split}&(\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}f)(\alpha):=\frac{1}{\pi i}\mathrm{p.v.}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{f^{\#}(\beta)\gamma^{\prime}(\beta)}{\gamma(\alpha)-\gamma(\beta)}\,d\beta:=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{|\beta-\alpha|\in[\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}]}\frac{f^{\#}(\beta)\gamma^{\prime}(\beta)}{\gamma(\alpha)-\gamma(\beta)}\,d\beta.\end{split}

(8.2)

Clearly
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}f
is
2\pi R
-periodic. For any
z\in\Omega^{\#}
and
f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{R})
we define

F_{f}(z):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{f^{\#}(\beta)\gamma^{\prime}(\beta)}{z-\gamma(\beta)}\,d\beta:=\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|\beta-\Re z|\leq\lambda}\frac{f^{\#}(\beta)\gamma^{\prime}(\beta)}{z-\gamma(\beta)}\,d\beta.

(8.3)

Clearly,
F_{f}
is an analytic function in
\Omega^{\#}
. For
\epsilon>0
let

(\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}^{\epsilon}f)(\alpha):=F_{f}(\gamma(\alpha)-i\epsilon)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{f^{\#}(\beta)\gamma^{\prime}(\beta)}{\gamma(\alpha)-i\epsilon-\gamma(\beta)}\,d\beta.

(8.4)

It is easy to see that

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}^{\epsilon}f=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})f

(8.5)

uniformly on
\mathbb{R}
, for any
f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{R})
.

Step 2.
The perturbed Hilbert transform can be used to derive explicit formulas for
G(h)\phi
. To justify the calculations we need a technical lemma:

Lemma 8.1
.

Assume that
\varepsilon,A
are as in Proposition
3.1
and
f\in H^{3}({\mathbb{T}}_{R})
satisfies

\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}f\|_{\dot{W}^{1,0}}\leq 1.

(8.6)

Let

(H_{0}f)(\alpha):=\frac{-1}{\pi}\mathrm{p.v.}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{f^{\#}(\beta)}{\alpha-\beta}\,d\beta,

(8.7)

denote the standard Hilbert transform, and consider the real operators
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
defined by

\begin{split}&(T_{1}f)(\alpha):=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{h^{\#}(\alpha)-h^{\#}(\beta)-\partial_{\beta}h^{\#}(\beta)(\alpha-\beta)}{|\gamma(\alpha)-\gamma(\beta)|^{2}}f^{\#}(\beta)\,d\beta,\\
&(T_{2}f)(\alpha):=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{h^{\#}(\alpha)-h^{\#}(\beta)-\partial_{\beta}h^{\#}(\beta)(\alpha-\beta)}{|\gamma(\alpha)-\gamma(\beta)|^{2}}\frac{h^{\#}(\alpha)-h^{\#}(\beta)}{\alpha-\beta}f^{\#}(\beta)\,d\beta.\end{split}

(8.8)

For
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we define the real operators

(R_{n}f)(\alpha):=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{h^{\#}(\alpha)-h^{\#}(\beta)-\partial_{\beta}h^{\#}(\beta)(\alpha-\beta)}{(\alpha-\beta)^{2}}\Big(\frac{h^{\#}(\alpha)-h^{\#}(\beta)}{\alpha-\beta}\Big)^{n}f^{\#}(\beta)\,d\beta.

(8.9)

Then

\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}=iH_{0}-T_{1}+iT_{2},

(8.10)

and

T_{1}=\sum_{n\geq 0}(-1)^{n}R_{2n},\qquad T_{2}=\sum_{n\geq 0}(-1)^{n}R_{2n+1}.

(8.11)

Moreover, for
a\in\{0,1,2\}

\sum_{n\geq a}\|R_{n}f\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0},1/10}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{a},\qquad\sum_{n\geq a}\|R_{n}f\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1},1/10}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{a+1}.

(8.12)

This is similar to
[
37
, Lemma B.3]
, and we sketch the proof in subsection
8.2
below.

Step 3.
We can return now to the proof of Proposition
3.1
. For
\alpha\in[-2,2]
let
\mathcal{E}^{\alpha}
denote the space defined by the norm

\|f\|_{\mathcal{E}^{\alpha}}:=A^{-1}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}+\alpha,1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-1}\|f\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}+\alpha,1/2}}.

The Cauchy integral formula applied to the analytic function
F_{f}
defined in (
8.3
) and the limit in (
8.5
) show that

\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}^{2}=I\text{ on }\mathcal{E}^{-2}.

(8.13)

We are now ready to define the conjugate function
\psi
. Indeed, recall that
\|T_{1}\|_{\mathcal{E}^{0}\to\mathcal{E}^{0}}\leq 1/2
(due to (
8.11
)–(
8.12
)), and define
\psi\in\mathcal{E}^{0}
such that

(I+T_{1})\psi=(H_{0}+T_{2})\phi.

(8.14)

Clearly,
\psi
is real-valued. Let
P:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})(-i\phi+\psi)
and notice that
(I+\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})P=0
, as a consequence
of (
8.13
). Moreover,
\Re P=0
, as a consequence of (
8.14
). Therefore
P=0
, using again the fact that
T_{1}
is a contraction on
\mathcal{E}^{0}
. To summarize, this shows that if
\phi\in\mathcal{E}^{0}
is a real-valued function then there is a unique real-valued function
\psi\in\mathcal{E}^{0}
such that

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})(\phi+i\psi)=0.

(8.15)

Moreover, the function
F:\Omega^{\#}\to\mathbb{C}
,

F(z):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(\phi+i\psi)^{\#}(\beta)\gamma^{\prime}(\beta)}{z-\gamma(\beta)}\,d\beta

(8.16)

in a bounded analytic function in
\Omega^{\#}
, satisfying
F(z+2\pi R)=F(z)
, which extends to a
C^{1}
function in
\overline{\Omega^{\#}}

with the property that
F(x+ih^{\#}(x))=(\phi+i\psi)^{\#}(x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

We decompose now
F=G+iH
and define

v_{1}:=\partial_{x}G=\partial_{y}H,\qquad v_{2}:=\partial_{y}G=-\partial_{x}H.

Notice that
G(x+ih^{\#}(x))=\phi^{\#}(x)
and
H(x+ih^{\#}(x))=\psi^{\#}(x)
. Thus

-\partial_{x}\psi^{\#}=\partial_{y}G(x+ih^{\#}(x))-\partial_{x}h^{\#}(x)\partial_{x}G(x+ih^{\#}(x))=(G(h)\phi)^{\#}(x).

Let
\widetilde{V}(x):=v_{1}(x+ih^{\#}(x))
and
\widetilde{B}(x):=v_{2}(x+ih^{\#}(x))
. Taking derivatives (and dropping the superscripts
\#
since the functions involved are all
2\pi R
-periodic on
\mathbb{R}
), we have
\partial_{x}\phi=\widetilde{V}+h_{x}\widetilde{B}
and
-\partial_{x}\psi=\widetilde{B}-h_{x}\widetilde{V}
, where
h_{x}=\partial_{x}h
. Therefore

\widetilde{V}=\frac{\partial_{x}\phi+h_{x}\partial_{x}\psi}{1+h_{x}^{2}}=V,\qquad\widetilde{B}=\frac{h_{x}\partial_{x}\phi-\partial_{x}\psi}{1+h_{x}^{2}}=B,

(8.17)

where
V,B
are defined in (
3.3
). In addition, the definition (
8.2
) shows that

\partial_{x}\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}f=\gamma_{x}\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}(\partial_{x}f/\gamma_{x}),

where
\gamma_{x}=\partial_{x}\gamma=1+ih_{x}
. Therefore the identity (
8.15
) gives

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})\Big(\frac{\partial_{x}\phi+i\partial_{x}\psi}{\gamma_{x}}\Big)=0.

Notice also that
\partial_{x}\phi+i\partial_{x}\psi=(1+ih_{x})(V-iB)
, as a consequence of (
8.17
). Therefore

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})\big(V-iB\big)=0.

To summarize, we proved the identities

G(h)\phi=-\partial_{x}\psi,\qquad V=\frac{\partial_{x}\phi+h_{x}\partial_{x}\psi}{1+h_{x}^{2}},\qquad B=\frac{h_{x}\partial_{x}\phi-\partial_{x}\psi}{1+h_{x}^{2}},

(8.18)

and

\partial_{x}\phi=V+h_{x}B,\qquad-\partial_{x}\psi=B-h_{x}V,\qquad(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})\big(V-iB\big)=0.

(8.19)

Using now the definition (
8.14
), for any
g\in\{\partial_{x}\phi,\partial_{x}\psi\}
,

A^{-1}\|g\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-1,-1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-1}\|g\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-1,-1/2}}\lesssim 1.

(8.20)

Using also Lemma
2.4
, for any
f\in\{\partial_{x}\phi,\partial_{x}\psi,V,B\}
we have

A^{-1}\|f\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}}+\varepsilon^{-1}\|f\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-1}}\lesssim 1.

(8.21)

Step 4.
It remains to prove the paralinearization identities in part (iii), in particular the quartic bounds (
3.8
) for the remainders
G_{\geq 4}
defined according to (
3.5
). The main issue is to avoid loss of derivative, so we will prove first that

\big\|P_{\geq 0}G_{\geq 4}\big\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{3}\quad\text{ and }\quad\big\|P_{\geq 0}G_{\geq 4}\big\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}.

(8.22)

Taking real and imaginary parts of the identity
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})\big(V-iB\big)=0
we have

V-H_{0}B=-T_{1}V+T_{2}B,\qquad-B-H_{0}V=T_{2}V+T_{1}B.

(8.23)

Using also the formulas
-\partial_{x}\psi=B-h_{x}V
, see (
8.19
), and
|\partial_{x}|\phi=-H_{0}(\partial_{x}\phi)
, we have

\begin{split}|\partial_{x}|\phi-G(h)\phi&=-H_{0}(\partial_{x}\phi)-B+h_{x}V\\
&=T_{2}V+T_{1}B+h_{x}V-H_{0}(h_{x}B).\end{split}

(8.24)

Let

D:=T_{2}V+T_{1}B=-H_{0}V-B.

(8.25)

With
G_{2}
defined as in (
3.6
), let

\begin{split}G_{\geq 3}&:=G(h)\phi-|\partial_{x}|\phi+|\partial_{x}|T_{B}h+\partial_{x}T_{V}h-G_{2}=I+II,\\
I:&=-h_{x}V-H_{0}(h_{x}H_{0}V)-|\partial_{x}|T_{H_{0}V}h+\partial_{x}T_{V}h-G_{2}+R_{0}(H_{0}V),\\
II:&=-D-R_{0}(H_{0}V)-H_{0}(h_{x}D)-|\partial_{x}|T_{D}h.\end{split}

(8.26)

The multiplier of the Hilbert transform
H_{0}
is
\eta\to i\,\mathrm{sgn}(\eta)
. Using the definitions and (
8.45
), we write

\widehat{I}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{V}(\xi-\eta)p(\xi,\eta)-\widehat{G_{2}}(\xi)

where

\begin{split}p(\xi,\eta):&=-i\eta+i\eta\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta)-i|\xi|\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta)\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)+i\xi\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)\\
&-i(\xi-\eta)[1-\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta)]\\
&=-i\xi[1-\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)][1-\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta)].\end{split}

Using also the formula
V=\partial_{x}\phi-h_{x}B
(see (
8.19
)), we have

\widehat{I}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\mathcal{F}(V-\partial_{x}\phi)(\xi-\eta)p(\xi,\eta)=\frac{-1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\mathcal{F}(h_{x}B)(\xi-\eta)p(\xi,\eta).

(8.27)

Using the definitions we calculate now

\begin{split}II&=-T_{2}V-T_{1}B-R_{0}(H_{0}V)-[H_{0}(h_{x}T_{1}B)+|\partial_{x}|T_{T_{1}B}h]-[H_{0}(h_{x}T_{2}V)+|\partial_{x}|T_{T_{2}V}h]\\
&=-R_{1}V-R_{0}B-R_{0}(H_{0}V)-[H_{0}(h_{x}R_{0}B)+|\partial_{x}|T_{R_{0}B}h]+G_{\geq 4}^{1},\end{split}

(8.28)

where

\begin{split}G_{\geq 4}^{1}&:=-(T_{2}-R_{1})V-(T_{1}-R_{0})B-[H_{0}(h_{x}(T_{1}-R_{0})B)+|\partial_{x}|T_{(T_{1}-R_{0})B}h]\\
&-[H_{0}(h_{x}T_{2}V)+|\partial_{x}|T_{T_{2}V}h].\end{split}

(8.29)

Notice that, for any
F\in H^{2}
,

\mathcal{F}\big\{H_{0}(h_{x}F)+|\partial_{x}|T_{F}h\big\}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{F}(\xi-\eta)\,\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)[\xi\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)-\eta].

(8.30)

Assuming now that
F\in\{(T_{1}-R_{0})B,\,T_{2}V\}
, we notice that
\|F\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0},1/10}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{2}
and
\|F\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1},1/10}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{3}
, as a consequence of (
8.12
). Using the formula (
8.30
) it is easy to see that

\begin{split}&\big\|P_{\geq 0}\big\{H_{0}(h_{x}F)+|\partial_{x}|T_{F}h\big\}\big\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{3},\\
&\big\|P_{\geq 0}\big\{H_{0}(h_{x}F)+|\partial_{x}|T_{F}h\big\}\big\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4},\end{split}

for
G\in\{(T_{1}-R_{0})B,\,T_{2}V\}
. Using again (
8.12
)
together with the bounds (
8.21
) we see that the terms
P_{\geq 0}(T_{2}-R_{1})V
and
P_{\geq 0}(T_{1}-R_{0})V

satisfy similar quartic
H^{N_{0}}
bounds.
Therefore
P_{\geq 0}G_{\geq 4}^{1}
is an acceptable quartic error,

\big\|P_{\geq 0}G_{\geq 4}^{1}\big\|_{H^{N_{0}}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{3}\quad\text{ and }\quad\big\|P_{\geq 0}G_{\geq 4}^{1}\big\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}.

(8.31)

We examine now the main terms in the right-hand side of (
8.28
). Using (
8.23
) we write

\begin{split}&-R_{1}V-R_{0}B-R_{0}(H_{0}V)=-R_{1}(H_{0}B)+R_{0}(R_{0}B)+G_{\geq 4}^{2},\\
&G_{\geq 4}^{2}:=R_{1}(T_{1}V-T_{2}B)+R_{0}(T_{2}V)+R_{0}(T_{1}-R_{0})B.\end{split}

Using (
8.12
) and (
8.21
) as before, we see that
P_{\geq 0}G_{\geq 4}^{2}
is an acceptable quartic error, satisfying bounds similar to (
8.31
). Moreover, using (
8.47
), (
8.45
), and (
8.30
), we write the cubic terms in
II
in the form

\begin{split}-\mathcal{F}[R_{1}(H_{0}B)](\xi)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{\eta,\rho\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{h}(\rho)\widehat{B}(\xi-\eta-\rho)p_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho),\\
\mathcal{F}[R_{0}(R_{0}B)](\xi)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{\eta,\rho\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{h}(\rho)\widehat{B}(\xi-\eta-\rho)p_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho),\\
-\mathcal{F}[H_{0}(h_{x}R_{0}B)+|\partial_{x}|T_{R_{0}B}h](\xi)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{\eta,\rho\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{h}(\rho)\widehat{B}(\xi-\eta-\rho)p_{3}(\xi,\eta,\rho),\end{split}

where

\begin{split}p_{1}(\xi,\eta,\rho)&:=(1/2)|\xi-\eta-\rho|[|\xi|+|\xi-\eta-\rho|-|\xi-\eta|-|\xi-\rho|],\\
p_{2}(\xi,\eta,\rho)&:=[\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)-\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta)](\xi-\eta)[\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta)-\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta-\rho)](\xi-\eta-\rho),\\
p_{3}(\xi,\eta,\rho)&:=-\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)[\xi\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)-\eta][\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta)-\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta-\rho)](\xi-\eta-\rho).\end{split}

Recalling also (
8.27
), we have

\begin{split}\widehat{I}(\xi)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{\eta,\rho\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{h}(\rho)\widehat{B}(\xi-\eta-\rho)p_{4}(\xi,\eta,\rho),\\
p_{4}(\xi,\eta,\rho)&:=-\xi\rho[1-\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)][1-\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta)].\end{split}

Using also (
8.26
) and symmetrizing the multipliers in
\eta
and
\rho
, we get

\mathcal{F}\{G_{\geq 3}\}(\xi)=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{\eta,\rho\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)\widehat{h}(\rho)\widehat{B}(\xi-\eta-\rho)p^{\ast}(\xi,\eta,\rho)+\mathcal{F}\{G_{\geq 4}^{1}\}(\xi)+\mathcal{F}\{G^{2}_{\geq 4}\}(\xi),

where

\begin{split}p^{\ast}&(\xi,\eta,\rho):=\frac{1}{2}|\xi|[1-\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)][|\xi-\eta|-|\xi-\eta-\rho|-\rho\,\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)]\\
&+\frac{1}{2}|\xi|[1-\chi(\xi-\rho,\rho)][|\xi-\rho|-|\xi-\eta-\rho|-\eta\,\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)]+\frac{1}{2}\big[|\xi||\xi-\eta-\rho|-\xi(\xi-\eta-\rho)\big].\end{split}

We notice now that the symbol
p^{\ast}
is symmetric in
\eta
and
\rho
and smoothing, in the sense that
p^{\ast}(\xi,\eta,\rho)=0
if
|\eta|+|\rho|\leq 2^{-40}|\xi|
or if
|\eta|+|\xi-\eta-\rho|\leq 2^{-40}|\xi|
or if
|\rho|+|\xi-\eta-\rho|\leq 2^{-40}|\xi|
. Moreover, for any
k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

\big\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big[p^{\ast}(\xi,\eta,\rho)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{\leq k_{1}}(\eta)\varphi_{\leq k_{2}}(\rho)\varphi_{\leq k_{3}}(\xi-\eta-\rho)\big]\big\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{3})}\lesssim 2^{k}2^{\max(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})},

These bounds follow easily from the explicit formula of
p^{\ast}
above. Therefore, using (
8.19
) and (
8.14
) we may replace
B
with
-\partial_{x}\psi
and then with
|\partial_{x}|\phi
at the expenses of acceptable quartic errors. The desired bounds (
8.22
) follow.

Step 5.
Finally, we show that the low frequencies of the quartic remainders satisfy

\|P_{\leq 0}G_{\geq 4}\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0},-3/4}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{3},\qquad\|P_{\leq 0}G_{\geq 4}\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1},-3/4}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}.

(8.32)

The proof is easier than before, but we need to write the remainders
G_{\geq 4}
differently, as perfect derivatives. We start from the definition (
8.14
), which we rewrite in the form

(I+R_{0})\psi=(H_{0}+R_{1})\phi+(T_{2}-R_{1})\phi-(T_{1}-R_{0})\psi.

We apply the operator
-\partial_{x}(I-R_{0}+R_{0}^{2})
to both sides, so

\begin{split}-\partial_{x}\psi&=-\partial_{x}(I-R_{0}+R_{0}^{2})H_{0}\phi-\partial_{x}R_{1}\phi+G_{\geq 4}^{4},\\
G_{\geq 4}^{4}&:=\partial_{x}(R_{0}-R_{0}^{2})R_{1}\phi-\partial_{x}(I-R_{0}+R_{0}^{2})(T_{2}-R_{1})\phi\\
&+\partial_{x}(I-R_{0}+R_{0}^{2})(T_{1}-R_{0})\psi+\partial_{x}R_{0}^{3}\psi.\end{split}

(8.33)

Recalling now that
G(h)\phi=-\partial_{x}\psi
we have

\begin{split}G(h)\phi&-\big\{|\partial_{x}|\omega-\partial_{x}(T_{V}h)+G_{2}(\phi,h)+G_{3}(\phi,h,h)\big\}\\
&=\big\{\partial_{x}R_{0}H_{0}\phi+|\partial_{x}|T_{B}h+\partial_{x}T_{V}h-G_{2}(\phi,h)\big\}\\
&-\big\{\partial_{x}R_{0}^{2}H_{0}\phi+\partial_{x}R_{1}\phi+G_{3}(\phi,h,h)\big\}+G_{\geq 4}^{4}.\end{split}

(8.34)

It follows from the formulas in (
8.33
) and Lemma
8.1
that

\|G_{\geq 4}^{4}\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-1,-3/4}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{3},\qquad\|G_{\geq 4}^{4}\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-1,-3/4}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4},

(8.35)

and

\begin{split}&G(h)\phi=|\partial_{x}|\phi+\partial_{x}R_{0}H_{0}\phi+[G(h)\phi]_{\geq 3},\\
&\|[G(h)\phi]_{\geq 3}\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-1,-3/4}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{2},\qquad\|[G(h)\phi]_{\geq 3}\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-1,-3/4}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{3}.\end{split}

(8.36)

Using the identities (
8.18
) and Lemma
2.4
(i) it follows that

\begin{split}&V=\partial_{x}\phi-\partial_{x}h|\partial_{x}|\phi+V_{\geq 3},\\
&B=|\partial_{x}|\phi+\partial_{x}R_{0}H_{0}\phi+\partial_{x}h\partial_{x}\phi+B_{\geq 3},\end{split}

(8.37)

where the cubic remainders
V_{\geq 3}
and
B_{\geq 3}
satisfy

\|V_{\geq 3}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}}+\|B_{\geq 3}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{2},\qquad\|V_{\geq 3}\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-1}}+\|B_{\geq 3}\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-1}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{3}.

(8.38)

We examine now the formula (
8.34
). The quadratic term in the right-hand side is

\partial_{x}R_{0}H_{0}\phi+|\partial_{x}|T_{|\partial_{x}|\phi}h+\partial_{x}T_{\partial_{x}\phi}h-G_{2}(\phi,h),

which is identically equal to
0
due to the formulas (
8.45
) and (
3.6
). The cubic term is

|\partial_{x}|T_{\partial_{x}R_{0}H_{0}\phi+\partial_{x}h\partial_{x}\phi}h+\partial_{x}T_{-\partial_{x}h|\partial_{x}|\phi}h-\big\{\partial_{x}R_{0}^{2}H_{0}\phi+\partial_{x}R_{1}\phi+G_{3}(\phi,h,h)\big\},

due to the expansion (
8.37
). An explicit calculation using the formulas (
3.7
), (
8.42
), (
8.44
), and (
8.47
) shows that this expression vanishes identically as well. The remaining terms
|\partial_{x}|T_{B_{\geq}3}h
and
\partial_{x}T_{V_{\geq}3}h
are acceptable quartic remainders, satisfying bounds similar to (
8.35
) as a consequence of (
8.38
). The desired bounds (
8.32
) follow using (
8.34
) and (
8.35
).

8.2.
Proof of Lemma
8.1

The identities (
8.10
)-(
8.11
) follow directly from definitions. The main point is to prove the bounds (
8.12
).

Step 1.
We rewrite first the operators
R_{n}
in the form

(R_{n}f)(\alpha)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{h^{\#}(\alpha)-h^{\#}(\alpha+\rho)+\rho{h^{\prime}}^{\#}(\alpha+\rho)}{\rho^{2}}\Big(\frac{h^{\#}(\alpha+\rho)-h^{\#}(\alpha)}{\rho}\Big)^{n}f^{\#}(\alpha+\rho)\,d\rho.

Notice that

\frac{h^{\#}(\alpha+\rho)-h^{\#}(\alpha)}{\rho}=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widehat{h}(\eta)e^{i\eta\alpha}\frac{e^{i\eta\rho}-1}{\rho}.

Therefore, we can take the Fourier transform on
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
and rewrite

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}\big[R_{n}f\big](\xi)=\frac{1}{\pi(2\pi R)^{n+1}}&\sum_{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\widetilde{M}_{n+1}(\xi;\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1})\\
&\times\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta_{1}-\ldots-\eta_{n+1})\widehat{h}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\widehat{h}(\eta_{n+1}),\end{split}

(8.39)

where

\begin{split}\widetilde{M}_{n+1}(\xi;\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{e^{-i\xi\rho}}{\rho}\frac{e^{i\eta_{n+1}\rho}-1-i\eta_{n+1}\rho}{\rho}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\frac{e^{i\eta_{l}\rho}-1}{\rho}\,d\rho.\end{split}

(8.40)

As in
[
37
, Subsection B.2]
we can symmetrize in the variables
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1}
to get more favorable formulas. Indeed, letting

M_{n+1}(\xi;\underline{\eta}):=\frac{-i(\xi-\eta_{1}-\ldots-\eta_{n+1})}{n+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-i\xi\rho}\prod_{l=1}^{n+1}\frac{e^{i\eta_{l}\rho}-1}{\rho}\,d\rho,

(8.41)

where
\underline{\eta}:=(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1})
, it follows by symmetrization from (
8.39
) that

\begin{split}\widehat{R_{n}f}(\xi)=\frac{1}{\pi(2\pi R)^{n+1}}\sum_{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}M_{n+1}(\xi;\underline{\eta})&\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta_{1}-\ldots-\eta_{n+1})\widehat{h}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\widehat{h}(\eta_{n+1}).\end{split}

(8.42)

Step 2.
We consider first the operators
R_{0}
and
R_{1}
, for which we can derive explicit formulas. Recall the general (formal) identities

\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-i\xi x}\,dx=2\pi\delta_{0}(\xi),\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-i\xi x}\frac{1}{x}\,dx=-i\pi\,\mathrm{sgn}(\xi),\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{e^{-i\xi x}-1}{x^{2}}\,dx=-\pi\,|\xi|,

(8.43)

for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. Using these formulas, the symbol
M_{1}(\xi;\eta_{1})
can be calculated easily,

M_{1}(\xi;\eta_{1})=\pi(\xi-\eta_{1})\big[\,\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)-\,\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta_{1})\big].

(8.44)

Therefore

\widehat{R_{0}f}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\big[\,\mathrm{sgn}(\xi)-\,\mathrm{sgn}(\xi-\eta_{1})\big](\xi-\eta_{1})\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta_{1})\widehat{h}(\eta_{1}).

(8.45)

The main observation is that the multiplier of the operator
R_{0}
vanishes when
|\eta_{1}|\leq|\xi|
. Recalling that
\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}f\|_{\dot{W}^{1,0}}\lesssim 1
we can use Lemma
2.1
to estimate, for
p\in\{2,\infty\}
,

\begin{split}\|P_{k}R_{0}f\|_{L^{p}}&\lesssim\sum_{k_{2}+8\geq\max(k,k_{1})}2^{k_{1}}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{1}}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{2}}h\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\sum_{k_{2}+8\geq k}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{2}}h\|_{L^{p}},\end{split}

for any
k\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
. Thus

\|R_{0}f\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0},1/10}}\lesssim A,\qquad\|R_{0}f\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1},1/10}}\lesssim\varepsilon.

(8.46)

We can analyze similarly the operator
R_{1}
. Using (
8.43
), its multiplier is

M_{2}(\xi;\eta_{1},\eta_{2})=\frac{i\pi}{2}(\xi-\eta_{1}-\eta_{2})\big[|\xi-\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}|+|\xi|-|\xi-\eta_{1}|-|\xi-\eta_{2}|\big].

(8.47)

As before, we observe that
M_{2}(\xi;\eta_{1},\eta_{2})=0
if
|\eta_{1}|+|\eta_{2}|\leq|\xi|
. Moreover, it is easy to see that

\Big\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big[M_{2}(\xi;\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi-\eta_{1}-\eta_{2})\varphi_{k}(\xi)\big]\Big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim 2^{k_{3}}2^{\min(k_{1},k_{2})}.

Therefore, using Lemma
2.1
, the assumption
\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}f\|_{\dot{W}^{1,0}}\lesssim 1
, and the bounds (
3.2
) on
h
,

\begin{split}\|P_{k}R_{1}f\|_{L^{p}}&\lesssim\sum_{k_{2}+10\geq\max(k,k_{1},k_{3})}2^{k_{1}+k_{3}}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{1}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{2}}h\|_{L^{p}}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{3}}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}\sum_{k_{2}+10\geq k}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{2}}h\|_{L^{p}},\end{split}

for
p\in\{2,\infty\}
and any
k\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
. Therefore

\|R_{1}f\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0},1/10}}\lesssim A\varepsilon,\qquad\|R_{1}f\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1},1/10}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

(8.48)

Step 3.
To analyze the operators
R_{n}
,
n\geq 2
, we prove in fact the slightly stronger bounds (
8.52
) below, which clearly suffice to complete the proof of the desired estimates (
8.12
).

Lemma 8.2
.

For any
n\geq 1
and
\underline{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1})\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}^{n+1}
we define the operators
F_{n;\underline{k}}
by

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}\big(F_{n;\underline{k}}(f_{\underline{k}})\big)(\xi):=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{n+1}}&\sum_{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}M^{\prime}_{n+1}(\xi;\underline{\eta})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta_{1})\ldots\varphi_{k_{n+1}}(\eta_{n+1})\\
&\times\widehat{f_{\underline{k}}}(\xi-\eta_{1}-\ldots-\eta_{n+1})\widehat{h}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\widehat{h}(\eta_{n+1}),\end{split}

(8.49)

where

M^{\prime}_{n+1}(\xi;\underline{\eta}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-i\xi\rho}\prod_{l=1}^{n+1}\frac{e^{i\eta_{l}\rho}-1}{\rho}\,d\rho.

(8.50)

Assume that
h
satisfies the bounds in (
3.2
) and the functions
f_{\underline{k}}
satisfy the uniform bounds

\|f_{\underline{k}}\|_{\widetilde{W}^{1/2}}\leq 1.

(8.51)

Then there is a constant
C_{0}\geq 1
such that if
n\geq 2
,
p\in\{2,\infty\}
, and
l\in\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}
then

\sum_{\underline{k}\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}^{n+1}}\big\|P_{l}[F_{n;\underline{k}}(f_{\underline{k}})]\big\|_{L^{p}}\leq(C_{0}{\varepsilon})^{n}\big(\sum_{l^{\prime}\geq l-6n}\|P_{l^{\prime}}h\|_{L^{p}}\big).

(8.52)

Proof.

Notice that

M^{\prime}_{n+1}(\xi,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1})=0\qquad\text{ if }\qquad\eta_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot\eta_{n+1}=0.

(8.53)

Taking partial derivatives in
\underline{\eta}
it follows that

M^{\prime}_{n+1}(\xi,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1})=0\qquad\text{ if }\qquad|\eta_{1}|+\ldots+|\eta_{n+1}|\leq|\xi|.

(8.54)

Using the Fourier inversion formula we rewrite, in the physical space,

F_{n,\underline{k}}(f_{\underline{k}})(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{n+1}}f_{\underline{k}}(x-\rho)\prod_{l=1}^{n+1}P^{\prime}_{k_{l}}h(x-\rho-y_{l})\cdot\prod_{l=1}^{n+1}L_{k_{l}}(y_{l},\rho)\,d\underline{y}d\rho,

(8.55)

where, with
K_{k}
defined as in (
2.4
),

L_{k}(y,\rho):=\frac{1}{2\pi R}\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}\varphi_{k}(\eta)e^{iy\eta}\frac{e^{i\eta\rho}-1}{\rho}=\frac{K_{k}(y+\rho)-K_{k}(y)}{\rho}.

(8.56)

Using the identities (
2.5
) it is easy to see that

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}}\big|L_{k}(y,\rho)\big|\,dy\lesssim\min\big(|\rho|^{-1},2^{k}\big).

(8.57)

Therefore, assuming that
\underline{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n+1}
and letting
\widetilde{k}_{1}\leq\ldots\leq\widetilde{k}_{n+1}
denote the nondecreasing rearrangement of the integers
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{n+1}}\Big|\prod_{l=1}^{n+1}L_{k_{l}}(y_{l},\rho)\Big|\,d\underline{y}d\rho\leq C^{n+1}2^{\widetilde{k}_{1}+\ldots+\widetilde{k}_{n}}(1+|\widetilde{k}_{n+1}-\widetilde{k}_{n}|),

(8.58)

for some constant
C\geq 1
(which is allowed to change from now on from line to line).

Therefore, using (
8.55
), for any
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n+1}
and
p\in\{2,\infty\}
,

\big\|F_{n,\underline{k}}(f_{\underline{k}})\big\|_{L^{p}}\leq C^{n+1}(1+|\widetilde{k}_{n+1}-\widetilde{k}_{n}|)\cdot\|f_{\underline{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P^{\prime}_{\widetilde{k}_{n+1}}h\|_{L^{p}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\big(2^{\widetilde{k}_{l}}\|P^{\prime}_{\widetilde{k}_{l}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}\big).

(8.59)

We notice that the bounds (
8.59
) suffice to prove (
8.52
) if
l\leq 0
, due to the identity (
8.54
). On the other hand, if
l\geq 0
then these bounds suffice to control the part of the sum in (
8.52
) corresponding to
P_{>\widetilde{k}_{n+1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}}
, and also the part of the sum corresponding to multi-indices
\underline{k}
for which
\widetilde{k}_{n}\geq\widetilde{k}_{n+1}-6n
. After these reductions for (
8.52
) it remains to prove that

\sum_{k_{2},\ldots,k_{n+1}\leq k_{1}-6n}\Big\|F_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}})\Big\|_{L^{p}}\leq(C_{1}\varepsilon)^{n}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{1}}h\|_{L^{p}},

(8.60)

for
p\in\{2,\infty\}
,
k_{1}\geq-6
, and some constant
C_{1}\geq 1
.

The bounds (
8.59
) cannot be used directly to prove (
8.60
), due to derivative loss. We use instead induction over
n
. The case
n=1
follows from our earlier discussion of the operator
R_{1}
. To prove the induction step we decompose

L_{k_{n+1}}(y_{n+1},\rho)=\widetilde{L}_{k_{n+1}}(y_{n+1},\rho)+K^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}(y_{n+1}),

where
K^{\prime}_{k}(y)=(\partial_{y}K_{k})(y)
and

\widetilde{L}_{k}(y,\rho):=\frac{K_{k}(y+\rho)-K_{k}(y)-\rho K^{\prime}_{k}(y)}{\rho}.

Then we decompose

F_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}})=F^{1}_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}})+F^{2}_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}}),

where

F^{1}_{n,\underline{k}}(g)(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}^{n+1}}g(x-\rho)\prod_{l=1}^{n+1}P^{\prime}_{k_{l}}h(x-\rho-y_{l})\cdot\widetilde{L}_{k_{n+1}}(y_{n+1},\rho)\prod_{l=1}^{n}L_{k_{l}}(y_{l},\rho)\,d\underline{y}d\rho,

(8.61)

and

F^{2}_{n,\underline{k}}(g)(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}^{n+1}}g(x-\rho)\prod_{l=1}^{n+1}P^{\prime}_{k_{l}}h(x-\rho-y_{l})\cdot K^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}(y_{n+1})\prod_{l=1}^{n}L_{k_{l}}(y_{l},\rho)\,d\underline{y}d\rho.

(8.62)

Using (
2.5
) it is easy to see that the kernels
\widetilde{L}_{k}
satisfy the better estimates

\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}}\big|\widetilde{L}_{k}(y,\rho)\big|\,dy\lesssim 2^{k}|\rho|\min\big(|\rho|^{-1},2^{k}\big),

compare with (
8.57
). Using also (
8.57
) it follows that if
k_{2},\ldots,k_{n+1}\in(-\infty,k_{1}-6n]
then

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}^{n+1}}\Big|\widetilde{L}_{k_{n+1}}(y_{n+1},\rho)\prod_{l=1}^{n}L_{k_{l}}(y_{l},\rho)\Big|\,d\underline{y}d\rho\leq C^{n+1}(1+|\widetilde{k}_{n}-\widetilde{k}_{n-1}|)2^{\widetilde{k}_{1}+\ldots+\widetilde{k}_{n}},

where
\widetilde{k}_{1}\leq\ldots\leq\widetilde{k}_{n}
denote the nondecreasing rearrangement of the integers
k_{2},\ldots,k_{n+1}
. This is slightly stronger than the inequality (
8.58
). Therefore

\Big\|F^{1}_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}})\Big\|_{L^{p}}\leq C^{n+1}(1+|\widetilde{k}_{n}-\widetilde{k}_{n-1}|)\cdot\|f_{\underline{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{1}}h\|_{L^{p}}\prod_{l=2}^{n+1}\big(2^{k_{l}}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{l}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}\big).

Therefore, for
p\in\{2,\infty\}
and
k_{1}\geq-6
,

\sum_{k_{2},\ldots,k_{n+1}\leq k_{1}-6n}\Big\|F^{1}_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}})\Big\|_{L^{p}}\leq(C\varepsilon)^{n}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{1}}h\|_{L^{p}}.

(8.63)

We estimate now the contributions of the terms
F^{2}_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}})
. We integrate the variable
y_{n+1}
in the defining formula (
8.62
). Let

g^{k_{n+1}}_{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})}(z):=P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1})}(z)\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}}P^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}h(z-y_{n+1})K^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}(y_{n+1})\,dy_{n+1}.

Using Lemma
2.4
(i) and the assumptions (
8.51
) and (
3.2
), it is easy to see that

\|g^{k_{n+1}}_{(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})}\|_{\widetilde{W}^{1/2}}\lesssim\varepsilon 2^{-|k_{n+1}|/2},

for any
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}
as in (
8.60
). Since

F^{2}_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}})=F_{n-1,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n+6}g_{\underline{k}}),

the induction hypothesis shows that

\sum_{k_{2},\ldots,k_{n+1}\leq k_{1}-6n}\Big\|F^{2}_{n,\underline{k}}(P_{\leq k_{1}-6n}f_{\underline{k}})\Big\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\sum_{k_{n+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(C_{1}\varepsilon)^{n-1}\varepsilon 2^{-|k_{n+1}|/2}\|P^{\prime}_{k_{1}}h\|_{L^{p}}.

The desired bounds (
8.60
) follow, using also (
8.63
) provided that
C_{1}
is sufficiently large.
∎

8.3.
Proof of Lemma
3.2

Using (
8.45
) we have

\partial_{x}R_{0}H_{0}\phi=-\partial_{x}(h\partial_{x}\phi)-|\partial_{x}|(h|\partial_{x}|\phi).

(8.64)

The identities (
3.13
) and the bounds (
3.14
) for the cubic remainders
B_{\geq 3}
and
V_{\geq 3}
follow from (
8.37
)–(
8.38
). The identities (
3.12
) and the bounds (
3.14
) for the remainders
\dot{h}_{\geq 3}
and
\dot{\phi}_{\geq 3}
follow from the evolution equations (
1.4
), the identities and inequalities (
8.36
), and Lemma
2.4
(i).

The remaining conclusions of Lemma
3.2
follow easily from the following:

Lemma 8.3
.

Assume
(h,\phi)\in C([T_{1},T_{2}]:H^{N_{0}}\times\dot{H}^{N_{0},1/2})
is a solution of the system (
1.4
) satisfying the bounds (
3.11
). We define
\psi\in\mathcal{E}^{0}
as in (
8.14
). Then

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\psi=-H_{0}h+\dot{\psi}_{2}+\dot{\psi}_{\geq 3},\\
&\dot{\psi}_{2}:=\big[|\partial_{x}|\phi\partial_{x}\phi-h\partial_{x}h\big]-H_{0}\Big[\frac{1}{2}(|\partial_{x}|\phi)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{x}\phi)^{2}-h|\partial_{x}|h\Big],\\
&\|\dot{\psi}_{\geq 3}\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-1,1/10}}\lesssim A[\varepsilon(t)]^{2},\qquad\|\dot{\psi}_{\geq 3}\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-1,1/10}}\lesssim[\varepsilon(t)]^{3}.\end{split}

(8.65)

Moreover

\begin{split}&\partial^{2}_{t}\psi=-\partial_{x}\phi+\ddot{\psi}_{2}+\ddot{\psi}_{\geq 3},\\
&\ddot{\psi}_{2}:=-\partial_{x}h|\partial_{x}|\phi-|\partial_{x}|h\partial_{x}\phi+2H_{0}\big[\partial_{x}h\partial_{x}\phi+|\partial_{x}|h|\partial_{x}|\phi\big],\\
&\|\ddot{\psi}_{\geq 3}\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-2,1/10}}\lesssim A[\varepsilon(t)]^{2},\qquad\|\ddot{\psi}_{\geq 3}\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-2,1/10}}\lesssim[\varepsilon(t)]^{3}.\end{split}

(8.66)

Assuming this lemma, we can easily complete the proof of Lemma
3.2
. Indeed, the identities (
8.18
) show that

\partial_{t}V=\frac{\partial^{2}_{xt}\phi+\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}\psi+\partial^{2}_{xt}h\partial_{x}\psi}{1+(\partial_{x}h)^{2}}-\frac{2\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}h(\partial_{x}\phi+\partial_{x}h\partial_{x}\psi)}{[1+(\partial_{x}h)^{2}]^{2}}

and

\partial_{t}B=\frac{-\partial^{2}_{xt}\psi+\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}\phi+\partial^{2}_{xt}h\partial_{x}\phi}{1+(\partial_{x}h)^{2}}-\frac{2\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}h(-\partial_{x}\psi+\partial_{x}h\partial_{x}\phi)}{[1+(\partial_{x}h)^{2}]^{2}}.

The desired identities (
3.15
)–(
3.16
) follow using the expansions (
3.12
), (
8.36
), and (
8.65
), and then using Lemma
2.4
(i) to estimate the cubic remainders
\dot{B}_{\geq 3}
and
\dot{V}_{\geq 3}
as claimed in (
3.18
).

We take one more time derivative to calculate

\begin{split}\partial^{2}_{t}B&=\frac{-\partial^{3}_{xtt}\psi+2\partial^{2}_{xt}h\partial^{2}_{xt}\phi+\partial_{x}h\partial^{3}_{xtt}\phi+\partial^{3}_{xtt}h\partial_{x}\phi}{1+(\partial_{x}h)^{2}}-\frac{2\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}h(-\partial^{2}_{xt}\psi+\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}\phi+\partial^{2}_{xt}h\partial_{x}\phi)}{[1+(\partial_{x}h)^{2}]^{2}}\\
&-\frac{2(\partial^{2}_{xt}h)^{2}(-\partial_{x}\psi+\partial_{x}h\partial_{x}\phi)+2\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}h(-\partial^{2}_{xt}\psi+\partial^{2}_{xt}h\partial_{x}\phi+\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}\phi)}{[1+(\partial_{x}h)^{2}]^{2}}\\
&+\frac{8(\partial_{x}h\partial^{2}_{xt}h)^{2}(-\partial_{x}\psi+\partial_{x}h\partial_{x}\phi)}{[1+(\partial_{x}h)^{2}]^{3}}.\end{split}

We observe that only the expression in the first fraction contributes linear and quadratic terms. All the terms in the last 3 fractions are cubic and higher order, so they can be incorporated as part of the remainder
\ddot{B}_{\geq 3}
and estimated using Lemma
2.4
(i). Moreover, notice that

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}^{2}h+|\partial_{x}|h\|_{H^{N_{0}-2}}\lesssim A\varepsilon(t),\qquad\|\partial_{t}^{2}h+|\partial_{x}|h\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-2}}\lesssim[\varepsilon(t)]^{2},\\
\|\partial_{t}^{2}\phi+|\partial_{x}|\phi\|_{H^{N_{0}-2}}\lesssim A\varepsilon(t),\qquad\|\partial_{t}^{2}\phi+|\partial_{x}|\phi\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-2}}\lesssim[\varepsilon(t)]^{2},\end{split}

(8.67)

using again the main system (
1.4
) and the identities (
3.12
) and (
8.65
). We can then use these bounds and the identities (
3.12
) and (
8.66
) to extract the linear and the quadratic terms from the first fraction in the formula of
\partial_{t}^{2}B
above, and control the cubic remainder as claimed in (
3.17
)–(
3.18
). This completes the proof of Lemma
3.2
.

8.3.1.
Proof of Lemma
8.3

We start from the defining formula
(I+T_{1})\psi=(H_{0}+T_{2})\phi
and recall the main definitions (
8.11
) and (
8.41
)–(
8.42
).

Step 1.
For
n\geq 0
we define the operators
\dot{R}_{n}
,
\ddot{R}_{n,1}
, and
\ddot{R}_{n,2}
by the formulas

\begin{split}\widehat{\dot{R}_{n}f}(\xi)=\frac{1}{\pi(2\pi R)^{n+1}}\sum_{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}&M_{n+1}(\xi;\underline{\eta})\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta_{1}-\ldots-\eta_{n+1})\\
\times&\widehat{h}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\widehat{h}(\eta_{n})\cdot\widehat{(\partial_{t}h)}(\eta_{n+1}),\end{split}

(8.68)

\begin{split}\widehat{\ddot{R}_{n,1}f}(\xi)=\frac{1}{\pi(2\pi R)^{n+1}}\sum_{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}&M_{n+1}(\xi;\underline{\eta})\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta_{1}-\ldots-\eta_{n+1})\\
\times&\widehat{h}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\widehat{h}(\eta_{n})\cdot\widehat{(\partial_{t}^{2}h)}(\eta_{n+1}),\end{split}

(8.69)

\begin{split}\widehat{\ddot{R}_{n,2}f}(\xi)=\frac{1}{\pi(2\pi R)^{n+1}}\sum_{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}/R}&M_{n+1}(\xi;\underline{\eta})\widehat{f}(\xi-\eta_{1}-\ldots-\eta_{n+1})\\
\times&\widehat{h}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\widehat{h}(\eta_{n-1})\cdot\widehat{(\partial_{t}h)}(\eta_{n})\cdot\widehat{(\partial_{t}h)}(\eta_{n+1}).\end{split}

(8.70)

We claim that if
f\in H^{3}({\mathbb{T}}_{R})
satisfies
\||\partial_{x}|^{1/2}f\|_{\dot{W}^{1,0}}\leq 1
then

\sum_{n\geq a}2^{n}\|\dot{R}_{n}f\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-1,1/10}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{a},\qquad\sum_{n\geq a}2^{n}\|\dot{R}_{n}f\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-1,1/10}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{a+1}

(8.71)

and

\sum_{n\geq a}2^{n}\|\ddot{R}_{n,b}f\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-2,1/10}}\lesssim A\varepsilon^{a},\qquad\sum_{n\geq a}2^{n}\|\ddot{R}_{n,b}f\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-2,1/10}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{a+1}

(8.72)

for
a\in\{0,1\}
and
b\in\{1,2\}
. These are similar to the bounds (
8.12
) in Lemma
8.1
once we notice that the functions
\partial_{t}h
and
\partial_{t}^{2}h
satisfy estimates of the form

\|\partial_{t}^{l}h(t)\|_{H^{N_{0}-l}}\lesssim A,\qquad\|\partial_{t}^{l}h(t)\|_{\widetilde{W}^{N_{1}-l}}\lesssim\varepsilon,\qquad l\in\{1,2\},

for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
(see (
3.12
), (
3.14
), and (
8.67
)).

Step 2.
We prove now the expansions in Lemma
8.3
. It follows from (
8.14
) that

(I+T_{1})\dot{\psi}=(H_{0}+T_{2})\dot{\phi}+\dot{T}_{2}\phi-\dot{T}_{1}\psi,

(8.73)

where
\dot{\psi}:=\partial_{t}\psi
,
\dot{\phi}:=\partial_{t}\phi
, and

\dot{T}_{1}:=\sum_{n\geq 0}(-1)^{n}(2n+1)\dot{R}_{2n},\qquad\dot{T}_{2}:=\sum_{n\geq 0}(-1)^{n}(2n+2)\dot{R}_{2n+1}.

(8.74)

It follows from (
8.12
), (
8.71
), and (
8.73
) that for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]

\|\dot{\psi}(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-1,1/10}}\lesssim A,\qquad\|\dot{\psi}(t)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-1,1/10}}\lesssim\varepsilon(t).

(8.75)

We apply the operator
I-R_{0}
to (
8.73
) to see that

\dot{\psi}-R_{0}^{2}\dot{\psi}=(I-R_{0})\big\{(H_{0}+T_{2})\dot{\phi}+\dot{T}_{2}\phi-\dot{T}_{1}\psi-(T_{1}-R_{0})\dot{\psi}\big\},

which can be rewritten in the form

\dot{\psi}=(I-R_{0})H_{0}\dot{\phi}-\dot{R}_{0}\psi+(I-R_{0})\big\{T_{2}\dot{\phi}+\dot{T}_{2}\phi-(\dot{T}_{1}-\dot{R}_{0})\psi-(T_{1}-R_{0})\dot{\psi}\big\}+R_{0}\dot{R}_{0}\psi+R_{0}^{2}\dot{\psi}.

We notice that most of the terms in the formula above are cubic and higher order acceptable remainders, due to (
8.71
), and only the first two terms in the right-hand side give linear and quadratic contributions. Using also (
3.12
) these contributions are

H_{0}(-h+\dot{\phi}_{2})+R_{0}H_{0}h-\dot{R}_{0}\psi.

The identities (
8.65
) follow since
R_{0}f=-H_{0}(h\partial_{x}f)-h|\partial_{x}|f
and
\dot{h}-|\partial_{x}|\phi
and
\psi-H_{0}\phi
are suitable quadratic terms (see (
3.12
)).

The proof of the expansion (
8.66
) is similar. We start from from (
8.14
) and take 2 time derivatives, so

(I+T_{1})\ddot{\psi}=(H_{0}+T_{2})\ddot{\phi}+2\dot{T}_{2}\dot{\phi}+\ddot{T}_{2}\phi-2\dot{T}_{1}\dot{\psi}-\ddot{T}_{1}\psi,

(8.76)

where
\ddot{\psi}:=\partial^{2}_{t}\psi
,
\ddot{\phi}:=\partial^{2}_{t}\phi
, and
\ddot{T}_{1},\ddot{T}_{2}
are defined naturally as in (
8.74
). Using (
8.12
) and (
8.71
)–(
8.72
) it follows that for any
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]

\|\ddot{\psi}(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{0}-2,1/10}}\lesssim A,\qquad\|\ddot{\psi}(t)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{1}-2,1/10}}\lesssim\varepsilon(t).

(8.77)

As before, we apply the operator
I-R_{0}
to (
8.76
) to see that
\ddot{\psi}
is equal to

(I-R_{0})H_{0}\ddot{\phi}-2\dot{R}_{0}\dot{\psi}-\ddot{R}_{0}\psi

up to acceptable cubic remainders.
Then we notice that
\psi-H_{0}\phi
,
\dot{\psi}+H_{0}h
,
\dot{h}-|\partial_{x}|\phi
,
and
\ddot{h}+|\partial_{x}|h
satisfy suitable quadratic bounds, while

\ddot{\phi}+|\partial_{x}|\phi-\partial_{x}(h\partial_{x}\phi)-|\partial_{x}|(h|\partial_{x}|\phi)-\partial_{x}h\partial_{x}\phi+|\partial_{x}|h|\partial_{x}|\phi
is an acceptable cubic remainder.
The desired expansion (
8.66
) follows after some simplifications of the quadratic terms,
which completes the proof of the lemma.

9.
Vanishing at the Benjamin-Feir resonances

In this section we prove Proposition
6.2
. Assume that
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})\in\mathbb{H}^{3}_{\infty}
satisfies

\begin{split}&y_{1}\in(-\infty,0],\qquad y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}\in[0,\infty),\qquad|y_{1}|\geq y_{3}\geq y_{4}\geq y_{2},\\
&y_{3}\geq|y_{1}|/8,\qquad y_{2}\geq y_{4}/8.\end{split}

(9.1)

We would like to calculate explicitly the symmetrized symbols
SK_{1}^{a}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})
,
a\in\{1,\ldots,9\}
, and
SK_{2}^{b}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})
,
b\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
.

Since
y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}+y_{4}=0
, it follows from (
9.1
) that many of the
\chi
coefficients vanish, i.e.

0=\chi(y_{1},y_{j})=\chi(y_{3},y_{j})=\chi(y_{4},y_{2})=\chi(y_{2},y_{4})

(9.2)

for any
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
. Similarly, for any
j\neq k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,

\begin{split}&0=\chi(y_{1},y_{j}+y_{k})=\chi(y_{3},y_{j}+y_{k})=\chi(y_{2},y_{1}+y_{3})=\chi(y_{4},y_{1}+y_{3}),\\
&0=\chi(y_{j}+y_{k},y_{2})=\chi(y_{j}+y_{k},y_{4}),\\
&0=\chi(y_{2}+y_{3},y_{1})=\chi(y_{3}+y_{4},y_{1})=\chi(y_{1}+y_{2},y_{3})=\chi(y_{1}+y_{4},y_{3}).\end{split}

(9.3)

For simplicity of notation let
\chi_{i,j}:=\chi(y_{i},y_{j})
,

\chi_{i,j+k}:=\chi(y_{i},y_{j}+y_{k})
,
\chi_{i+j,k}:=\chi(y_{i}+y_{j},y_{k})
,
i,j,k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.
Using (
2.43
) it is easy to see that the nontrivial cutoffs
\chi
satisfy

\begin{split}&\chi_{2,1+4}=\chi_{2,3},\qquad\chi_{2,3+4}=\chi_{2,1},\qquad\chi_{4,1+2}=\chi_{4,3},\qquad\chi_{4,3+2}=\chi_{4,1},\\
&\chi_{2+4,1}=\chi_{2+4,3}=:\chi_{2+4}.\end{split}

(9.4)

We can also calculate explicitly the symbols
n^{rr}_{2}
,
n^{ii}_{2}
, and
n^{ri}_{2}

defined in (
4.78
)–(
4.80
): for
j\in\{2,4\}
we have

\begin{split}&n_{2}^{rr}(y_{1},y_{3})=n_{2}^{rr}(y_{3},y_{1})=n_{2}^{rr}(y_{2},y_{4})=n_{2}^{rr}(y_{4},y_{2})=0,\\
&n_{2}^{rr}(y_{1},y_{j})=n_{2}^{rr}(y_{j},y_{1})=(1/4)|y_{1}+y_{j}|^{1/2}|y_{j}|\chi_{j,1},\\
&n_{2}^{rr}(y_{3},y_{j})=n_{2}^{rr}(y_{j},y_{3})=(1/4)|y_{3}+y_{j}|^{1/2}|y_{j}|\chi_{j,3},\end{split}

(9.5)

\begin{split}&n_{2}^{ii}(y_{1},y_{3})=n_{2}^{ii}(y_{3},y_{1})=0,\\
&n_{2}^{ii}(y_{2},y_{4})=n_{2}^{ii}(y_{4},y_{2})=|y_{2}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2},\\
&n_{2}^{ii}(y_{1},y_{j})=n_{2}^{ii}(y_{j},y_{1})=-|y_{1}+y_{j}|^{1/2}|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{j}|^{1/2}\chi_{j,1}/2,\\
&n_{2}^{ii}(y_{3},y_{j})=n_{2}^{ii}(y_{j},y_{3})=|y_{3}+y_{j}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{j}|^{1/2}(1-\chi_{j,3}/2),\end{split}

(9.6)

\begin{split}&n_{2}^{ri}(y_{1},y_{3})=-2|y_{1}+y_{3}||y_{3}|^{1/2},\qquad n_{2}^{ri}(y_{3},y_{1})=0,\\
&n_{2}^{ri}(y_{2},y_{4})=n_{2}^{ri}(y_{4},y_{2})=0,\\
&n_{2}^{ri}(y_{1},y_{j})=|y_{1}+y_{j}||y_{j}|^{1/2}(-2+\chi_{j,1})-|y_{j}|^{3/2}\chi_{j,1}/2,\qquad n_{2}^{ri}(y_{j},y_{1})=-|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{j}|\chi_{j,1}/2,\\
&n_{2}^{ri}(y_{3},y_{j})=|y_{j}|^{1/2}(|y_{3}|+|y_{j}|/2)\chi_{j,3},\qquad n_{2}^{ri}(y_{j},y_{3})=-|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{j}|\chi_{j,3}/2.\end{split}

(9.7)

For simplicity of notation let
m_{j}:=m_{N_{0}}(y_{j})
,
m_{j+k}:=m_{N_{0}}(y_{j}+y_{k})
,
j,k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
. Recall that the symbols
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime\prime\prime}
are symmetric in all three variables. Using (
6.13
), (
6.20
), and the identities (
9.2
)–(
9.4
) we calculate explicitly

4A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})=\frac{|y_{1}|^{1/2}\big\{m_{1}^{2}(-2+\chi_{2,1})+m_{3+4}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\big\}}{3|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}},

(9.8)

4A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})=\frac{|y_{1}|^{1/2}\big\{m_{1}^{2}(-2+\chi_{4,1})+m_{3+2}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\big\}}{3|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}},

(9.9)

4A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})=\frac{|y_{1}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\big\{m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2,3}+m_{1+4}^{2}(-2+\chi_{2,3})\big\}}{3|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}},

(9.10)

4A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})=\frac{|y_{1}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\big\{m_{3}^{2}\chi_{4,3}+m_{1+2}^{2}(-2+\chi_{4,3})\big\}}{3|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}},

(9.11)

4A^{\prime}(y_{2},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{3})=\frac{-2|y_{1}+y_{3}|^{1/2}m_{1+3}^{2}}{3|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}},

(9.12)

4A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{3},y_{2}+y_{4})=\frac{|y_{1}|^{1/2}\big\{m_{1}^{2}(-2+\chi_{2+4})+m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2+4}\big\}}{3|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}+y_{4}|^{1/2}},

(9.13)

4B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})=4|y_{2}|m_{2}^{2}+4|y_{3}+y_{4}|m_{3+4}^{2}-2\chi_{2,1}\big[|y_{1}|m_{1}^{2}+|y_{1}|m_{3+4}^{2}-2|y_{2}|m_{1}m_{3+4}\big],

(9.14)

4B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})=4|y_{4}|m_{4}^{2}+4|y_{3}+y_{2}|m_{3+2}^{2}-2\chi_{4,1}\big[|y_{1}|m_{1}^{2}+|y_{1}|m_{3+2}^{2}-2|y_{4}|m_{1}m_{3+2}\big],

(9.15)

4B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})=4|y_{3}|m_{3}^{2}+4|y_{2}|m_{2}^{2}-2\chi_{2,3}\big[|y_{1}+y_{4}|m_{1+4}^{2}+|y_{1}+y_{4}|m_{3}^{2}-2|y_{2}|m_{3}m_{1+4}\big],

(9.16)

4B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})=4|y_{3}|m_{3}^{2}+4|y_{4}|m_{4}^{2}-2\chi_{4,3}\big[|y_{1}+y_{2}|m_{1+2}^{2}+|y_{1}+y_{2}|m_{3}^{2}-2|y_{4}|m_{3}m_{1+2}\big],

(9.17)

4B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{2},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{3})=4|y_{2}|m_{2}^{2}+4|y_{4}|m_{4}^{2},

(9.18)

4B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{3},y_{2}+y_{4})=4|y_{3}|m_{3}^{2}+4|y_{2}+y_{4}|m_{2+4}^{2}-2\chi_{2+4}\big[|y_{1}|m_{1}^{2}+|y_{1}|m_{3}^{2}-2|y_{2}+y_{4}|m_{1}m_{3}\big].

(9.19)

The symbols
B^{\prime\prime}
are symmetric in the first 2 variables. Using (
6.18
)–(
6.19
) we calculate

\begin{split}4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})&=-2|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}\big[2m_{2}^{2}+\chi_{2,1}(m_{1}^{2}-m_{3+4}^{2})\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{4},y_{1},y_{2})&=-2|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\big[2m_{3+4}^{2}-\chi_{2,1}(m_{1}^{2}+m_{3+4}^{2})\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{4},y_{2},y_{1})&=4|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\big[m_{3+4}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}-\chi_{2,1}m_{3+4}^{2}\big],\end{split}

(9.20)

\begin{split}4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})&=-2|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}\big[2m_{4}^{2}+\chi_{4,1}(m_{1}^{2}-m_{3+2}^{2})\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{2},y_{1},y_{4})&=-2|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\big[2m_{3+2}^{2}-\chi_{4,1}(m_{1}^{2}+m_{3+2}^{2})\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{2},y_{4},y_{1})&=4|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\big[m_{3+2}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-\chi_{4,1}m_{3+2}^{2}\big],\end{split}

(9.21)

\begin{split}4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})&=4|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}\big[m_{3}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}-\chi_{2,3}m_{3}^{2}\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{4},y_{3},y_{2})&=-2|y_{1}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}\big[2m_{3}^{2}-\chi_{2,3}(m_{1+4}^{2}+m_{3}^{2})\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{4},y_{2},y_{3})&=-2|y_{1}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}\big[2m_{2}^{2}+\chi_{2,3}(m_{1+4}^{2}-m_{3}^{2})\big],\end{split}

(9.22)

\begin{split}4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})&=4|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}\big[m_{3}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}-\chi_{4,3}m_{3}^{2}\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})&=-2|y_{1}+y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}[2m_{3}^{2}-\chi_{4,3}(m_{1+2}^{2}+m_{3}^{2})],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{2},y_{4},y_{3})&=-2|y_{1}+y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}[2m_{4}^{2}+\chi_{4,3}(m_{1+2}^{2}-m_{3}^{2})],\end{split}

(9.23)

\begin{split}4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{2},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{3})&=4|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}(m_{2}^{2}+m_{4}^{2}),\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{3},y_{2},y_{4})&=-4|y_{1}+y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}m_{2}^{2},\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{3},y_{4},y_{2})&=-4|y_{1}+y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}m_{4}^{2},\end{split}

(9.24)

\begin{split}4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{3},y_{2}+y_{4})&=-2|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}\big[2m_{3}^{2}-\chi_{2+4}(m_{1}^{2}+m_{3}^{2})\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{2}+y_{4},y_{1},y_{3})&=-2|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{2}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\big[2m_{2+4}^{2}+\chi_{2+4}(m_{1}^{2}-m_{3}^{2})\big],\\
4B^{\prime\prime}(y_{2}+y_{4},y_{3},y_{1})&=4|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\big[m_{3}^{2}+m_{2+4}^{2}-\chi_{2+4}m_{3}^{2}\big].\end{split}

(9.25)

9.1.
The symbols
SK_{i}^{j}

We calculate now the symmetrized symbols
SK
,
where
K
is any of the symbols from Lemma
4.7
,
and evaluate them at frequencies
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})
satisfying (
9.1
). Recall the main formulas

\begin{split}&SK(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=3\widetilde{K}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})+3\widetilde{K}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{4},y_{3})-3\widetilde{K}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1},y_{2})-3\widetilde{K}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{2},y_{1}),\\
&3\widetilde{K}(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)=K(\xi,\eta,\rho,\sigma)+K(\eta,\rho,\xi,\sigma)+K(\rho,\xi,\eta,\sigma),\end{split}

(9.26)

see (
5.3
) and (
5.6
). For simplicity of notation let
x_{j}:=|y_{j}|^{1/2}
,
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.

Contributions of
K_{1}^{1}
.
Using (
9.2
)–(
9.4
) we calculate the components

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=(1/2)m_{1}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}\cdot|y_{1}||y_{2}||y_{4}|^{1/2}-m_{1}^{2}\chi_{2+4}\cdot|y_{1}||y_{2}||y_{4}|^{1/2},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{3},y_{4})=2m_{2}^{2}|y_{3}||y_{2}||y_{4}|^{1/2}-2m_{2}^{2}\chi_{4,3}|y_{3}+y_{4}||y_{2}||y_{4}|^{1/2},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1},y_{4})=m_{3}^{2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{3}|\big\{2-\chi_{2+4}-\chi_{2,1}+\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}/2\big\}.\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{4},y_{3})=0,\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{4},y_{3})=m_{2}^{2}|y_{2}||y_{4}||y_{3}|^{1/2}(2-\chi_{4,1}),\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{4},y_{2},y_{1},y_{3})=m_{4}^{2}|y_{4}||y_{2}||y_{3}|^{1/2}(2-\chi_{2,1}).\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{1},y_{3},y_{4},y_{2})=(1/2)m_{1}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\chi_{2,1}\cdot|y_{1}||y_{4}||y_{2}|^{1/2}-m_{1}^{2}\chi_{2+4}\cdot|y_{1}||y_{4}||y_{2}|^{1/2},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{3},y_{1},y_{4},y_{2})=m_{3}^{2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}||y_{3}|\big\{2-\chi_{2+4}-\chi_{4,1}+\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}/2\big\},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{4},y_{1},y_{3},y_{2})=2m_{4}^{2}|y_{3}||y_{4}||y_{2}|^{1/2}-2m_{4}^{2}\chi_{2,3}|y_{3}+y_{2}||y_{4}||y_{2}|^{1/2},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{1}(y_{2},y_{3},y_{4},y_{1})=K_{1}^{1}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{4},y_{1})=K_{1}^{1}(y_{4},y_{2},y_{3},y_{1})=0.\end{split}

In terms of the variables
x_{j}=|y_{j}|^{1/2}
,
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, the formulas above show that

4SK_{1}^{1}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=\sum_{l\in\{1,\ldots,4\}}4H_{1,l}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}),

(9.27)

where

\begin{split}4H_{1,1}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4})=m_{1}^{2}\chi_{2+4}\cdot 4x_{1}^{2}x_{2}x_{4}(x_{4}-x_{2})\\
&+m_{1}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}\cdot 2x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{4}-m_{1}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\chi_{2,1}\cdot 2x_{1}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{2},\end{split}

(9.28)

\begin{split}4H_{1,2}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4})=m_{2}^{2}\cdot 8x_{2}^{2}x_{3}x_{4}(x_{3}+x_{4})\\
&-m_{2}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\cdot 8(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})x_{2}^{2}x_{4}-m_{2}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\cdot 4x_{3}x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{2},\end{split}

(9.29)

\begin{split}4&H_{1,3}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=m_{3}^{2}\cdot 8x_{3}^{2}x_{2}x_{4}(x_{2}-x_{4})-m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2+4}\cdot 4x_{3}^{2}x_{2}x_{4}(x_{2}-x_{4})\\
&-m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\cdot 4x_{3}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{4}+m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\cdot 2x_{3}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{4}+m_{3}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\cdot 4x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{2}-m_{3}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 2x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{2},\end{split}

(9.30)

\begin{split}4H_{1,4}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4})=m_{4}^{2}\cdot 8x_{4}^{2}x_{3}x_{2}(x_{2}-x_{3})\\
&+m_{4}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 8(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})x_{4}^{2}x_{2}-m_{4}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\cdot 4x_{3}x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{2}.\end{split}

(9.31)

Contributions of
K_{1}^{2}
. The nontrivial components are

\begin{split}K_{1}^{2}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{3},y_{4})=&-m_{1}m_{3+4}|y_{2}||y_{3}+y_{4}||y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\\
&+m_{3}m_{1+4}|y_{2}||y_{1}+y_{4}||y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3}(2-\chi_{4,1}),\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{2}(y_{4},y_{1},y_{3},y_{2})=&-m_{1}m_{3+2}|y_{4}||y_{3}+y_{2}||y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\\
&+m_{3}m_{1+2}|y_{4}||y_{1}+y_{2}||y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,3}(2-\chi_{2,1}).\end{split}

Therefore, in terms of the variables
x_{j}=|y_{j}|^{1/2}
,

\begin{split}4&SK_{1}^{2}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=4m_{1}m_{3+2}x_{4}^{2}x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}-4m_{1}m_{3+4}x_{2}^{2}x_{4}(x^{2}_{3}+x^{2}_{4})\chi_{4,3}\chi_{2,1}\\
&+4m_{3}m_{1+2}x_{4}^{2}x_{2}(x^{2}_{3}+x^{2}_{4})\chi_{4,3}(\chi_{2,1}-2)-4m_{3}m_{1+4}x_{2}^{2}x_{4}(x^{2}_{3}+x^{2}_{2})\chi_{2,3}(\chi_{4,1}-2).\end{split}

(9.32)

Contributions of
K_{1}^{3}
. The nontrivial components are

\begin{split}K_{1}^{3}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=-(1/2)m_{3+2}^{2}|y_{2}||y_{1}+y_{4}||y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3}(2-\chi_{4,1}),\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{3}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1},y_{4})=(1/2)m_{1+2}^{2}|y_{2}||y_{3}+y_{4}||y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}.\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{3}(y_{1},y_{3},y_{4},y_{2})=-(1/2)m_{3+4}^{2}|y_{4}||y_{1}+y_{2}||y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,3}(2-\chi_{2,1}),\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{3}(y_{3},y_{1},y_{4},y_{2})=(1/2)m_{1+4}^{2}|y_{4}||y_{3}+y_{2}||y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1},\end{split}

Since
m_{1+2}=m_{3+4}
and
m_{1+4}=m_{3+2}
it follows that

\begin{split}4SK_{1}^{3}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=&-2m_{1+4}^{2}x_{2}x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\chi_{2,3}\big\{x_{2}(2-\chi_{4,1})+x_{4}\chi_{4,1}\big\}\\
&+2m_{1+2}^{2}x_{2}x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})\chi_{4,3}\big\{x_{4}(2-\chi_{2,1})+x_{2}\chi_{2,1}\big\}.\end{split}

(9.33)

Contributions of
K_{1}^{4}
. The nontrivial components are

K_{1}^{4}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{3},y_{4})=2m_{1}m_{3}|y_{2}||y_{4}|^{3/2}\chi_{2+4},

K_{1}^{4}(y_{4},y_{1},y_{3},y_{2})=2m_{1}m_{3}|y_{4}||y_{2}|^{3/2}\chi_{2+4},

where we used the definition (
4.75
). Therefore

4SK_{1}^{4}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=m_{1}m_{3}\chi_{2+4}\cdot 8x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{2}(x_{4}-x_{2}).

(9.34)

Contributions of
K_{1}^{5}
. The nontrivial components are

K_{1}^{5}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=-(1/2)m_{1}m_{3+2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|^{3/2}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1},

K_{1}^{5}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1},y_{4})=-(1/2)m_{3}m_{1+2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|^{3/2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}.

K_{1}^{5}(y_{1},y_{3},y_{4},y_{2})=-(1/2)m_{1}m_{3+4}|y_{4}||y_{2}|^{3/2}\chi_{4,3}\chi_{2,1},

K_{1}^{5}(y_{3},y_{1},y_{4},y_{2})=-(1/2)m_{3}m_{1+4}|y_{4}||y_{2}|^{3/2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3},

Therefore

\begin{split}4SK_{1}^{5}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=&-2m_{1}m_{3+2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\cdot x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{3}+2m_{1}m_{3+4}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\cdot x_{4}^{2}x_{2}^{3}\\
&-2m_{3}m_{1+2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\cdot x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{3}+2m_{3}m_{1+4}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\cdot x_{4}^{2}x_{2}^{3}.\end{split}

(9.35)

Contributions of
K_{1}^{6}
. The nontrivial components are

K_{1}^{6}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{4},y_{3})=-(1/2)m_{3}(m_{1+4}-m_{3})|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3},

K_{1}^{6}(y_{4},y_{2},y_{1},y_{3})=-(1/2)m_{3}(m_{1+2}-m_{3})|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}.

K_{1}^{6}(y_{2},y_{3},y_{4},y_{1})=-(1/2)m_{1}(m_{3+4}-m_{1})|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|\chi_{4,3}\chi_{2,1},

K_{1}^{6}(y_{4},y_{2},y_{3},y_{1})=-(1/2)m_{1}(m_{3+2}-m_{1})|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1},

Therefore, in terms of the variables
x_{j}=|y_{j}|^{1/2}
,

\begin{split}4SK_{1}^{6}(y_{1},&y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=2x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{1}m_{1}\big\{m_{3+4}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}+m_{3+2}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}\big\}\\
&-2x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{3}m_{3}\big\{m_{1+4}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}+m_{1+2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\big\}\\
&-2x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{1}m_{1}^{2}[\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}+\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}]+2x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{3}m_{3}^{2}[\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}+\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}].\end{split}

(9.36)

Contributions of
K_{1}^{7}
. The nontrivial components are

K_{1}^{7}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{4},y_{3})=2m_{3}(m_{1}-m_{3})\chi_{2+4}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|,

K_{1}^{7}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{4},y_{1})=2m_{1}(m_{3}-m_{1})\chi_{2+4}|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|,

where we used the definition (
4.74
). Therefore

4SK_{1}^{7}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=-8m_{1}m_{3}x^{2}_{2}x^{2}_{4}(x_{1}-x_{3})\chi_{2+4}+8\big(x_{1}m_{1}^{2}-x_{3}m_{3}^{2}\big)x_{2}^{2}x_{4}^{2}\chi_{2+4}.

(9.37)

Contributions of
K_{1}^{8}
. Using the formulas (
4.98
) and (
9.5
)–(
9.7
) we calculate

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=&-3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})\\
&\times|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}\big\{|y_{3}+y_{2}|(4-2\chi_{4,1})+|y_{4}|\chi_{4,1}\big\},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{3},y_{4})=0,\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{3},y_{2},&y_{1},y_{4})=+3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{4}||y_{2}|\chi_{2,1}\\
&+3A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}(2|y_{3}|+|y_{4}|)\chi_{4,3},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{4},y_{3})=-12A^{\prime}(y_{2},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{3})\cdot|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}+y_{4}|^{3/2},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{4},y_{3})=&+3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}||y_{4}|\chi_{4,1}\\
&-3A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|\chi_{2,3},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{4},y_{2},y_{1},y_{3})=&+3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}||y_{2}|\chi_{2,1}\\
&-3A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|\chi_{4,3}.\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{1},y_{3},y_{4},y_{2})=&-3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})\\
&\times|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}\big\{|y_{3}+y_{4}|(4-2\chi_{2,1})+|y_{2}|\chi_{2,1}\big\},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{3},y_{1},&y_{4},y_{2})=+3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|\chi_{4,1}\\
&+3A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}(2|y_{3}|+|y_{2}|)\chi_{2,3},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{4},y_{1},y_{3},y_{2})=0,\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{2},y_{3},y_{4},y_{1})=&+3A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{3/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|\chi_{4,3}\\
&-3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}||y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,1},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{4},y_{1})=0,\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{8}(y_{4},y_{2},y_{3},y_{1})=&+3A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{3/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|\chi_{2,3}\\
&-3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{4}||y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,1},\end{split}

Therefore, using (
5.6
),

4SK_{1}^{8}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=\sum_{l\in\{1,\ldots,5\}}4I_{1,l}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})

(9.38)

where

\begin{split}I_{1,1}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4}):=3A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,3}\\
&\times\big\{-|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|+|y_{1}|^{1/2}(2|y_{3}|+|y_{4}|)-|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}\big\},\end{split}

(9.39)

\begin{split}I_{1,2}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4}):=-3A^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3}\\
&\times\big\{|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|+|y_{1}|^{1/2}(2|y_{3}|+|y_{2}|)+|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}\big\},\end{split}

(9.40)

\begin{split}&I_{1,3}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}):=-3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}\\
&\times\big\{4|y_{3}+y_{2}|+\chi_{4,1}\big(|y_{4}|-2|y_{3}+y_{2}|-|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}+|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}-|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}\big)\big\},\end{split}

(9.41)

\begin{split}&I_{1,4}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}):=3A^{\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}\\
&\times\big\{4|y_{3}+y_{4}|+\chi_{2,1}\big(|y_{2}|-2|y_{3}+y_{4}|+|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}+|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}+|y_{1}|^{1/2}|y_{2}|^{1/2}\big)\big\},\end{split}

(9.42)

\begin{split}I_{1,5}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}):=-3A^{\prime}(y_{2},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{3})\cdot 4|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2}|y_{2}+y_{4}|^{3/2}.\end{split}

(9.43)

Using the formulas (
9.8
)–(
9.13
) we can rewrite, in terms of the variables
x_{j}=|y_{j}|^{1/2}

\begin{split}4I_{1,1}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4})=-m_{1}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\cdot 2x_{1}x_{4}\big[-x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})+x_{1}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-x_{3}x_{4})\big]\\
&+m_{1}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\chi_{2,1}\cdot x_{1}x_{4}\big[-x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})+x_{1}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-x_{3}x_{4})\big]\\
&+m_{3+4}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\chi_{2,1}\cdot x_{1}x_{4}\big[-x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})+x_{1}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-x_{3}x_{4})\big],\end{split}

(9.44)

\begin{split}4I_{1,2}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4})=m_{1}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 2x_{1}x_{2}\big[x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})+x_{1}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{2})\big]\\
&-m_{1}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\cdot x_{1}x_{2}\big[x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})+x_{1}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{2})\big]\\
&-m_{3+2}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\cdot x_{1}x_{2}\big[x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})+x_{1}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{2})\big],\end{split}

(9.45)

\begin{split}4I_{1,3}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4})=m_{1+4}^{2}\cdot 8x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{2}\\
&-m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 4x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{2}-m_{1+4}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 4x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{2}\\
&-m_{1+4}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\cdot 2x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})(x_{1}x_{4}+x_{3}x_{4}+2x_{3}^{2}+2x_{2}^{2}-x_{4}^{2}-x_{2}x_{4})\\
&+m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}\cdot x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})(x_{1}x_{4}+x_{3}x_{4}+2x_{3}^{2}+2x_{2}^{2}-x_{4}^{2}-x_{2}x_{4})\\
&+m_{1+4}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}\cdot x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})(x_{1}x_{4}+x_{3}x_{4}+2x_{3}^{2}+2x_{2}^{2}-x_{4}^{2}-x_{2}x_{4}),\end{split}

(9.46)

\begin{split}4I_{1,4}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4})=-m_{1+2}^{2}\cdot 8x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})^{2}\\
&+m_{3}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\cdot 4x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})^{2}+m_{1+2}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\cdot 4x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})^{2}\\
&-m_{1+2}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\cdot 2x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})(x_{1}x_{2}+x_{3}x_{2}-2x_{3}^{2}-2x_{4}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{2}x_{4})\\
&+m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\cdot x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})(x_{1}x_{2}+x_{3}x_{2}-2x_{3}^{2}-2x_{4}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{2}x_{4})\\
&+m_{1+2}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\cdot x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})(x_{1}x_{2}+x_{3}x_{2}-2x_{3}^{2}-2x_{4}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{2}x_{4}),\end{split}

(9.47)

\begin{split}4I_{1,5}(y_{1},y_{2},&y_{3},y_{4})=m_{1+3}^{2}\cdot 8x_{3}(x_{2}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})^{2}.\end{split}

(9.48)

Contributions of
K_{1}^{9}
. Using the formulas (
4.99
) and (
9.5
)–(
9.7
) we calculate

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{2},y_{4},y_{1})\cdot|y_{2}||y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3}\\
&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{4}|^{1/2}\big\{|y_{3}+y_{2}|(4-2\chi_{4,1})+|y_{4}|\chi_{4,1}\big\},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{3},y_{4})=0,\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1},y_{4})=&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{2},y_{4},y_{3})\cdot|y_{2}||y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,1}\\
&+(1/2)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})\cdot(2|y_{3}|+|y_{4}|)|y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,3},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{4},y_{3})=&-2B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{2},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{3})\cdot|y_{2}+y_{4}||y_{3}|^{1/2},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{2},y_{1},y_{4},y_{3})=&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{4},y_{3},y_{2})\cdot|y_{4}||y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,1}\\
&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{2}||y_{3}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{4},y_{2},y_{1},y_{3})=&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})\cdot|y_{2}||y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,1}\\
&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{4}||y_{3}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,3},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{1},y_{3},y_{4},y_{2})=&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{4},y_{2},y_{1})\cdot|y_{4}||y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,3}\\
&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{2}|^{1/2}\big\{|y_{3}+y_{4}|(4-2\chi_{2,1})+|y_{2}|\chi_{2,1}\big\},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{3},y_{1},y_{4},y_{2})=&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{4},y_{2},y_{3})\cdot|y_{4}||y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,1}\\
&+(1/2)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})\cdot(2|y_{3}|+|y_{2}|)|y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{4},y_{1},y_{3},y_{2})=0,\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{2},y_{3},y_{4},y_{1})=&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{4},y_{1},y_{2})\cdot|y_{4}||y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,3}\\
&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{2}||y_{1}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,1},\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{4},y_{1})=0,\end{split}

\begin{split}K_{1}^{9}(y_{4},y_{2},y_{3},y_{1})=&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{2},y_{1},y_{4})\cdot|y_{2}||y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3}\\
&-(1/2)B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{4}||y_{1}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,1}.\end{split}

Therefore, using (
5.6
),

4SK_{1}^{9}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=\sum_{l\in\{1,\ldots,5\}}4J_{1,l}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})

(9.49)

where

\begin{split}4&J_{1,1}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}):=2B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{4}|^{1/2}(2|y_{3}|+|y_{4}|-|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2})\chi_{4,3}\\
&+2\big[B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{4},y_{2},y_{1})+B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{4},y_{1},y_{2})\big]\cdot|y_{4}||y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,3},\end{split}

(9.50)

\begin{split}4&J_{1,2}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}):=-2B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{4},y_{3}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{2}|^{1/2}(2|y_{3}|+|y_{2}|+|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{3}|^{1/2})\chi_{2,3}\\
&-2\big[B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{2},y_{4},y_{1})-B^{\prime\prime}(y_{3}+y_{2},y_{1},y_{4})\big]\cdot|y_{2}||y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,3},\end{split}

(9.51)

\begin{split}4&J_{1,3}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})\\
&:=-2B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{2},y_{1}+y_{4})\cdot|y_{4}|^{1/2}\big\{|y_{3}+y_{2}|(4-2\chi_{4,1})+(|y_{4}|-|y_{4}|^{1/2}|y_{1}|^{1/2})\chi_{4,1}\big\}\\
&+2\big[B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{4},y_{2},y_{3})-B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{4},y_{3},y_{2})\big]\cdot|y_{4}||y_{3}+y_{2}|^{1/2}\chi_{4,1},\end{split}

(9.52)

\begin{split}4&J_{1,4}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})\\
&:=2B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{3},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{2})\cdot|y_{2}|^{1/2}\big\{|y_{3}+y_{4}|(4-2\chi_{2,1})+(|y_{2}|+|y_{2}|^{1/2}|y_{1}|^{1/2})\chi_{2,1}\big\}\\
&-2\big[B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{2},y_{4},y_{3})+B^{\prime\prime}(y_{1}+y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})\big]\cdot|y_{2}||y_{3}+y_{4}|^{1/2}\chi_{2,1},\end{split}

(9.53)

\begin{split}4J_{1,5}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}):=-8B^{\prime\prime\prime}(y_{2},y_{4},y_{1}+y_{3})\cdot|y_{2}+y_{4}||y_{3}|^{1/2}.\end{split}

(9.54)

With
x_{j}:=|y_{j}|^{1/2}
as before, we use (
9.14
) and (
9.20
) to rewrite the formula (
9.50
) as

\begin{split}4J_{1,1}&(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=m_{2}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\cdot 2x_{2}x_{4}\big[x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})+x_{2}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-x_{3}x_{4})\big]\\
&+m_{3+4}^{2}\chi_{4,3}\cdot 2x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})\big[(x_{2}-x_{1})x_{4}+2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-x_{3}x_{4}\big]\\
&+m_{1}m_{3+4}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\cdot 2x_{2}^{2}x_{4}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-x_{3}x_{4})\\
&+m_{1}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\cdot x_{1}x_{4}\big[x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})-x_{1}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-x_{3}x_{4})\big]\\
&+m_{3+4}^{2}\chi_{2,1}\chi_{4,3}\cdot x_{4}\big[x_{4}(x_{1}-2x_{2})(x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2})-x_{1}^{2}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-x_{3}x_{4})\big].\end{split}

(9.55)

Using (
9.15
) and (
9.21
) we can rewrite the formula (
9.51
) as

\begin{split}4J_{1,2}&(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=-m_{4}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 2x_{2}x_{4}\big[x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})+x_{4}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{2})\big]\\
&-m_{3+2}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 2x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\big[(x_{4}+x_{1})x_{2}+2x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{2}\big]\\
&-m_{1}m_{3+2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 2x_{2}x_{4}^{2}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{2})\\
&+m_{1}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\cdot x_{1}x_{2}\big[x_{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})+x_{1}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{2})\big]\\
&+m_{3+2}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\chi_{2,3}\cdot x_{2}\big[x_{2}(x_{1}+2x_{4})(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})+x_{1}^{2}(2x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{2})\big].\end{split}

(9.56)

Using (
9.16
) and (
9.22
) we can rewrite the formula (
9.52
) as

\begin{split}&4J_{1,3}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})=-m_{2}^{2}\cdot 8x_{4}x_{2}^{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})-m_{3}^{2}\cdot 8x_{4}x_{3}^{2}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\\
&+m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 4x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{2}+m_{1+4}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 4x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{2}-m_{3}m_{1+4}\chi_{2,3}\cdot 8x_{2}^{2}x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\\
&+m_{2}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\cdot 2x_{2}x_{4}\big[x_{2}(x_{1}x_{4}-x_{4}^{2}+2x_{3}^{2}+2x_{2}^{2})-x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\big]\\
&+m_{3}^{2}\chi_{4,1}\cdot 2x_{4}x_{3}\big[x_{3}(x_{1}x_{4}-x_{4}^{2}+2x_{3}^{2}+2x_{2}^{2})+x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\big]\\
&-m_{3}^{2}\chi_{2,3}\chi_{4,1}\cdot x_{4}(x_{3}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\big[x_{1}x_{4}-x_{4}^{2}+2x_{3}^{2}+2x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}x_{4}-x_{2}x_{4}\big]\\
